# Observations inbox

Raw observations land here as they are noticed, get triaged, and are handed to a worker session as a
grounded prompt. This file is the source of truth between sessions — an item that is only in a chat
transcript is an item that will be lost.

**How to use it.** Add anything to CAPTURED, however rough, without stopping to diagnose. Triage moves an
item into a bucket with a verdict. When a bucket is worth a session, a prompt is written from it and the
items are marked with the version that fixed them.

**Status vocabulary:** `captured` (raw) · `triaged` (understood, not started) · `assigned` (a session has
it) · `fixed vX.Y.Z` · `wont-fix` (with the reason).

Last triage: 2026-07-31, at v2.1.313-beta. Bucket B worked 2026-08-01, at v2.1.331-336-beta.

B15 and B14 are fixed; **[[B12]] is a WONT-FIX on the constant with the anchor table to prove it** — Venus and Mercury both clamp to the same rotation factor and have opposite outcomes, so no measured body's result would change wherever the threshold moved. Its STRUCTURE is separately falsified by Mercury and Ganymede, and a geology-driven rebuild is scoped in the entry — but do NOT sell that rebuild as restoring the ~40 lost dynamos, because 24 of them are Venus-like episodic-lid worlds a better model would also suppress.

- **[[B17]] is CLOSED** (v2.1.340-beta): the belt term. The engine summed luminous sources only, so a

- **[[B22]] is CLOSED** (v2.1.341-beta): a planet was never inside its own belt, because the belt term
  resolves its host as the parent and a planet's parent is its star. Fixed with an INNER EDGE derived
  from atmospheric scale height, without which the same law puts 2.31 Sv/day on Earth's ground. Radiation
  is now TWO named figures on every body — surface / 1-bar, and the environment above the atmosphere —
  which is the shape **[[B20]] should extend rather than replace.** Derived diff was pure addition.

## A — UI bugs (self-contained, no physics)

| # | Observation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| A1 | **Guide-tip header/footer banner does not reflow on resize — it is a BITMAP being stretched.** Scoped 2026-07-30. The banner is a canvas built by `drawHud()` (`lib/catalogue/infoCard.ts`) and drawn onto a full-frame quad, so once built the wrap points are baked in at that width and any size change magnifies the picture. The font sizing is innocent: `fontPx = max(12, min(viewW*0.011, viewH*0.026, 20))`, clamped 12-20px — nothing computes a giant font, a small canvas is being upscaled. **Scope is narrow: three call sites, two at risk.** (1) Player STARMAP, 2D and 3D, via `Starmap3DView.applyTips()` — ALREADY GUARDED, it re-measures the container on every build and carries a comment about this exact failure. (2) Player SYSTEM 3D via `catalogue/+page.svelte:600`, a reactive `hudCanvas = drawHud({...})` feeding `HoloView` — **prime suspect**, check whether its viewW/viewH refresh on resize. (3) DOCUMENT headers/footers (`document/blocks.ts`, `documentStyles.ts`) are a SEPARATE path doing real layout, not a bitmap — expected unaffected, confirm only. **Note the puzzle:** the reported screenshot is the STARMAP, which is the guarded surface — so either the player starmap does not go through `applyTips`, or there is a second stale-size source. Identify which surface actually drew it before fixing. Best fix moves the re-measure INTO `drawHud` so a third caller cannot reintroduce it. **Session notes (2026-07-31, v2.1.304):** could not reproduce headless (the automated pane stops compositing, which suspends the ResizeObservers under test). Reviewed all three sites: (2) the catalogue hudCanvas IS reactive on hudW/hudH and `setHud` re-sizes its quad on scene resize, so it should track a visible-window resize — same for the starmap scene. One real mechanism found and closed: RO notifications deliver BEFORE PAINT, so a resize landing while the window is not painting (minimised, background tab, other desktop) leaves a canvas at the stale size until something forces a render — the DOCUMENT view (path 3) now also re-measures on window resize + visibilitychange. If the screenshot came from a resize-while-hidden, that may be the whole story for path 3 but paths 1-2 would still bitmap-stretch until their next prop change. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND (live repro by Alex, v2.1.305): the rebuild always ran and never reached the screen.** Both scene setHud implementations swapped the rebuilt canvas into a live texture via `map.image = ...` -- and WebGL2 texture storage is immutable once allocated (texStorage2D), so uploading a DIFFERENT-SIZED canvas fails silently and the quad keeps stretching the stale bitmap. Every re-measure guard upstream was therefore working and irrelevant. Fixed by recreating the texture whenever the canvas dimensions change (system holo + starmap scenes), and the same trap closed in the label sprites (drawLabel resizes its canvas under a live texture on font/text change). The document view was never affected -- its setSource already recreates per frame. Followed by a fine-tuning pass centring the text within the band (v2.1.306). | fixed v2.1.304-306-beta |
| A2 | Player-view construct panel is anaemic — Blip-A shows Type / Orbit distance / Atmosphere, i.e. BODY fields, while the data holds crew, engines, fuel tanks, cargo and delta-v. Constructs need their own block in the document / DocPanel builder. **FIXED v2.1.325-beta.** **Root cause: there was no construct branch at all.** `bodyFacts` had exactly one construct-aware line — the Type label at `:70` — and a construct then fell through the whole body block, matching only the rows a ship happens to share with a world. Blip-A's three rows were Type, Orbit distance and Atmosphere, and the last one said "None". **Everything in the new block is READ from `calculateFullConstructSpecs`**, the existing derivation behind the GM's Construct Derived Specs panel, the transit planner, the autopilot adapter and the interstellar modal — nothing is recomputed, so the player block and the GM panel cannot print different numbers for one ship. Confirmed at the formatter too: the GM uses `fmt.speedMs`, which is `formatSpeedKmS(ms / 1000, …)`, character-for-character what the block now calls for Δv. Twelve rows for Blip-A: class, location, crew, supplies, dimensions, dry mass, cargo against capacity, total mass, power surplus, fuel by name, max acceleration, Δv. **The rule pack had to be threaded in, and that is the one structural change:** engine and fuel DEFINITIONS live in the pack, not on the node, and without them a tank has no density and an engine no Isp. `GuideDocOpts` and both consumers (`DocPanel`, `FilteredDocumentView`) take an OPTIONAL `rulePack`, passed at all six mount sites; the host is resolved inside the builder the same way the parent-nav row resolves it, so the two cannot name different parents. **The honest-degradation rule was applied twice after testing the no-pack path and finding it QUIETLY WRONG rather than merely short** — the same fault class as A5. A tank whose fuel is not in the pack contributes zero mass, so "Total mass" read 1,200 t instead of 2,300 t; and an unresolved engine has no power draw, so what was labelled "Power surplus" was really the plant's output (100 MW against a true 60). Total mass is now suppressed unless every tank resolved, and the power row is relabelled "Power output" unless every engine did. **Redaction: nothing new is exposed.** The boundary is node-level — `computePlayerSnapshot` drops hidden subtrees and `gmNotes`, and optionally `description`; every field this block reads (class, placement, crew, `physical_parameters`, `systems.life_support`, `power_plants`, `fuel_tanks`, `engines`, `current_cargo_tonnes`) already crossed to players. **One field deliberately LEFT OUT because it is not clearly player-safe: `cargoDescription`** — see [[A27]]. **Verified on all 26 constructs in the campaign** (the block reads sensibly for a 6-million-crew colony drum, a 1,000 km ring gate and two derelicts alike) and live on BOTH consumers with identical rows: `FilteredDocumentView`, reached by drilling Tau Ceti → Adrian → Blip-A, and `DocPanel`. **Not exercised on live data, said plainly: the `Status` row and a construct's `Tags` row** — no construct in the bundled campaign carries a `flight_state` (it is set at runtime by the transit planner) or a single tag. **One knock-on fixed in the same commit:** the panel subtitle appended the singular `class`, which only constructs carry, so with the class now properly in the Type row it read "Ship · Ship/Interstellar/Eridian". Suppressed for constructs; bodies are untouched. Also raised: [[A28]]. | fixed v2.1.325-beta |

| A4 | **GM starmap labels grow with zoom (system names, depth cues, placement ghost).** System-name font should stay a constant screen size as you zoom, not scale with the map transform. `Starmap.svelte` — the labels are inside the world transform, so they scale with it. (Compare: the 3D starmap uses sprites with an explicit `setLabelSize`, which is why it does not have this.) Each label font size and its offset from the marker now divide by the zoom, cancelling the one inherited factor. **Not covered — see A8:** route labels, the measure read-out and the multi-star `+` indicator still scale. | fixed v2.1.295-beta |

| A5 | **Stat block pairs two different temperatures in adjacent rows.** Pandora: "Surface temp 45 °C" against "Temp range -28 °C to -23 °C" — the mean sits 70 °C outside its own range. 45 °C is 318 K, the range is 245–250 K, and the ~71 K gap is the GREENHOUSE: the block shows the composed surface temperature on one row and the EQUILIBRIUM min/max on the next. A proper surface range already exists — `SurfaceTempProfile.totalMinK` / `.totalMaxK`, which is what the GM body panel decomposes by cause. Use that. Check every other paired figure in the same block while there. **FIXED v2.1.323-beta, exactly as scoped — and the SWEEP found a second row of the same class, which is the more valuable half.** Root cause confirmed at `bodyFacts.ts:92-93`: one row printed `temperatureK` (the composed SURFACE temperature) and the next printed `equilibriumTempMinK/MaxK`, which omit the greenhouse and every other heat term. Now `temperatureRangeK`, which `surfaceTempProfile` builds as `mean ± rss(swings)` and therefore brackets `temperatureK` **by construction**, not by luck. **Verified numerically over the whole bundled Sol map rather than on the reported body: 34 of 34 means sit inside the surface range; 11 of 34 sat outside the equilibrium range that was being shown** — Venus (754 K against "229–231"), Earth (287 against "252–256"), all four giants, Io, Europa, Titan, Mimas. **Degradation needed no fallback and this was checked rather than assumed:** `processEnvironment` returns early for stars (`SystemProcessor.ts:356`) so a star has NEITHER field, and `importFixup`'s `DERIVED_FIELDS` strips and re-derives both together — so there is no state where one exists without the other, and the row simply drops. Confirmed live: Sol prints "Surface temp 5,778 K" and no range row. **THE SWEEP, item by item, so the next session need not redo it.** (a) **"Surface liquid" was the second bug, same class, and is fixed in the same commit.** The row printed raw `hydrosphere.coverage` and hard-coded the word "liquid", while the PHASE is separately derived and published as a `hydrosphere/*` tag — so the block contradicted its own Tags row two lines below. Europa read "Surface liquid 100% water" beside "Frozen surface: water", and Europa's liquid is famously UNDER the ice; **five of Sol's seven hydrosphere bodies are frozen** (Europa, Ganymede, Callisto, Enceladus, Triton). The label now follows the tag — surface liquid / surface ice / surface volatile, marked boiled off or supercritical — reading a published derived value, so no physics moved. (b) **Radiation band vs range: a real but INVISIBLE inconsistency, recorded as B8, not fixed.** The mean is composed from the per-star spectral components while the endpoints are rebuilt from a flat photon/particle fraction times the range total, so the mean can land marginally outside its own range — measured on Sol, 3 of 34 bodies (Neptune, Triton, Neptune's Rings), worst excess ~0.4%, which rounds away at the block's 1 d.p. Physics, and out of scope here. (c) **Day length vs "tidally locked" is the same class and is already B7** — an authored spin against a derived lock. Cross-referenced there. (d) **Type vs Surface temp is the same class and is already B6** — the classifier keys on `Teq_K` for 26 fingerprints. (e) **Ruled out, with the reason:** Orbit distance / Orbital period (the period is derived FROM `a_AU`, or for a binary member from the `n_rad_per_s` both members share, which comes from the same separation); Mass / Radius / Gravity / Density / Ascent Δv (one source pair, `massKg` + `radiusKm`); Atmosphere pressure / Air mix (one `body.atmosphere`, and atmospheric escape rewrites both together); Habitability (reads `planet.temperatureK`, the surface figure, and scores its solvent off the phase-aware fluid-layer model, so it agrees with both corrected rows). **Blast radius, and it is wider than the batch brief assumed: `bodyFacts` has FOUR consumers, not two** — the shared `buildGuideDocument` (so `DocPanel` AND `FilteredDocumentView`), the legacy `CatalogueBrowser` Field Guide, the holo HUD info card (`catalogue/+page.svelte:611`) and a direct fact list (`:893`). One line reaches all of them. **Verified on both document consumers, per F7/F9's lesson:** `FilteredDocumentView` (Venus 480 °C / 463–499 °C; Jupiter −114 / −129 to −98; Europa "Surface ice 100% water") and `DocPanel` (Europa identical on all three rows). **One judgement call worth knowing:** on a tidally-heated body the surface range is WIDE, because the profile's upper bound is the tidal-hotspot term — Pandora now reads 13 °C to 1,197 °C and Europa −250 °C to 401 °C. That is honest, it is inside the physics, and it is character-for-character what the GM's own body panel already prints, which is the point of a shared figure. | fixed v2.1.323-beta |

| A6 | **In the DOCUMENT view of a system, the star's page should list its natural satellites — the planets — exactly as a planet lists its moons.** It currently does not, so the only way down into the system from the star is the picker at the top, and that is sometimes ineffective. Every other body in the document gets a satellite list; the star is the one node missing it, and it is the node you land on first (E1 preselects the primary star on entry), so the omission is on the FIRST page a player sees. Treat it as the same list the planets already render — `buildGuideDocument` / `systemTopology` — not a new bespoke block. Fixing this also gives the document a working navigation path that does not depend on the picker at all. **FIXED v2.1.324-beta.** **Root cause: the helper existed and the drill-in section simply never called it.** `guideDocument.ts` built its navigator from `membersOf` / `moonsOf` / `constructsOf` only; `listBodiesOf` — the direct-orbiters-in-orbit-order query the BodyPicker and the legacy `CatalogueBrowser` chip row both already use — was never imported. So this was a missing call, not missing topology. Same block shape as the moons, same `drillItems`, and using `listBodiesOf` rather than `planetsOf` deliberately, so BELTS are listed too instead of being pickable only by tapping the chart. Heading is chosen by content: `Planets`, `Belts`, or `Planets & belts`. **Keyed on `selected.id`, NOT `subject.id`**, because `subject` has already been resolved from a barycentre to its dominant member, and a circumbinary planet hangs off the barycentre. **The de-duplication filter is load-bearing twice, and both cases are real rather than defensive:** (1) `orbiters()` excludes only moons, and **Pluto is `roleHint: 'planet'` parented to the Pluto-Charon barycentre**, so that page would have offered Pluto as its own satellite; (2) `membersOf` matches on `n.parentId === bary.id` as well as `memberIds`, so a circumbinary planet is ALREADY claimed as a barycentre "member" and would have been listed twice. **Verified numerically over the whole campaign, not on the reported body: all 60 stars in 42 systems, every list length compared against the ground truth read straight from the data — 33 stars list 92 bodies between them, 0 mismatches, and the 27 stars with no orbiters correctly render no block.** Also swept all 210 body pages for a page offering ITSELF as a drill target: none. **Verified live** on `FilteredDocumentView`: Sol lists Mercury→Kuiper Belt in orbit order with belts carrying the `⋯` glyph, and Sol → Jupiter → Europa drills all the way down without touching the picker. In the Alpha Centauri triple: Rigil Kentaurus → Polyphemus, Proxima → its two planets, Toliman correctly empty. **Honestly NOT verified on live data: the circumbinary (P-type) path, because the bundled campaign contains no such body** — 20 barycentres, none with a non-member planet child. Proved on a synthetic fixture instead, where both circumbinary planets appear exactly once. **Three things found while doing it, all recorded rather than fixed:** duplicate node IDs in two bundled systems ([[D3]]), the "Moons" heading being used for companion STARS on a barycentre page ([[A25]]), and a ringed planet's ring appearing in no drill list at all ([[A26]]). | fixed v2.1.324-beta |

## F — Player Views: preset editor (one batch, 2026-07-30)

All from a single pass over the preset editor. Several may share a root — F4/F5 are both "the preview
re-frames when it should not", F2/F3 are both "true-scale mode breaks proportion".

| # | Observation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| F1 | **Body graphics were being drawn on the SYSTEM MAP itself. They must NEVER be.** Body graphics (photo / disc / sphere) are for the INFO BLOCK only; the map always uses the 2D or 3D render. This was stated once before during the D5/E2 work and fixed for the 3D list — so this is either a regression or the fix never covered 2D. **Highest priority in this bucket: it is a rule, not a preference.** Cause of the recurrence: the earlier fix (f947826) forced sphere for `holo3d` only and deliberately LEFT the control on the 2D map, because a flat body sprite was then considered a legitimate 2D-map look. Fixed at the source instead — `bodyGfx` is gone from `HoloStyle` and from the scene entirely (type, setter, render branch, `buildBodyDisc`), so no map style can carry one. | fixed v2.1.286-beta |
| F2 | At TRUE scale, the "glow" points are far too large and do not stay in proportion to the orbit lines — an oversaturated field of giant blobs. One root with F3: everything the scene draws at a FIXED size (minimum body radius, wireframe glow vertices, belt rubble, ring particles) was sized for the READABLE end of the dial and then applied at every setting. `markerScale()` now shrinks all of them with the dial, floored at 2% of readable. 2D and 3D are the same engine, so both. | fixed v2.1.288-beta |
| F3 | Same at true scale for RINGS and BELTS — they lose proportion when zoomed to actual sizes rather than readable sizes. The diagnosis was right: same root as F2, same fix. **Residual, see A9:** a body can be VISIBLE at true scale or in PROPORTION at true scale, not both — Earth's true radius is about 0.05 px at whole-system framing. The floor survives at 2%, so bodies are still over-sized against their orbits at the extreme end; the belt no longer buries them. | fixed v2.1.288-beta |
| F4 | Changing a general setting (e.g. belt type) re-zooms the preview to the star. Appearance settings should never move the camera. `setFraming` was the one setter in the scene that did not bail when handed the values it already held — it re-armed its 48-frame ease on every call, and `applyStyle` calls it for every style object, which the editor rebuilds on every keystroke. | fixed v2.1.290-beta |
| F5 | "Frame whole system (never zoom to a body)" did not show the whole system in the preview. The distance was a fixed `GRID_RADIUS * 1.5` with no reference to the lens: at fov 45 that fits a half-extent of about 7.5 of the 12 units a system occupies, so the outer third was always off the edge. Now the same fov/aspect maths `frameDistance` already used for a single body — then fitted to the BOUNDING SPHERE rather than a flat extent, so a tilted shot does not clip either (v2.1.298). | fixed v2.1.291-beta, v2.1.298-beta |
| F6 | 2D low-poly / wireframe render styles apply to the STAR but not to the bodies. Same root as F1: those styles only exist on the sphere path, and the 2D map's bodies were flat sprites. The star is never a sprite, which is why it alone obeyed. | fixed v2.1.286-beta |
| F7 | Body Graphics → photo: nothing appears in the preview until you leave the tab and come back. The loader was keyed on the selected body alone, and the line that clears the image for every non-photo mode stamped that same key — so switching TO photo without changing body looked, to the loader, like nothing had happened. Keyed on subject AND mode now, in `DocPanel` and `FilteredDocumentView` alike (both carried the same pair of lines). | fixed v2.1.292-beta |
| F8 | **VERIFIED, no re-unification needed.** One info-block component: the catalogue renders a single `inspectorAside` snippet for the holo AND the 2D-map tiers, containing exactly one `<DocPanel>`. `DocPanel` and `FilteredDocumentView` import the same builder (`buildGuideDocument`), the same theme resolver (`makeDocTheme`) and the same renderer (`renderDocument`). The settings DID reach both — but two arrived wrong, which is what the 2D-only testing hid: F7 and F9 were both found by this check, and both were faults in the SHARED layer rather than in one surface. One cosmetic gap left, not worth changing: `DocPanel` does not pass `navStyle` to `makeDocTheme`, harmless because panel mode builds no navigator block. | verified v2.1.296-beta |
| F9 | The selected font COLOUR should carry through to the headings in the info box, **including Rainbow**. Two faults in one line of `makeDocTheme`. (1) It flattened the `rainbow` sentinel to a hex before passing the theme on, and the rainbow-heading path in `renderDocument` tests for exactly that sentinel — so those headings had never rendered in ANY surface since v2.1.266, not merely failed to reach `DocPanel`. (2) The preset accent was not part of the colour seeding at all, so choosing a colour moved the chrome and left headings on the document style default. The accent now outranks the style seed for the accent + heading slots; an explicitly tweaked slot still outranks both. A follow-up found the live player view passing the pre-flattened accent to the engine while the editor passed the raw one — the exact preview-vs-live drift the shared resolver exists to prevent. | fixed v2.1.293-beta, v2.1.296-beta |
| F10 | The info-panel WIDTH slider should show its effect live as you drag, and should read as a percentage of the screen rather than a raw pixel count. Now a stored FRACTION of the viewport (`inspectorWidthPct`), converted to the pixels of whatever display it lands on when a preset is applied — a proportion travels from the GM's screen to a player's, a pixel count does not. The preview drew the raw pixel figure capped at 340, so the top half of the slider's travel moved nothing; it is a percentage of the stage now. | fixed v2.1.294-beta |
| F11 | **FEATURE, not a bug.** Pin the info panel to top / bottom / left / right to change the look and feel, with the view sliding to re-frame around it the way it already does when the panel appears. | captured, feature |

## G — Features (deliberately not bugs — do not hand these to a bug batch)

| # | Observation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| G2 | **Document navigation links flow side by side and wrap, and that is now the DEFAULT.** Requested directly 2026-08-01 while the A25/A26 work was being verified: "sometimes it is many rows — side by side buttons is the default". The drill-in lists drew one full-width row per item, so Sol's thirteen planets turned the end of a page into a column of links; A26 had just added a Rings row on top. **Built as a third `NavStyle`, `chips`**, alongside the existing `plain` and `boxed` — laid out inside `drawList` rather than as a new block kind, because it is the same list with a different arrangement, so every drill-in list (planets, moons, rings, companions, constructs, the parent row) got it at once without any of them knowing. **The one real risk was hit-testing**, since side-by-side chips share a y band where rows never did: each chip pushes an X-BOUNDED region, which the document's hit test already prefers when present (the schematic's boxes work the same way). Verified by clicking Jupiter out of thirteen chips on one line and landing on Jupiter. An over-long name ellipsises to a full-width chip rather than running off the edge — the one way a flowing layout can break where rows cannot. **Default changed to `chips` for new presets**, per the request; `boxed` and `plain` remain selectable and are relabelled "Buttons — one per row" and "Plain text". A preset already stored keeps whatever it has. **FOLLOW-UP v2.1.331: in rainbow mode each button carries the hue of that body's MARKER on the schematic above.** Asked for as "can each of those buttons be a different colour?", then refined to "make those colours match the planet colour on the diagram above". **Built by SHARING the index rather than re-deriving it:** `systemSchematic` now exports `rainbowHue` and `rainbowHueIndex`, and its own drawer consumes the exported builder instead of an inline copy — so there is one map, and a chip and its dot cannot drift apart by construction. The builder attaches `ListItem.color`; the renderer only paints what it is handed, which keeps the look in the data layer rather than in the drawer. **Off-chart items are handled honestly:** moons and constructs are not drawn on the schematic and have no index, so they fall back to their position in their own list — varied, but not pretending to match a marker that is not there. Monochrome ignores the colour entirely, the same exemption the rainbow headings take. **The `hsl()` colour forced one change:** `hexA` only parses `#rrggbb` and silently returns its input, which would have painted a fully opaque chip — the tint goes through `globalAlpha` instead, which works whatever the format. **Verified across BOTH bundled maps: 490 navigator buttons that point at a charted body, ZERO whose colour differs from `rainbowHue(rainbowHueIndex(system).get(id))` — the exact expression the schematic's `markerCol` paints the dot with.** A pixel check of the drawn markers agreed on 7 of 12 and was INCONCLUSIVE on the rest, which is worth recording rather than hiding: each disagreement sampled a NEIGHBOUR's hue (Earth read Venus's, Pluto and the Kuiper Belt read Neptune's) because the schematic's hit boxes overlap on a crowded row, so it is a limitation of the sampler and not a colour fault. The parent-nav row was found uncoloured by that sweep and fixed in the same pass. | shipped v2.1.330-331-beta |

| # | Observation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| G1 | **More ARRANGEMENTS for the starmap textual view — a form, pickable boxes, star/planet icons.** Requested 2026-07-31. Today `buildStarmapDocument` (`catalogue/document/starmapDocument.ts:30`) emits exactly ONE shape: heading, optional italic description, `rule`, and a single `list` block of one row per system with a "1 star · 8 planets · 12 moons" sub-line. **The engine is far more capable than that view uses** — `blocks.ts` defines twelve block kinds (`heading, text, keyValue, tags, list, image, bodyDisc, rule, spacer, columnStart, columnEnd, schematic`) and `ListStyle` alone already offers six (`plain, illustrated-bullets, numbered-dossier, terminal-log, ledger, manifest`). The starmap document uses four blocks, no list style and no `keyValue` at all. So most of this is arranging capability that already exists. **Four candidates, cheapest first.** (1) DOSSIER/FORM — per system a level-2 heading then a stack of `keyValue` rows (Primary / Companions / Planets / Moons / Depth / Distance) then a `rule`. Literally a form, ZERO new block kinds, and the most different from what is there. Build this one first. (2) STAR-GLYPH CATALOGUE — a row of real coloured discs per system, primary large, companions smaller, planets a trailing run. `bodyDisc` already draws "from the body's true colour" and `systemVisualStars` already returns the star list, so the colours are DERIVED. **Hard rule: do not add a class-to-colour lookup table for this** — that is precisely the renderer shortcut that was reverted before. Needs one small addition: a glyph ROW of several small discs inline, rather than the current one-disc-per-block. (3) PICKABLE BOXES — **note the trap:** `columnStart`/`columnEnd` is NOT a general grid, it is image-specific (a left image strip with text flowing right), so a true 2-3 column card grid needs new layout capability in the renderer and is a real job. The cheap 80% is a new `ListStyle: 'cards'` — each item a bordered full-width box that highlights when selected. One enum value, no layout engine. Ship that and see whether the grid is still wanted. (4) SCHEMATIC PER SYSTEM — `SchematicBlock` already exists and takes a whole `System`, and `systemSchematic.ts` already draws them; a small schematic per system would be the standout look and reuses machinery already paid for. Most work of the four. **THE DESIGN STEER THAT MATTERS MORE THAN ANY OF THEM:** make this ONE new `layout` option on `StarmapDocOpts` and the preset, composing WITH the existing `documentStyle`, `listStyle` and colouration — a matrix (layout x style), not four bespoke views. That is a lot of distinct looks for very little code and it preserves the one-builder-one-renderer property F8 verified and that caught F7 and F9. **Follow-on, not part of this:** the SYSTEM-level document (`guideDocument`) has the same single-shape limitation, so whatever lands here should extend to it later. **RAINBOW — an open design question per arrangement, to be answered while building rather than up front.** When the preset accent is `rainbow`, what does the rainbow colour here? Either each ROW or BOX takes a different part of the spectrum walking down the list — the system document already does something like this, giving each drill-in item a hue (`guideDocument.ts:64-71`, `rainbowHue()`) — or it applies to the LABELS and headings only, leaving rows in the document's normal colours. It may well differ by arrangement: a stack of cards can carry a hue each without shouting, where a dense form probably wants it on the field labels alone. Set the colour in the BUILDER, not the renderer (`blocks.ts:105`). **One landmine:** never flatten the `'rainbow'` sentinel before `renderDocument` sees it — doing so is what broke the Guide's rainbow headings from v2.1.266 until F9 found it (`documentStyles.ts:85-86`). Check every arrangement in Rainbow as well as the plain styles. | captured, feature |

## B — Physics engine (needs someone holding that context)

| # | Observation | Status |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | `albedo.ts` derives its OWN clouds (`CONDENSE_BOIL`, `teqK < boil * 1.6`) while `cloudDecks.ts` is documented as "THE single evaluation". They disagree on Adrian: albedo declares a CO2 deck, the column physics reports none. The crude model is UPSTREAM of everything. **Circular** — albedo → Teq → profile → decks → albedo. | fixed v2.1.282-beta |
| B2 | Greenhouse looks strong: Adrian +337 K from 8 bar CO2, vs Venus +505 K from 92 bar. Checked while fixing B1: **not downstream of it.** Adrian's greenhouse is +336.8 K before AND after — B1 moved the Teq underneath it (246 → 304 K), not the greenhouse term. The ratio is the model's own shape: forcing goes as `log(1 + sqrt(100·pp))`, then the response is `log` again, so partial pressure enters twice-logarithmically and saturates hard (Adrian pp 7.3 → 3.33, Venus pp 88.8 → 4.56, a ratio of 0.73 on 8.7% of the pressure). `broadening = min(1, sqrt(P))` is also pinned at 1 for anything over 1 bar, so it distinguishes nothing above that. Then CHECKED against every anchor the Solar System offers, not just Venus: Mars 0.006 bar +5.8 K (measured 6), Earth 1.013 bar +32.8 (33), Titan 1.45 bar +14.1 (12), Venus 92 bar +523.6 (505). It hits all four across four orders of magnitude of pressure. There is no measurement anywhere near 8 bar, so the apparent anomaly is an intuition with nothing to check it against, and re-shaping the curve to satisfy it would put the four anchors it does hit at risk. **Recommend no change** unless a real anchor turns up. | wont-fix — model matches every anchor there is |
| B9 | **A newly created system has rotation period 0 and magnetic field 0 — on stars especially, though it is not only stars.** Reported 2026-07-31: "I thought we generated magnetic field... magnetars do... perhaps we dropped that ball". **TWO SEPARATE FAULTS WEARING ONE COAT. Do not fix them as one.** **(a) The cheap one: a placeholder zero is a CLAIM, not an absence.** `core/BodyFactory.ts` initialises every manually created body with `axial_tilt_deg: 0`, `rotation_period_hours: 0` (`:20-21`) and `magneticField: { strengthGauss: 0 }` (`:36-38`), under the comment "Default Physical Properties (Avoid NaNs)". Nothing fills them in afterwards. This makes the two reported symptoms ONE root, not two: `SystemProcessor.ts:775` overwrites `magneticField` from `deriveMagnetism`, and that model derives strength from ROTATION plus composition plus core size — so a zero rotation produces a zero field by construction. It hits manually created PLANETS as well as stars. Whether the fix is to leave the fields absent (letting a derivation see "unknown" rather than "zero") or to derive a plausible primordial spin at creation is the design call; note D2a already argued rotation is the obvious next inference, and that "inferred must be distinguishable from measured" or the map starts asserting invented facts. **(b) The real one: there is no stellar rotation or magnetism model at all.** A procedurally generated star gets a field only if its rule-pack template happens to carry `mag_gauss` (`generation/star.ts:32-33`) — otherwise `starMagneticField` stays undefined — and nothing anywhere derives a star's rotation. `deriveMagnetism` is a PLANETARY DYNAMO model (rotation + core + composition); pointing it at a star would be wrong physics, not a shortcut. Magnetars are precisely the case that exposes the gap: their 1e14-1e15 gauss comes from flux and angular-momentum conservation through collapse, i.e. it is derivable from mass and end state rather than authored. **This belongs with the banked HR-diagram / stellar-generation phase** ([[project_sse_v2_hr_stellar]]), which already planned to put stellar age, lifespan and TIME into the stellar model — rotation is the same family, since a star spins down with age and its field follows. Do NOT build a bespoke stellar field formula in isolation ahead of that work. Related: [[D2]], [[D2a]], [[B7]]. **FIXED (a) v2.1.332-beta; (b) DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT — see the last paragraph.** **Root cause of (a) confirmed, and one HALF of the triage above is wrong, which matters because it is the half that looked like the whole bug.** The placeholder zeros are real: `BodyFactory` stamps `axial_tilt_deg: 0`, `rotation_period_hours: 0` and `magneticField: { strengthGauss: 0 }` on every body it makes. But **"a zero rotation produces a zero field by construction" is NOT true** — `rotationFactor()` (`magnetism.ts:45-49`) has an explicit `if (!h) return 0.6; // unknown -> middling` branch, and 0 is falsy, so it takes it. Reproduced: a manually-created Earth-mass planet processes to **0.3 G**, not zero. **And `deriveMagnetism` never runs on a star at all** — it lives in `processClassification`, which returns at `:539` for anything that is not a planet or a moon. So the reported symptom had a different cause entirely. **The real cause of the zero FIELD: there are two star-creation paths and only one of them read the pack.** `generation/star.ts` drew `strengthGauss` from the class's `mag_gauss` band; `generateFromConfig.ts`'s `starSeedToBody` — **the path the generation wizard uses**, which is exactly "create a new system" — set nothing, so BodyFactory's zero survived to the screen. Both now go through one `starFieldFromPack`, and it looks up the EVOLVED class, so a 13 Gyr G-star draws the white-dwarf band. Measured: wizard stars went 0 G to **7.86 G (G, band 1-10), 433 G (M, band 100-1000), 4.6e5 G (WD, band 1e4-1e6)**. It takes its own rng stream keyed on the star id so no existing seed's planets move — verified byte-identical output for three legacy-random seeds. **This is NOT (b), and the distinction is the point.** The pack already carries every class from `star/BH` at [0,0] to `star/magnetar` at [1e11, 1e15], so the magnetar case the report asked about is answered by DATA, which is where the architecture rule says it belongs. Reading a band is not a dynamo model. **The zeros themselves are gone.** All three fields are simply absent now; every reader already guarded with `?? 0` / `|| 0`, `planet.ts:537` already set `magneticField = undefined`, and `generateFromConfig.ts:52` already worked around the tilt placeholder with a falsy test and a comment about it. The star editor shows rotation and tilt EMPTY with a "not set" note rather than a confident 0, and clearing either box deletes the field instead of writing zero — otherwise the honest state is unreachable once you leave it. **The derived diff is EMPTY, and that is correct rather than suspicious:** the Sol baseline is loaded from JSON and never passes through `BodyFactory`. Verified on generated systems instead. **(b) THE STELLAR ROTATION/MAGNETISM MODEL IS NOT BUILT, deliberately, and stays open.** Nothing derives a star's spin; `deriveMagnetism` is a planetary dynamo and pointing it at a star would be wrong physics rather than a shortcut. Scoped for the HR / stellar-generation phase, where age already belongs: a star spins down with age (Skumanich, roughly t^-1/2) and its field follows, so ROTATION is the derived quantity and the field falls out of it — which is also what makes a magnetar derivable (flux and angular-momentum conservation through collapse) rather than authored. Until then a star's field is a pack band and its rotation is honestly absent. **Two things found while doing this, recorded separately: [[B10]] and [[B11]].** | fixed v2.1.332-beta (a) — (b) scoped, NOT built |
| G3 | **FEATURE, DESIGN INVESTIGATION ONLY: ship appearance — lightweight 3D models for constructs.** Raised 2026-08-01 as a future requirement, explicitly an idle thought to be investigated rather than built. The want: a construct should be able to carry a simple 3D model the way a body can carry a photo, as an ALTERNATIVE OR ADDITIONAL upload beside the ship image — "like planets they can have photos or 3D viz". Lots of PRE-EXISTING models rather than bespoke art, kept lightweight, with procedurally applied texture/colour options so a handful of models covers many ships. STL was floated on the grounds that 3D-printing models are abundant; that wants testing rather than assuming, since printing meshes carry no materials or UVs and tend to be very high-poly. **This ANSWERS A30, which is already open and deliberately unanswered** — A30 removed the wrong picture (a ship illustrated with a planet's sphere) and did not choose a right one, so a construct currently has a blank where every body has a graphic. Any outcome here should close A30 or explicitly leave it to a cheaper interim. **Three things already established, so the investigation does not have to find them.** (1) The upload path EXISTS: `image?: ImageRef` on the node type (`types.ts:308`), with `ImageRef.custom` meaning a GM-uploaded picture the processor will not overwrite. (2) three.js is ALREADY a dependency and `three/examples/jsm/*` is already imported (`holo/filteredCanvas.ts`, `holo/galleryScene.ts`), so a `GLTFLoader` is an import rather than a new dependency. (3) **The load-bearing assumption a model BREAKS:** the scene treats a construct as "a fixed-screen-size glyph with no rendered globe, so it contributes no radius" (`holo/scene.ts:1722`). Real geometry has extent, which touches framing, the A9 true-scale floor and the bounding sphere F5 frames the whole system by. **The design tension to address head-on rather than trip over:** body graphics (photo/disc/sphere) are INFO-BLOCK ONLY and must never appear on the system map — a rule that has come back twice and was closed at source in v2.1.286. Whether a 3D ship model on the 3D map is a violation of that rule or its opposite (the honest render of a thing that really is that shape) is a genuine question, and the answer decides whether this is an info-block feature or a scene feature. Do not assume either. Related: [[A30]], [[F1]]. **INVESTIGATED 2026-08-01 — full design at `docs/dev/ship-appearance-design.md`; read that before building any of it. THE RECOMMENDATION IN FOUR SENTENCES.** (1) **Format: GLB, meshopt- or Draco-compressed; STL and OBJ are accepted at upload and CONVERTED, never stored or bundled** — measured on identical ~30k-triangle geometry, Draco GLB is 111 KB where binary STL is 1,461 KB with no materials/UVs/units, and printing STLs average ~126k triangles across NASA's own repo; every loader and decoder needed already ships inside the existing `three@0.169` dependency, so zero new packages. (2) **Sourcing: bundled models must be CC0/public domain and enough exist** — NASA 3D Resources (public domain, 257 GLBs, real craft mostly under 500 KB; a measured spectrum from fully textured PBR (Hubble, InSight) to per-part flat-colour materials (Voyager, Juno) — none of it bare geometry, and textured craft fit the budget after a 512 px texture downscale: Hubble 1,655→128 KB), Kenney Space Kit (CC0, 150 models) and Quaternius Ultimate Space Kit (CC0, 92) are verified; which hull a ship uses is EXPLICITLY ASSIGNED, never inferred from the free-form class string. (3) **The map-versus-info-block question: BOTH, DIFFERENTLY, and the scene half is NOT an F1 violation** — the info block gets a lit turntable viewer (the `bodyDisc` sphere is the exact precedent), and the 3D scene may draw the model ONLY AS THE MARKER (sprite slot, same focus rule, glyph below a legibility threshold) because unlike a pasted photo the model IS the honest render of the hull's shape; the 2D plan view and starmap keep the glyph, a GM photo stays info-block-only, and the model must keep contributing ZERO radius to ring clearance and F5 framing. (4) **The binding constraint is the broadcast, not storage** — `sendIfChanged` re-stringifies and re-sends the whole snapshot on any change, so custom models must live in a hash-addressed side store sent once per session, not as inline data URLs. Dressing (tint from `icon_color`, decal from `icon_type`, status from tags once constructs have them) keeps the tags-drive-the-image rule; five owner decisions are flagged at the end of the doc, the first being whether the scene marker (Phase 2, severable) is wanted at all. **STEER 2026-08-01: the primary use case is USER-UPLOADED SF ships; NASA/bundled is rarely used.** Phasing reordered accordingly — Phase 1 is the full import pipeline (GLB, STL and OBJ with convert-at-import, `meshopt_simplifier` for high-poly printing STLs, orientation/up-axis preview, `icon_color` tint for material-less meshes), and the bundled starter set drops to optional Phase 4. The `meshoptimizer` dependency (MIT) moves from flagged-optional to recommended-accept: without it the front door rejects most fan models. **ALL FIVE OWNER DECISIONS TAKEN 2026-08-02 — the doc's section 11 should be updated to match by whoever builds this.** (1) **SCENE MARKER: YES, as Phase 2**, severable as designed. A ship model in a 3D scene is the honest render of a thing that really is that shape, unlike a photo sprite which is a fake — but Phase 1 ships without it and the never-put-body-graphics-on-the-map rule ([[F1]]) must be re-read before Phase 2 starts. (2) **`meshoptimizer`: ACCEPTED** (MIT, ~50 KB, Phase 1). (3) **UPLOAD CAPS: ACCEPTED as proposed** — warn at 500 KB, hard limit 2 MB post-conversion. (4) **STARTER SET: a SMALL NASA-ONLY set.** Real public-domain craft, matching the engine's physically-derived tone and sidestepping the low-poly-reads-game-y tension entirely. Stylised CC0 game-asset libraries are NOT wanted. Keep it small — this is Phase 4 and uploads lead. (5) **LICENSING: CC0 **AND** CC-BY, not CC0-only** — the doc's recommendation was overruled, so **attribution plumbing is now IN SCOPE and does not currently exist**. Note the interaction the decisions create: the bundled starter set is NASA public-domain and needs no attribution, so the plumbing exists for USER-SUPPLIED and shared models rather than for anything shipped. Design it for that case, and be clear where attribution is displayed and how it survives an export or a share. **PHASE 1 BUILT, v2.1.387-394-beta (2026-08-03), four commits in the stage order the prompt asked for.** What shipped: `ModelRef` on the node + hash-addressed IndexedDB model store (own DB, so no shared-DB version bump) + GLB/STL/OBJ parsing with Draco and meshopt decoding wired (decoder vendored to `static/draco/`, Apache-2.0 from three's own distribution); conversion with meshopt simplification (30k+ triangles down to ~20k — `meshoptimizer` accepted per decision 2), warn 500 KB / hard 2 MB caps, and a pass-through that stores a compliant GLB BYTE-IDENTICAL so upstream compression survives; the import modal (preview shows the CONVERTED bytes, 90-degree orientation fix stored on the ref and applied at view time, attribution fields with CC-BY-needs-credit flagged); and the info block on BOTH consumers — turntable in the reserved `__bodygfx` gap via one shared renderer (`modelViewer.ts`, also the modal's preview, so approval and display cannot drift), flat-shaded `icon_color` finish with EdgesGeometry panel lines for material-less meshes, credit line beneath, A38 filter capture honoured, icon-glyph fallback on a store miss. Priority chain photo > model > glyph pinned by test. **HONESTLY NOT SEEN BY A HUMAN EYE: the browser pane would not composite (the standing-rules case), so verification is 52 passing unit tests including a full STL-to-GLB-and-back round trip, a green build, and a clean boot of the built bundle. FIRST HUMAN PASS SHOULD CHECK: upload a printing STL — the preview should show a tinted faceted hull with panel-line edges, not grey; save and the info-block panel should show the same turntable; on a CRT-filtered player view the model must render INSIDE the phosphor effect, not float above it; and the credit line must appear under the model when licence or credit is set.** Three follow-ups recorded unnumbered in Captured: export .json does not yet carry binaries (helpers ready, call sites in `+page.svelte` named, file was hot), remote players need a broadcast fetch-by-hash (VTT territory), and the pre-existing `bodyGfxSystem` second copy. Phase 2 (scene marker) NOT started, by instruction — re-read [[F1]] before it ever is. **SUPERSEDED BY TWO OWNER-DIRECTED ROUNDS THE SAME DAY (v2.1.396-397), after live testing.** The owner cleared the hot files ("no one else is touching code — you CAN integrate"), so both deferred integrations landed: the .json export embeds model binaries (restored on load, hash-verified) and remote players fetch missing models by hash over the broadcast (`REQUEST_MODEL`/`SYNC_MODEL`, one-shot, chunked). Feedback fixes: the GM construct pane got the missing picture (new `ConstructPortrait`, chain model > image > glyph — the GM had NO graphic at all, which was the reported "does not appear in the GM view"); the player document now puts the MODEL first too (was photo-first, changed on the owner's "if a construct is told to be 3D, display it first"); and the import modal aligns by the MAIN DRIVE against an orange marker with the convention NOSE +Z / DRIVE -Z stored on the ref. **And the scene half was built ON THE OWNER'S EXPLICIT DIRECTION, superseding the stop above** ("zooming into a construct on the player view in 3D should show the ship, orientated correctly, standard framing"): the focused construct's glyph gives way to the actual hull, nose-first along its motion, FLIPPED during a deceleration burn (torch ships brake engines-first — decided from the transit sampler's velocities, threshold above solar gravity so a coast never reads as a burn), with a stern drive plume whose length/light scale with the fraction of the ship's OWN capability in use (host computes max accel from the pack; the scene never reads pack data). The F1 conditions from design §6 are all honoured: model-as-marker only, zero radius contributed to clearance/F5 framing, 2D views keep the glyph, GM photo stays info-block-only. **VERIFIED LIVE IN THE BROWSER** (the pane composited this session): STL upload → modal preview with drive marker → GM pane turntable → player Holo Table view shows the hull in the Sol scene at the ISS with "Model: wedge-ship by Test Modeller (CC-BY)" beneath the info-block turntable. **Still needing a human eye: the brake flip and the plume** (needs a ship with a real journey mid-burn), the CRT-filter capture of the model (A38 path), and a CC-BY model surviving an export/share round trip. Follow-ups banked in Captured (nozzle picker, shading option, wire-style parity, plume colour as pack data). | Phase 1 + owner-directed scene round built v2.1.387-397-beta — brake/plume/CRT need a human eye |
| G4 | **Grid styling on the player 3D starmap: keep the Traveller-hex 3D look, fade the grid with distance, and give every grid type the same treatment.** Requested 2026-08-01. Four parts, and they are separable. (1) **The Traveller-hex 3D look is liked and should be the model** — whatever gives those hexes their dimensional quality should become an option the other grid types can take. (2) **BRIGHTNESS FALLOFF: the initial hexes need to be bright and it needs to be fainter after.** Read as a distance or depth fade from the focus outward, so the grid reads as ground near the eye and does not fight the starmap further out. Applies to POLAR, SQUARES and HEX as well as Traveller-hex — all four should offer it. Note POLAR does not exist in `Grid.svelte` today (typed `'grid' | 'hex' | 'traveller-hex' | 'none'`), so it is either a new type or lives in a different renderer — establish which before scoping. (3) **Traveller Map specifically wants thicker SUBSECTOR EDGES and HEX NUMBERING**, both of which are conventions of the real Traveller map and are what makes it read as one. `travellerMetadata.importedSubsectors` already exists on the starmap type, so the subsector boundaries may already be derivable rather than needing authoring — check before designing. (4) Blocked by [[A37]]: squares do not render at all right now, so fix that first or three quarters of this work cannot be seen. **Constants belong in rule-pack or preset DATA, not in the renderer.** **BUILT v2.1.375-beta, all four parts.** **(4) was unblocked by [[A37]] first**, as the item required - squares now render, so three quarters of this was visible to work on. **(3) IS DONE and landed early, at v2.1.373-374**, because the reported fault forced it: hex and Traveller hex were the SAME PICTURE, so "verify hex and traveller-hex still render unchanged" could not even be answered. Thicker subsector edges are drawn as RIBBONS with real world-space width - THREE ignores line thickness on nearly every platform, so heavier has to be geometry rather than a parameter - and the numbering gate was lowered from 0.5 scene units to 0.22, which is why the numbers had never appeared on a normal map. **`travellerMetadata.importedSubsectors` was checked as the entry suggested and is NOT the source**: it is per-import metadata for hover titles, whereas the boundary is a property of the LATTICE (every 8th column, every 10th row), so it is derived, and the geometry now comes from the same shared generator as the hexes. The boundary is a continuous line including the bridge between columns - see the note at the foot of [[A37]]. **(1) EVERY GRID TYPE NOW TAKES THE SAME TREATMENT, and that meant moving POLAR onto the shared path rather than adding options to it.** The dimensional quality the request liked is the depth curtain under each line; polar was raw `LineLoop`s with a per-ring dim baked in, so it could take neither the depth nor the falloff. Rings and spokes are now edges through `addLattice` like every lattice, which gives both for free. The spokes are SEGMENTED for the same reason the squares had to be - a per-vertex fade judges a full-length spoke by its far end and drops it whole. **(2) BRIGHTNESS FALLOFF IS A DIAL: "Grid falloff", 0 to 100%, on every overlay type including polar.** 0 is even brightness across the field; 100% is bright near cells gone by the edge. One function maps the dial to the fade window, so the numbers sit in ONE place instead of being sprinkled through the renderer, and the dial itself is preset data (`starmapGridFalloff`) as the entry requires. **RULED OUT, since the entry asked it to be established: POLAR is not missing.** It does not exist in `Grid.svelte` because that is the GM's 2D SNAP-grid, a different renderer with a different job; the 3D starmap has had `plain` and `scaled` polar all along, and the shared `MapOverlay` vocabulary already carries both. No new type was needed. **ALIGNMENT AND SCALE VERIFIED against the GM's own grid, not assumed.** `Starmap.svelte` mounts `Grid.svelte` with `originX={0} originY={0}` and `gridSize = 50`, which is `DEFAULT_MAP_CELL`, so both lattices start at map (0,0) at the same cell size; the scene hands the generator `cfg.size * mapK`, the map-to-scene fit, so a GM cell is exactly one player cell. Pinned by two tests that reproduce Grid.svelte's centre formula VERBATIM and compare it against `hexCentres` to nine decimal places over a hundred hexes, plus a linear-scaling check. A system the GM snapped to a hex lands dead-centre in the player's. **COMPLETED v2.1.376-beta - the falloff dial now reaches EVERY view that draws a grid, not only the starmap.** The system stage has its own grid picker and therefore needed its own dial. It has one: a second, separate preset field (`gridFalloff`), **defaulted to 0 so the system view is unchanged unless a GM asks for it** - that view has always been evenly lit and a silent change to the most-used surface in the app is not a feature. Deliberately NOT sharing `starmapGridFalloff`: the grid TYPE already shares one field across both stages, which is a recorded fault, and repeating it here would have been repeating a known mistake. **Making the system view able to fade at all needed a different mechanism from the starmap's, and this is the part worth recording.** `holo/scene.ts` draws its grid through `addGridLines`, which keeps every vertex's ABSOLUTE position for the floating-origin rebase. The fade is computed from those absolute positions, once, at build time - so it stays anchored to the system while rebasing moves the drawn coordinates. A fade computed from the drawn coordinates would have slid with the camera focus, which is the trap `project_sse_v2_floating_origin` exists to warn about. **Found on the way and NOT unified, recorded unnumbered: the system view has a THIRD copy of the lattice geometry**, and it has already drifted - its hexes are POINTY-topped (axial q/r, `60k-30` corners) where the GM grid and both starmaps are FLAT-topped. It is harmless today only because `forSystemScale` folds hex to square before it can be drawn, so the drifted branch is dead. That is luck, not design. **THE CONSTANTS QUESTION, answered rather than fudged.** G4 says constants belong in rule-pack or preset DATA. **The DIAL is preset data**, and that is the thing a GM wants to change. The four numbers the dial interpolates between are the SHAPE of the response curve, not tuning. Putting them in the rule pack WAS built and then BACKED OUT: `createStarmapScene` takes its options at CONSTRUCTION, so a pack swapped afterwards would never reach the scene, and a knob that silently goes stale is worse than one honestly in code. If a schema for renderer curves is ever wanted it should arrive with a setter, not a constructor argument. **CONFIRMED ON SCREEN 2026-08-02 and signed off.** Three changes came out of that pass, all shipped: **(a) THE NUMBERING WAS TRUNCATED, and the screenshots showed it plainly** - numbers covered the left and centre of the field and stopped. The 400-sprite cap was applied in ITERATION order, column by column from the left, so a field of more than 400 hexes lost its right-hand half. **Now selected by CONTENT: a subsector with a system in it is numbered WHOLE, an empty one not at all.** Whole subsectors rather than just the star's hex, because the numbering exists to give a reference frame around what is there and half a numbered block is no frame. Distance-sorting was tried first and rejected - it only moves the ragged edge somewhere less obvious. **(b) A THIRD HEX OVERLAY, `subsector-hex`: the 8x10 boundaries with NO numbering.** The borders are what make a map read as sectored; the numbers are an addressing scheme, and a table can want the first without the second. Adding it put the "is this a hex overlay?" test in five places, so it now has a NAME (`isHexFamily`, `hasSubsectors`) - that is [[A34]]'s failure in miniature and it was one edit from happening. A test asserts all three hex overlays produce the IDENTICAL lattice, so the difference stays what is drawn ON it. **(c) GRID DEPTH IS A SLIDER, not a switch** - how far the curtain hangs is the whole of the effect. Reads a boolean from presets saved before this rather than migrating them. Still 3D-only and still forced to 0 in flat mode. **STILL NOT DONE, and neither was asked for:** no styling for the hex NUMBERS themselves (size, colour), and the falloff curve constants remain in the scene for the reason recorded above. | complete v2.1.378-beta, confirmed on screen |
| G5 | **An opacity control for orbit lines — in GM system settings, and on the player side under system map settings.** Requested 2026-08-01. Straightforward presentation setting with one design point worth stating: the GM's is a LOCAL preference for their own screen, while the player-side one belongs to the PRESET and travels with it, so they are two settings rather than one shared value — the same split [[A29]] settled for Live readings and [[F10]] settled for panel width. Do not wire the player view to a GM-local store; that fault has already appeared twice ([[A10]], [[A3]]). Orbit lines are drawn in the holo scene for both 2D and 3D tiers (one engine), so a single opacity input should reach both by construction — verify it does rather than assuming. | captured, feature |
| G6 | **BANKED FOR LATER: a minimum-mass filter on the printed reports, to cut asteroids and other small bodies out.** Requested 2026-08-01 and explicitly banked rather than queued. A slider setting a minimum mass, with bodies below it omitted from the report, and **the slider showing how many bodies are included and excluded** as you move it — that read-out is the part that makes it usable, because a bare mass figure means nothing to a GM but "42 of 156 bodies" does. Applies to the paper/report tier (`lib/reports/ReportDocument.svelte`), which for a large system currently prints every rock. Worth considering whether the same filter belongs on the guide document and the info surfaces, since the underlying complaint — "there are too many trivial bodies in the way" — is not unique to paper; but the request was specifically about reports and should not be widened without asking. **REFINED 2026-08-02, and the refinement is the good part.** (1) **The user sets the gate; the app does not guess.** Explicitly framed as "doing a mass gate slider you can set rather than try and tell you what you are not interested in" — so no heuristic, no clever inference about which bodies matter. A slider and a number. (2) **Show the MOST MASSIVE BODY EXCLUDED, not merely a count.** "42 of 156 included" tells a GM nothing about whether the gate is set right; "the largest thing you are hiding is Ceres" tells them instantly. That single read-out is what makes the control usable, and it should update live as the slider moves, before the report is generated. **Show BOTH — the count is wanted too, confirmed 2026-08-02: "we can still have the numbers so they know how many have been excluded."** So the read-out carries how many are in, how many are out, AND the largest one out. They answer different questions and neither substitutes for the other: the count tells a GM the SCALE of what the gate is doing ("you are hiding 114 of 156 things"), while the named body tells them whether the gate is in the RIGHT PLACE ("...and the biggest is Ceres"). A worker should treat neither as optional. **THE PATTERN ALREADY EXISTS AND IS TESTED — REUSE IT RATHER THAN REINVENTING.** The ubox importer solves the identical problem: `import/ubox/convert.ts:57` takes `options.minBodyMassKg` defaulting to `RECOMMENDED_MIN_MASS_KG`, counts what falls below at `:76`, and reports it as a skip reason (`types.ts:104`, `'below-mass-threshold'`); `import/ubox/index.ts:28` computes the live "N bodies included" count WITHOUT a full conversion, which is exactly the responsiveness a slider needs; and `ubox.spec.ts:188` pins the default against real bodies — "keeps Pluto, drops the 4e20 TinyRock". **So the threshold constant is already calibrated and already means something**, and the report filter should share it rather than inventing a second number that disagrees. If the two genuinely need different defaults, say why. | captured, feature — banked |
| G7 | **Let the gas-giant gallery hand you the recipe: click to copy the DATA that produced that colour.** Requested 2026-08-02 — "atmo inclusions ranges and temp-range, I imagine, just so users can copy a colourful gas giant if they want". A genuinely good fit for this engine's philosophy: the gallery already demonstrates that colour comes from data, and this turns the demonstration into a recipe book an author can lift from. **CRITICAL DISTINCTION — two different giant rows exist and only ONE has a recipe to copy.** (1) **`buildGiantLab(pack)` (`catalogue/galleryExamples.ts:~318-345`) is genuinely physics-driven and IS the copy target.** Each lab body is built from real inputs — `temperatureK`, `equilibriumTempK`, `atmosphere.pressure_bar` and a trace composition (the Jovian row holds `{ CH4: 0.003, NH3: 0.00026, H2S: 0.00008 }` fixed and simply cools it) — and the colour is then DERIVED through `deriveCloudDecks` -> `applyCloudDeckTags` + `deriveWeather` -> `deriveApparentColorParts`. Everything a user would need to reproduce that world already exists as an object before the colour is computed. (2) **The `giants` row (`:94-104`) is HAND-AUTHORED and must NOT get a copy button** — its palettes are literal hex triples (`ammonia('#e8d3ab', '#c89868', '#9c6b3e')`), so there is no recipe behind them and a copy would hand the user a colour dressed up as a derivation. If a copy affordance appears on that row at all it should say plainly that the entry is an authored look, or the row should be excluded. **What to copy:** the composition with its fractions, the pressure, the temperature pair, and enough structure to paste straight into a body's `atmosphere` block — the test is whether pasting it into a real world reproduces the giant, so verify by doing exactly that rather than by eye. Include the DERIVED deck list too if it is cheap; seeing "this is the ammonium-hydrosulphide deck" alongside the inputs is what teaches the model. **Constants and thresholds stay in rule-pack data**; this feature only reads. Related: [[D1]], [[C1]]. | captured, feature |
| G11 | **DESIGN INVESTIGATION ONLY: spec the Traveller importer properly.** Requested 2026-08-03 after [[D6]] — the importer will be improved at some point, and now is a good moment to specify it rather than patch it. **DELIVERABLE IS A DESIGN DOCUMENT** (`docs/dev/traveller-import-design.md`) plus an updated G11 entry. Do not build the feature. **START FROM THE EVIDENCE, WHICH IS ALREADY IN HAND.** [[D6]] found that a Traveller-imported Earth freezes at -16 C with a 70% ice surface, because `Traveller-6 (Standard - Earth-like)` carries no water vapour while the pack's own `Nitrogen-Oxygen (Earth-like)` does — one concept defined twice, drifted. That is a symptom; the question this doc answers is how many more of the same kind there are and what the importer SHOULD do. **QUESTIONS TO ANSWER.** (1) **AUDIT the Traveller atmosphere table against the pack's native equivalents.** Where a Traveller entry names the same physical thing as a native one, do their compositions agree? Every disagreement is a D6 waiting to happen. Deliver the table, including the entries that match. (2) **Are declared constraints ENFORCED?** Traveller-6 states `temp_range_K: [280, 310]` and was applied to a 257 K world. Does the selection honour the ranges the data declares, and if not, are the ranges meaningful or decoration? A constraint the data states and the code ignores is worth more than any single bug. (3) **WHAT DOES TRAVELLER ACTUALLY GIVE US, and what must be inferred?** A UWP is a handful of digits — size, atmosphere, hydrographics, population, government, law, tech. Everything else this engine needs (mass, radius, orbital distance, temperature, composition) is inferred. Map that boundary explicitly: which fields come from the import, which are inferred, and — following [[B10]]'s precedent — WHICH CARRY AN "INFERRED" TAG so a GM can tell an imported fact from a guessed one. That distinction is a promise to the reader and it should be designed in, not retrofitted. (4) **HOW SHOULD HYDROGRAPHICS DRIVE THE ATMOSPHERE?** Traveller states a world's water coverage directly. D6's underlying gap is that surface water does not reach the greenhouse; an importer that knows hydrographics is exactly where that could be honoured, or where it should defer to a derived-vapour model. Say which, and note the physics half is its own item. (5) **WHAT SHOULD HAPPEN WHEN TRAVELLER AND THE PHYSICS DISAGREE?** A UWP may assert a breathable atmosphere on a world this engine would freeze. Options are to trust the UWP and let the physics contradict it, to let the physics win and flag the divergence, or to nudge the inferred inputs (orbital distance, albedo) until the stated outcome is reachable. This is the central design question and the doc should recommend one with reasons. **SCOPE WIDENED 2026-08-03 — THE IMPORTER IS PRODUCING PHYSICALLY INVALID BODIES, AND THAT IS NOW THE HEADLINE.** Reported symptoms: **about half of imported gas giants come out TOROIDAL**, and planets land **implausibly close to their star**. **(6) THE TOROID CAUSE IS FOUND AND IT IS A KNOWN FAULT CLASS IN AN UNCOVERED PATH.** `src/lib/traveller/importer.ts` NEVER SETS `rotation_period_hours` — a grep across it finds no assignment. `physics/rotation.ts` computes `spinFraction(rotationHours, densityGcc)`, so an absent or zero period reads as INFINITELY FAST ROTATION, which lands past `NEAR_BREAKUP_AT` (0.8) or `BREAKUP_AT` (1.0) and yields `near-breakup` / `unstable` — the toroidal-tending shapes. This is exactly [[B9]]a's finding, **"a placeholder zero is a CLAIM, not an absence"**, in a route that fix never reached: [[B10]] pushed spin provenance through "all three body-creation routes" and the importer appears to be a FOURTH nobody counted. Confirm that, and if so `docs/dev/generation-duplication-map.md` is understating the problem and should be corrected. **(7) THE DEEPER PATTERN, AND IT IS THE REASON THIS SURFACED NOW.** The importer's inferences were tolerable when the engine derived little. The engine now derives rotational deformation, thermal state, classification, radiation and geology, so a guessed or missing input no longer sits quietly — it becomes a visible absurdity. **Every field the importer leaves absent or guesses is a candidate for the same failure**, so enumerate them all rather than fixing the two that were noticed: what does the importer set, what does it leave to a default, and what does that default MEAN to a physics model that now reads it? The orbital distances are the second known case; find the rest before they are reported one at a time. **(8) COMPARE THE GENERATION SYSTEMS AND RECOMMEND A SIMPLIFICATION.** There are already two live system generators and three body-creation routes (`docs/dev/generation-duplication-map.md`), and the importer is another. Set them side by side: what each one sets, what each infers, where they disagree, and what a single shared "make a plausible body" path would have to provide to replace the lot. **The tidy-up is scoped for V2.3+ and is NOT this session's job to build** — but this audit is the right place to say what it should look like, because nobody has yet had all four paths in view at once. **METHOD:** verify claims against the code and the rule-pack data, say plainly where you are reasoning rather than measuring, give a RECOMMENDATION rather than a survey, phase it, and include a "not worth doing" section. Flag anything needing an owner decision instead of deciding it. **DO NOT** implement, change the rule pack, or touch `src/lib/traveller/**` beyond reading it — but DO say which of the faults you find are one-line data fixes worth doing immediately and which need the redesign. Related: [[D6]], [[B9]], [[B10]], [[D2]]. | captured, design only |
| G10 | **The system map should offer the same grid options and falloff sliders as the starmaps — minus hexes, with a 1 AU fixed grid and polar.** Requested 2026-08-03. **MOST OF THIS ALREADY SHIPPED IN [[G4]] — check before building.** `lib/map/mapOverlay.ts` is now THE overlay vocabulary shared by all four spatial views (2D orrery, 3D holo system, 2D starmap, 3D starmap), `lib/map/latticeGeometry.ts` is the one generator behind them, `MAP_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` is described as "the one dropdown list — every view's overlay picker renders THIS, so they can never drift apart", polar is already in the set (`plain` = rings and spokes, `scaled` = rings labelled with real distances), and the falloff dial was reported as reaching every view that draws a grid. **THREE THINGS ARE GENUINELY LEFT, and the middle one is the valuable one.** **(1) "EXCEPT THE HEXES" IS CURRENTLY SILENT RATHER THAN HONEST.** `forSystemScale` folds hex to square before the system view can draw one, so hexes already do not appear — but because every view renders the SAME dropdown, the system map still OFFERS "Hex", "Subsector hex" and "Traveller hex", and a user picking one silently gets squares. Filter the options per view so a view only offers what it can draw. Do this WITHOUT splitting the list into two — the whole point of `MAP_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` is that it cannot drift, so filter it, do not copy it. **(2) THE SYSTEM GRID IS NOT A DISTANCE AT ALL — MAKE IT 1 AU.** `holo/scene.ts:1005` sets the cell to `GRID_RADIUS / 7`, a fraction of the scene radius, so it scales with the view and means nothing physical. A 1 AU cell turns a decorative plate into a SCALE REFERENCE a GM can read at a glance, which is much the more useful thing. **Handle the extremes deliberately rather than discovering them:** a 40 AU system gives about 40 cells across, which is fine; a compact system half an AU wide would show no lines at all, and a map out to the Kuiper belt would show a hundred. Either step by decade (0.1 / 1 / 10 AU, picked from the framed extent) or clamp the count and SAY which — a fixed 1 AU that vanishes on a tight system is worse than a labelled decade. Note `scaled` already labels polar rings with real distances, so there is a precedent for a physically meaningful overlay to follow. **(3) CONFIRM THE SLIDERS ARE EXPOSED** on the system map's own settings, not merely reaching it in code — [[A32]] and [[A40]] were both cases of a control whose range never arrived at the view. **REFINED 2026-08-03, AND THE TWO REFINEMENTS ARE ONE FUNCTION — build it once.** **(a) THE GRID STEPS BY DECADE WITH A CROSSFADE.** The 1 AU cell should BRIGHTEN and subdivide into ten as you zoom in, and do the reverse on the way out so you see 10 AU and then 100 AU squares. That is the honest answer to the extremes problem raised above — a fixed 1 AU vanishes on a compact system and draws a hundred lines on a Kuiper-belt view — and the crossfade is what makes it read as one continuous grid rather than a jump: as the finer level approaches usefulness it fades UP while the coarser fades DOWN, so at any zoom you see one dominant level and a ghost of the next. Standard map and CAD behaviour, and worth doing properly because a grid that jumps is more distracting than one that does not adapt at all. **(b) THE SCALED POLAR RINGS SHOW UNTIDY DISTANCES** on BOTH the starmap and the system map — they should land on round numbers ending in 0 or 5 rather than whatever the current ring spacing produces. Almost certainly the same cause as the grid: the ring radius derives from a fraction of the scene extent (the system grid uses `GRID_RADIUS / 7`) rather than from a chosen interval, so the LABEL is honest about a distance nobody would choose. **(c) THE SHARED PIECE: one "nice interval" utility serves all of it.** Pick the step from the 1 / 2 / 5 x 10^n sequence so that a framed extent yields a sensible number of divisions — the classic nice-number algorithm — and let the square grid, the polar rings and their labels all call it. It must be UNIT-AGNOSTIC, taking a raw extent and returning a step, because the starmap works in light years or parsecs and the system map in AU; the same logic serves both and a second copy per unit is exactly the duplication the standing rule warns about. Put it beside `lib/map/latticeGeometry.ts` so all four spatial views can reach it. **This also makes the labels trustworthy rather than merely present** — a ring reading "5 AU" is a scale a GM can use; one reading "3.7 AU" is noise wearing a number. **BUILT v2.1.386-beta. `src/lib/map/niceInterval.ts` is the shared piece; (1) was ALREADY DONE and the entry was wrong about it; (2) and (3) are built; and the polar-label hunt turned up something much worse than untidiness.** **(1) HEXES ARE ALREADY FILTERED, and this was checked rather than taken on trust.** `SYSTEM_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` exists in `mapOverlay.ts` — `MAP_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` filtered by `isHexFamily`, not copied — and BOTH system-scale pickers render it: `SystemView.svelte:2238` (the GM's overlay popover) and `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte:507` (the preset editor's system stage). Only the starmap picker at `:404` gets the full list. It landed on 2026-07-29 in "Guard the preset editor; hexes are starmap-scale", five days BEFORE this item was captured, so the observation had simply gone stale. Nothing to do, and nothing was done. **(2) THE GROUND GRID IS A REAL DISTANCE NOW, WITH THE DECADE CROSSFADE.** `GRID_RADIUS / 7` is gone. Lines sit at whole multiples of an AU step chosen from the extent actually on screen, and **verified against the live scene they land at exactly 1, 2, 3, 4, 5 and 6 AU** (total error 0.0012 over six lines). **Two levels are drawn at once and crossfade** — measured opacities 0.228 and 0.137 at the default framing — with the geometry rebuilt only when the DECADE changes and the fade run per frame, which is the split that makes it affordable. **THE LEVELS ARE DECADES, NOT THE 1/2/5 LADDER, and that is not a detail.** A crossfading grid needs its levels NESTED, or the subdivision appears alongside the cells instead of inside them and the two grids beat against each other. Consecutive rungs of the 1/2/5 ladder are not integer multiples — 5 to 2 is a factor of 2.5 — so the ladder cannot promise that and powers of ten can, exactly. So the two sequences do different jobs and both are right: decades for a grid that subdivides, 1/2/5 for rings and labels, where nothing nests. This was got wrong first and caught by a test asserting the levels are nested. **WHY IT DOES NOT USE `latticeFor`, stated so it does not read as a missed chance to share.** The shared generator tiles MAP space with a CONSTANT cell, which is exactly right for both starmaps and the GM's snap grid. It cannot express this: the orrery's radial map is nonlinear at any toytown compression (0.65 by default), so equal steps in AU are not equal steps on screen. These are two different objects, not two copies of one. **What is exact and what is not, said rather than discovered later:** every line is at the distance it claims ALONG ITS OWN AXIS, and at true scale the whole thing is a genuine uniform square grid; under compression the cells narrow outward, matching the orrery beneath. A mathematically exact image of a square grid under a radial map would be CURVED, and straight lines at compressed positions are the readable approximation of it — which is the honest reason to reach for polar when precision matters, since rings stay exactly right. **`expandRadius` is new in `floatingOrigin.ts`**, the inverse of `compressRadius`, because picking a step needs the AU extent on screen. It BISECTS THE FORWARD MAP rather than inverting the algebra, so it cannot drift from it; round-trip tested at every compression. **A 40x geometry saving fell out of it:** the runs are only segmented when the falloff dial is on, because with the dial at 0 — this view's default — `addGridLines` writes no colour attribute and the pieces bought nothing. 65,440 vertices down to 1,604 on the fine level. **(3) THE POLAR LABELS WERE NOT UNTIDY, THEY WERE WRONG — BY A FACTOR OF 43.** This is the real find and it is worse than the entry supposed. On the 3D starmap the scaled rings labelled `extent` directly, where `extent` is in MAP units and the suffix is the campaign's distance unit, with nothing between them but a comment reading "map units at this ring ~ distance". On the bundled Local Neighbourhood the rings read **182 / 364 / 546 / 727 / 909 / 1091 ly** across a neighbourhood whose half-extent is **25.2 ly**. `pixelsPerUnit` never reached the scene at all — it takes `distanceUnit` as a label suffix and nothing else. Fixed by giving the controller `setDistanceScale(pixelsPerUnit)`, wired from `Starmap3DView`; the rings now read **4 / 8 / 12 / 16 / 20 / 24 ly**, round AND true. The system view's own scaled rings were never wrong, only untidy — they had a private list holding 3, 30 and 300, which are not on the 1/2/5 ladder — and now come off the shared one at 10 / 20 / 30 / 40 AU. **(c) THE SHARED PIECE, and it turned up an ELEVENTH DUPLICATION on the way in.** `map/niceInterval.ts` is unit-agnostic by construction — raw extent in, raw step out, no unit and no formatting — because the starmap works in ly or pc and the system view in AU. It holds the 1/2/5 ladder, the ring series, the decade levels and one label formatter. **`SystemVisualizer.svelte` — the GM's 2D orrery — already had the same algorithm inlined at `:441-445`, with its own label formatter at `:491`.** It had the right idea first and kept it private, so the moment anything else needed a round number it was going to become a second copy. Both now call the shared module; the arithmetic is identical, only the home moved. All four spatial views reach it. **THE SLIDERS ARE EXPOSED, checked against what each renderer can actually DO rather than against a wish-list.** The system stage offers Overlay (filtered, no hexes) and Grid falloff, which are exactly the two grid controls `HoloController` has — `setGrid` and `setGridFalloff`, and there is no third.
