| D3 | **TWO NODES SHARE ONE ID in two bundled systems, so one of each pair is unreachable.** Found by the A6 sweep (v2.1.324) while checking the drill-in lists for duplicates — the duplicate was in the DATA, not the builder. `Struve 2398`: the planet "Struve 2398 A b" and the star "Struve 2398 B (GJ 725 B)" are BOTH `struve2398-b`. `Groombridge 34`: the planet "Groombridge 34 A b" and the star "Groombridge 34 B (GQ Andromedae)" are BOTH `groombridge34-b`. Same mechanism both times — the slug for "<system> A b" drops the component letter and collides with "<system> B". Swept all 42 systems; these two are the only collisions. **Consequences, and they are not cosmetic.** (a) `nodeById` is a `.find()`, so the FIRST match wins and the second node cannot be selected by id at all — in both systems the planet is ordered first, so tapping the companion STAR opens the planet. (b) `membersOf` tests `ids.includes(n.id)` against the barycentre's `memberIds`, so the PLANET matches the star's id and is listed as a barycentre member: the Struve 2398 barycentre page shows "Moons: ★ Struve 2398 B | ● Struve 2398 A b" — two rows, one id. (c) Anything else keyed by node id — selection, focus, the player broadcast, routes, constructs — is ambiguous in those two systems. **Where the fix belongs: the starmap build kit (`scripts/starmap-build/`), not the app.** Note the constraint before touching it — stable ids are deliberate and are referenced from elsewhere, so renaming one is a data migration, not an edit; the planet is the safer one to re-slug (`struve2398-ab` / `struve2398-a-b`) since the star's id follows the system's own naming. Related: [[A6]]. **FIXED v2.1.326-beta.** **Mechanism, one line in the generator:** `build-starmaps.mjs:174` forms a planet id as `` `${planetIdPrefix ?? slug}-${letter}` ``, and Struve 2398 A's prefix is the bare system slug — so its planet "b" became `struve2398-b`, which the roster authors two lines below as the companion star's `starId`. The planet's NAME already carries the component letter ("Struve 2398 A b"); its id did not. **Which one moved, decided on evidence rather than the guess above:** counted every occurrence of each id in both maps — 3 each, being the two nodes' own ids plus ONE external reference, the barycentre's `memberIds`, and that reference means the STAR. The planet's id is referenced by nothing. So the planet moved to `struve2398-a-b` / `groombridge34-a-b` and the star kept the id everything points at. **`groombridge34-c` was deliberately left alone** — it is a working stable id on the second planet, and renaming it for symmetry would break the very rule this fix exists to protect ("Never renumber", `scripts/starmap-build/README.md`). The two planets of that star now have differently-shaped ids; that is a cosmetic wart preferred to a real broken reference. **Fixed in the build kit AND in the shipped JSON, because the kit can no longer be run** — see [[D4]]: regenerating would revert twelve hand-applied changes including C3's `frame: 'ecliptic'`. The kit uses `planetOverrides: { '<archive name>': { id } }`, the same mechanism `epseri-aegir` already uses, so the two now agree on these ids; the shipped maps were patched by anchored text surgery (2 lines each, verified as exactly 2) rather than re-serialised, since a JSON round-trip is not byte-identical and would have buried the fix in a 20,000-line reformat. **A duplicate-id assertion now runs on every build and THROWS** — naming the system and both colliding nodes — rather than de-duplicating, because a generator that quietly renames a clash hides the next one exactly as well as silence did. Proven both ways: it passes with the fix, and removing one override reproduces the original failure verbatim. **Verified as an A/B, which is what makes it more than a rename:** on the pre-fix file, asking for the id of "Struve 2398 B (GJ 725 B)" returns "Struve 2398 A b" — both systems, both maps; on the fixed file every node in both systems resolves to itself, and the barycentre page now reads "Companion star: Struve 2398 B" instead of listing the planet as a member. Swept both maps again afterwards: 414 nodes, zero collisions. **What this does NOT fix, and it matters:** an EXISTING campaign built from the old map still carries the old ids — the bundled files are a template, not a live link — so a GM already playing Struve 2398 keeps the collision until they rebase. Related: [[A6]], [[D4]]. | fixed v2.1.326-beta |
| D4d | **DECIDED 2026-08-01: fold the twelve fixes IN and keep the kit authoritative.** The kit stays the source of truth its README claims to be. Each hand-applied fix moves into the kit's generator input, roster or rules — whichever is the honest home for that particular correction — so that a rebuild reproduces the shipped maps EXACTLY. Then pin it: a test that regenerates and asserts equality against the shipped JSON, so the next drift fails loudly at the moment it is introduced rather than being discovered a month later by someone who stopped to check. The alternatives were rejected deliberately: demoting the kit to a one-time bootstrap leaves it to rot and makes every future roster change manual, and a diff-only gate makes drift visible without making regeneration usable. **Sequencing note: do this BEFORE any further starmap data work**, including the C3 moon audit and anything arising from B9 — every one of those wants to change generator input, and doing so while the kit cannot reproduce its own output is how the twelve become twenty. Each fix should be folded in as its own commit, naming which fix it is, so a wrong home for one can be rewound alone. **DONE v2.1.360-beta. The kit reproduces both maps and the manifest byte for byte, and a test now says so on every run.** **The formatting went first and alone, which is what made the rest readable.** The SciFi map shipped at 2-space because v2.1.277 re-serialised the whole file while adding Astrophage; the generator writes `JSON.stringify(obj, null, 1)`. Parsed, re-serialised at 1-space, asserted the parsed object unchanged, committed with nothing else in it — 376,051 bytes to 300,491, so the map also ships 75 KB smaller. Every content commit after it has a diff you can read; the last one is nine lines per map where it would otherwise have been twenty thousand. **Where each fix went, one commit each, named for the fix.** (1) **Luna's `frame: 'ecliptic'` → `static/examples/Sol_2030-System.json`, NOT the kit** — the generator does not author Sol, it copies that file wholesale and strips one fictional ship, so a generator patch would have fixed the two maps and left the standalone Sol example still drawing the Moon 23.4 deg out of plane. The flag is a fact about Luna's data, not about map building. Re-audited that file's nineteen moons against [[C3]] while there: Luna is the only one that qualifies, exactly as C3 concluded. (2) **Phoebe's `frame` → `systems-fiction.mjs`**, because the overlay adds Phoebe and the canonical Sol does not have it; `manualPlanetNode` gained a guarded `frame` spread written as the FIRST key inside `orbit` so the serialisation matches, and absent the field the other ~200 generated orbits are byte-identical to before. (3) **Rocinante `gmNotes`** — the personal name, the only one of the set that is a policy breach rather than a correction; swept both data files, it was the only occurrence. (4) **Astrophage drives and tanks on the Hail Mary and Blip-A** — one fix, two ships, so one commit; verified `engine-astrophage-spin` and `fuel-astrophage` actually exist in `static/rulepacks/starter-sf/` before copying the ids across, since a construct pointing at a missing engine is the exact fault v2.1.277 was fixing elsewhere. (5) **Taumoeba 0.0005 in Adrian's air** and (8) **Astrophage 0.002 over it** kept as SEPARATE commits at their original figures (CO2 0.9095/N2 0.08, then 0.9077/0.0798) rather than merged into one line, so which release did which stays visible; both sum to exactly 1. (6) **Adrian's `radiusKm: 9219` pinned on the spec, and `estimateRadiusRe` deliberately left alone** — it implements Chen & Kipping faithfully and its 2.04 Me break puts a 3.93 Me body on the Neptunian branch, right for the population and wrong for a body an author has decided is rocky; changing the relation would move every planet that uses it. (7) **"Amaze, Amaze!"**. (9) **Blip-A back at the Petrova line** — `parentId` and `orbitSpec.hostId` moved together in one commit, because a construct whose parent and orbital host disagree is a worse state than either being wrong. **Each of the ten was traced to the commit that made it before it was copied, which is what stopped two of them going into the wrong layer** — fix 1 into the generator instead of the canonical Sol, and fix 6 into `estimateRadiusRe` instead of onto Adrian; both would have regenerated correctly and meant something different. **An ELEVENTH delta that D4 could not have listed, because D3 created it after D4 was written.** D3 (v2.1.326) re-slugged two colliding planets by anchored text surgery on the JSON and changed nothing else — but the kit derives each body's phase angles by hashing its id, so both planets have since carried elements computed from an id that no longer exists. Resolved in the GENERATOR's favour rather than pinned: the elements are a function of the id and the id legitimately changed, and preserving the old values would mean teaching the kit to remember a name it no longer uses. Nothing physical moves — a and e still come from the archive; i, omega, Omega and M0 are deterministic noise whose only job is to stop every planet starting at the same longitude. Eight numbers, and existing campaigns are unaffected either way since the bundled maps are a template copied at import. **What the test asserts** (`scripts/starmap-build/buildKit.spec.mjs`, and `vite.config.ts` gained `scripts/` to its include glob so it can live next to the generator it guards): the builder gained `--out <dir>`, the test rebuilds into a temp directory and compares all three files with what ships BYTE FOR BYTE. Two normalisations, both narrow and both stated in the file: line endings, because `core.autocrlf` rewrites the working copy on checkout while the generator always writes LF; and `appVersion`, because it stamps which build produced the map and every patch release bumps it — comparing it would mean regenerating three files on every release to keep a test green, which is how a test gets deleted. **Indentation is NOT normalised**: it is what hid the drift, so it is now part of what is asserted. On failure the test does not print a 300 KB string mismatch — it names the values that moved, keyed by node id rather than array index so a re-ordering reads as a move instead of four hundred changes, and says which side is likely wrong. **Proven in both directions, which is the only way to trust a guard:** reverted Phoebe's frame flag and the Hail Mary's sign-off in the kit and it failed naming exactly those two, one as "dropped by the build"; re-indented the shipped SciFi map to 2-space with no content change and it failed with the separate "same values, different bytes" message; restored both, green; then bumped the version and confirmed it stays green, which is the appVersion normalisation doing its job. **Full suite: 914 passed, the same 4 pre-existing `src/routes/page.spec.ts` failures as on a clean tree (a `starmapStorage` mock missing `loadPreUpgradeStarmap`, nothing to do with this).** **One erratum in the history:** the fix 4 commit message says the Astrophage tanks are "a two-thousandth" of the antimatter stand-in's; they are not — Hail Mary 4000/900 to 2000/450, Blip-A 9000/3000 to 3000/1100. The numbers in the data are right. **NOT amended, and that is worth knowing about this repo:** `git commit --amend` was run while the concurrent session had committed on top, and it rewrote THEIR commit's message onto their tree. Recovered intact with `git reset --soft` to their sha (identical tree and parent, so nothing was lost), but the rule now is: **while a second session is committing, never amend and never rewrite history — make a new commit.** **Two things left deliberately unfolded, both appended unnumbered below:** the standalone `Sol_Expanse-System.json` has the same missing Luna flag ([[C3]] audited the bundled starmaps, not the standalone examples), and the real map's RV super-Earths carry the archive's own inflated radii — the population-wide version of the fault that fix 6 pinned on Adrian. Neither is D4d's job and both would have widened a mechanical task into a physics one. Unblocks [[C5]], [[B10]], [[B11]]. | fixed v2.1.360-beta |
| D4 | **THE STARMAP BUILD KIT NO LONGER REPRODUCES THE SHIPPED MAPS — running it reverts twelve hand-applied fixes. Read this before touching `scripts/starmap-build/`.** Found by D3 (v2.1.326), which had to establish whether regenerating was safe before changing generator input; it is not. `README.md` presents the kit as the source of truth ("regenerate the three output files"), and it is offline and deterministic, so the natural move is to edit the roster and rebuild. **Do not.** Diffed shipped against regenerated node by node, ignoring formatting: same 42 systems and same node counts in both maps, no nodes added or lost, but **12 nodes differ in content** and every one of them is a fix applied to the JSON and never back-ported to the kit. The real map: `solar-system-luna :: orbit` — that is **C3's `frame: 'ecliptic'` (v2.1.316)**, and the string "ecliptic" appears nowhere in `scripts/starmap-build/`. The SciFi map: Luna and `sol-phoebe` again (C3's other body), plus `tauceti-adrian :: radiusKm` and `:: atmosphere`, `tauceti-hail-mary :: engines` and `:: fuel_tanks`, a second construct's engines/fuel_tanks, a `gmNotes`, a `description`, and a **`parentId`** — a re-parenting that a rebuild would silently undo. There is also a whole-file formatting difference: the SciFi map ships at 2-space indent and the generator writes 1-space, so a rebuild produces a 20,000-line diff that would bury any real change inside it. **Why this is worth its own entry rather than a note on D3:** the kit is not merely stale, it is stale in a way that LOOKS safe — deterministic, committed caches, a README inviting you to re-run it — and the damage would be a silent revert of shipped physics fixes rather than a build failure. D3 was fixed on both sides for exactly this reason. **Two ways out, and it is a real decision:** (a) back-port the twelve deltas into `systems-real.mjs` / `systems-fiction.mjs` so the kit is authoritative again, then regenerate once and accept the reformat as a single mechanical commit; or (b) accept that the shipped JSON is now the source of truth, and demote the kit in its README to "how these maps were first built" with a loud warning. (a) is the better end state and is a genuine piece of work — the twelve deltas have to be understood, not just copied, because some (C3's frame flags) encode physics decisions. **Cheap guard worth adding either way:** a test that regenerates to a temp dir and asserts content-equality with the shipped files, so the two can never drift silently again. Related: [[D3]], [[C3]]. **RESOLVED v2.1.360-beta — option (a), per [[D4d]]; read that row for what went where.** The kit is authoritative again and a test rebuilds and compares on every run, so "do not run the build" no longer applies: run it, and let the test tell you if the two disagree. **Two corrections to this entry, both only visible once the work was done.** The twelve are twelve node::field deltas, not twelve independent fixes — they fold into ten, because the two Luna rows are one flag applied to one source file, and the Hail Mary's engines and fuel tanks move as one change. And the count was ELEVEN by the time anyone acted, because [[D3]]'s text surgery had since added one of its own. **Everything else in this entry held up under the work, including the warning that reads most like alarmism.** All ten deltas are corrections applied to the JSON and never back-ported, exactly as described; the `parentId` really is the dangerous one, because it is the only delta where a rebuild MOVES a body rather than editing a scalar or a string — Blip-A was authored orbiting Adrian, corrected in the JSON to a 0.3 AU orbit around Tau Ceti itself, and a rebuild would have put the ship back at the planet it never visited without changing a single node count. | resolved v2.1.360-beta |

## E — Known-not-ours

| D5 | **The user-facing physics pages, the Newton explainer and the tag docs have drifted badly behind the engine.** Raised 2026-08-02: "we need to ensure we are always updating the Newton apple and physics pages, and documentation is kept up to date". **MEASURED, and the gap is large.** `src/routes/physics/+page.svelte` was last touched at **v2.1.282** (2026-07-30, the albedo/cloud unification) — seventy-eight versions ago. `src/lib/physics/physicsTrace.ts`, which is what the Newton explainer reads, dates from 2026-07-28. `docs/tags-guide.md` and `docs/classification-and-tags.md` have not moved since **2026-07-07**, roughly a hundred and twenty versions. **None of the three mentions `hazard/radiation` or `flight/ascent`** — a grep returns zero across all of them. **What is undocumented, as a starting list rather than the whole of it:** the trapped-particle belt model and its calibration ([[B17]]); a body being inside its own belt, the inner edge derived from scale height, and the two named figures a surfaceless body reports ([[B22]]); `process()` idempotence and the pass reordering ([[B13]]); the radiation hazard tag, the weathering tag keeping its own meaning, and the unified bucketing ([[B28]]); time-to-harm vocabulary and the ascent and orbital-dose tags ([[B30]], [[B31]]); fourteen classification fingerprints moving from equilibrium to surface temperature ([[B6]]); a gas giant no longer scoring on a surface axis ([[B18]]); the ejection asymmetry ([[B19]]); and the ice-giant formation-heat split from B13's sweep. **THE GAS GIANTS ARE THE PART MOST LIKELY TO BE DOCUMENTED WRONGLY, so give them their own passage.** The radiation story for a giant is genuinely unlike a rocky world's and each step of it is counter-intuitive: a giant is now inside ITS OWN belt where before its belt only reached its moons; it reports TWO figures rather than one, because it has no surface and the honest answers at the 1-bar level and at the cloud tops differ by orders of magnitude; the belt has an INNER EDGE derived from scale height, without which Earth would read as lethal; and the dose falls off exponentially in HOST RADII rather than by inverse square, which is why Io and Callisto differ by five orders of magnitude across a 4.4x distance. A reader who assumes any of those behaves like the stellar model will draw the wrong conclusion, and the pages currently say nothing at all. Cover the brown-dwarf boundary too — self-luminous bodies from 13 M_jup DO irradiate their moons and Jupiter correctly does not, which is a distinction a GM will otherwise get backwards. **The Newton explainer matters most of the three**, because it is the surface that claims to SHOW THE WORKING — a trace that omits the dominant term for a Galilean moon is not merely incomplete, it explains the number wrongly. Check `physicsTrace.ts` covers every term that now feeds a figure it explains. **This is a catch-up pass AND a standing habit** — the rule added at the foot of this file is the habit; this item is the backlog. Do not treat it as done until the physics page, the Newton trace and both tag docs each reflect the engine as it stands, and say plainly which parts you verified against the code rather than against the changelog. **DONE v2.1.361-362-beta. THE NEWTON TRACE WAS WORSE THAN THE ITEM SUSPECTED: it did not omit the dominant term, it omitted the ENTIRE QUANTITY.** **VERIFIED AGAINST THE CODE** (`grep` over `physicsTrace.ts`): fourteen layers, and **zero occurrences of `surfaceRadiation`, `belt`, `orbitalRadiation` or `Shielding`**. The only radiation-adjacent line was `irradiationDose` at `:327` — which is the space-weathering total, reads LOW on Io, and is a different quantity entirely. So the surface that promises to show the working showed a cumulative weathering ratio where a reader looks for the dose. **New `radiation` layer**, read post-hoc from committed fields like every other layer, so it cannot disagree with what is displayed: total incident flux, host belt (distance in HOST RADII plus the host field and spin that drive it), own belt inner edge, magnetospheric deflection, atmospheric absorption, then the dose at the right level with its hazard word and the time to a lethal dose. Verified on Io, Earth, Jupiter, Jupiter's Rings and Mars. **FOUR FAULTS IN MY OWN FIRST DRAFT, caught before commit, three of them the [[A33]] class this week has been spent fixing:** it labelled `body.stellarRadiation` "incident starlight" when since [[B17]] that field is `components.total` and CARRIES THE BELT (Io reads 26,279 against sunlight of 0.037); it rounded Earth's 99.937% atmospheric blocking to "100%", which reads as total; it printed the photon and particle doses in different units side by side, a millionfold apart; and it called 150 scale heights a depth of air rather than a model distance. Writing an explainer is exactly as easy to get wrong as the thing it explains. **PHYSICS PAGE: a new `#belts` section, "Trapped belts & the giants", with the four counter-intuitive steps the item demanded** — a belt is not a light source and falls off exponentially in host radii (with the r^-4 / r^-8.6 arithmetic that rules out any power law, and the reasoning behind each exponent); the inner edge from scale height, and that without it Earth reads 2.31 Sv/day at ground level; a giant being inside its own belt, which is WHY it needs two figures; and the brown-dwarf boundary, with Jupiter correctly excluded because its excess output is INFRARED and non-ionising — a heat source, not a dose source. The `#radiation` section gained the two-figure shape (surface / 1 bar / ring plane, and the orbital environment) and the time-to-harm vocabulary. **Verified in the browser**: the section renders, the nav entry resolves, and the displayed formula got a real style rule rather than the class I had invented with nothing behind it. **`docs/classification-and-tags.md` was FACTUALLY WRONG, not merely stale**, and this is the part worth keeping. It described the processor as "barycentres -> physical basics -> environment (temp/radiation) -> classification", which stopped being true at v2.1.356: radiation is its own pass 2c and interior/magnetism is 2b. Replaced with the real eight-row pass table, plus the B13 rule (nothing may read a value a later pass writes) and its two corollaries. The namespace table listed nine namespaces and now lists the fifteen that exist, with `hazard/*` correctly attributed to the processor as well as star generation, and a pointer saying `tagPresentation.ts` is the live registry and this table is not the list. **`docs/tags-guide.md` gained "Tags a crew would actually act on"** — the five decision-shaped tags with what each settles, and the [[B28]] pairing spelled out for a reader: a world can be the most violently irradiated surface in a system and still read lightly weathered, because volcanism repaints it faster than anything accumulates. **VERIFIED AGAINST THE CHANGELOG rather than the code, and flagged as such:** the calibration constants quoted in the belt passage (lambda 1.6324 R_J, D0 1451.1 Sv/day, the 0.71x and 1.54x hold-out ratios) and the substellar measurements (2 / 104 / 2,626 mSv/yr at 1 / 13 / 40 M_jup) come from [[B17]]'s entry, not from re-running them. Every figure I quote for a BODY (Io 36 Sv/day, Europa 1.3 days, Ganymede 40 days, Mars 23 years, Earth 2.3 mSv/yr, Jupiter 11 mSv/yr at 1 bar against 764 Sv/day above it) was read out of the derived fixture this session. **ALL THREE REMAINING ITEMS NOW DONE, v2.1.365-beta.** [[B6]] is written into `#classification` — which fourteen types key on SURFACE temperature and which twelve correctly still key on equilibrium, with the earth-analogue band as the giveaway that they were authored as surface figures all along (Earth's Teq of 254.1 sits just OUTSIDE its own type's 255-300 band while its 287 K surface sits mid-band), and why it is visible rather than internal: the stat block prints Type and Surface temp two rows apart. The ice-giant formation-heat split is in `#greenhouse`, including the trap — asking whether the CLASS contains "ice giant" reads a name assigned a pass later from a temperature this very figure produces — and that it had been handing mini-Neptunes and hycean worlds Jupiter's formation heat on a substring miss. [[B19]] has its own `#ejection` section, carrying the two things that were ASSUMED and wrong (the 5.5 threshold never fired, at a measured delta of 9.28; debris was already excluded) alongside the actual cause, plus the practical reading for a GM: a fate tag on a large planet only means something if that planet is the lighter of its pair. All four sections verified rendering in the browser with working nav links. The standing habit at the foot of this file is what should stop them accumulating again. | fixed v2.1.361-362-beta — trace, physics page and both tag docs; three non-radiation items left, named. **SECOND SWEEP v2.1.385-beta — the nine banked lines cleared to zero.** **THE NEWTON TRACE WAS AGAIN THE WORST, and again not in the way the debt list said.** It had no ALBEDO layer at all: B5's whole model sat as one line inside Temperature reading "0.256 — Cloud-free moderate oxide dust over bare ground", which states an answer and hides every step of it. It now has its own layer showing bare ground → deposit → cloud → Bond albedo, and `AlbedoBreakdown` gained `bareAlbedo` and `deposit` so it CAN — the trace is built post-hoc from committed fields by design, so a number it cannot read is a number it cannot show. Mars reads 0.105 / 0.252 / 0.256, which is the entire explanation in three figures. It had no SPIN layer either, and that one mattered more than it looks: the seasonal component of the temperature range is driven by the tilt, so a reader could see a seasonal swing explained by nothing. **AND THE TRACE HELD A THIRD COPY OF THE RADIATION PLACE TEST** — `ring ? … : mk.gas > 0.5 ? …` inline — reading the raw makeup fraction, so an ice giant fell through and reported a "surface" dose: the same bug [[B11]] had just fixed in the other copy, in the file that claims to show the working. **THREE THINGS THE DEBT LINES THEMSELVES GOT WRONG, all caught by reading the code:** (1) the eclipse kinds are total / annular / **partial** — "transit" is not one of them, it is the word for what falls BELOW the quarter-disc floor and is filtered out; (2) the kind comes from comparing the two discs' angular radii against their SEPARATION, not from the ratio, which sets the depth; (3) there are **three** occulter classes, not two — your satellites, your parent if you are a moon, and your barycentre PARTNER (Pluto and Charon eclipsed each other every few days through the late eighties, which is how Charon's radius was measured). **AND ONE ITEM NEEDED NOTHING: [[B24]].** The stability layer prints `orbitalStabilityDetails` whole and that string already carries the "Driven by:" attribution, so the fix reached the trace for free when it shipped — established by reading it, not assumed from the debt line. **A STALE STATEMENT FOUND BY SWEEPING RATHER THAN BY THE LIST:** `tags-guide.md` described the orbital-radiation tag as "the reading for the space a SHIP PARKS IN", which is exactly the reading [[B27]] had corrected. Relabelled with the ISS contrast. **New sections on the physics page:** `#albedo` (B5, which had no anchor of its own so the trace could not link to it), `#spin` (B10 + C3(c), including the inferred-versus-measured promise and the Laplace switch), `#eclipses` (G8, which had nothing anywhere), `#views` (G4/A37/A41). Plus B24 in the ejection section, and the Resurfacing section finally got an entry in the page's own contents list — nav and anchors now match exactly, 31 and 31. **Four new trace tests pin the layers**, since a trace that omits a term explains its number wrongly rather than merely incompletely. | swept to zero v2.1.385-beta |
| D6 | **A Traveller-imported "Earth-like" world freezes: no water vapour in the atmosphere, so no greenhouse, so a snowball.** Reported 2026-08-03 on Terra (Main World) — 0.919 AU, 0.95 bar, "Traveller-6 (Standard - Earth-like)", showing surface temp **-16 C**, `Surface ice 70% water`, and the tags `Structure/icy-shell: water`, `Frozen surface: water`. **THE PHYSICS IS CORRECT AND THE TAGS ARE CORRECT** — water IS solid at -16 C, so the frozen-surface and icy-shell tags are right given the temperature ([[B4]] gated exactly that). The fault is upstream. **VERIFIED, IT IS THE RULE-PACK DATA:** `static/rulepacks/starter-sf/atmospheres.json` gives Traveller-6 a composition of N2 0.78-0.79, O2 0.20-0.21, Ar 0.009-0.01 and CO2 0.0004-0.0006 — **and no H2O at all**. The bundled Earth carries `H2O: 0.00398`. Water vapour is the DOMINANT greenhouse gas on Earth, roughly half to two-thirds of the +33 K, so without it the world gets only the trace CO2 term and settles near its equilibrium temperature — about -8 C at 0.919 AU. It reads -16 C because it is colder still: the resulting ice raises the albedo, which cools it further. A snowball, arrived at honestly. **A SECOND SIGNAL IN THE SAME BLOCK, worth checking either way:** that atmosphere declares `temp_range_K: [280, 310]` — the rule pack says it belongs on a 280-310 K world, and Terra is at 257 K. So either the importer selects an atmosphere without honouring its own declared range, or the range is aspirational and unenforced. Find out which; a constraint the data states and the code ignores is worth more than this one bug. **TWO WAYS TO FIX IT, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME SIZE.** (1) **DATA, cheap:** add H2O to Traveller-6 (and audit the other Traveller atmospheres for the same omission), matching how the bundled Earth is authored. Fixes the symptom and is consistent with constants living in rule-pack data. (2) **PHYSICS, right:** water vapour should be DERIVED from surface temperature and available water rather than authored, because its abundance is set by Clausius-Clapeyron and authoring a fixed fraction ASSUMES THE ANSWER — 0.4% is Earth's equilibrium at 288 K, and stating it on a world that would be cold begs the question. That is the same circularity [[B1]] solved for albedo/temperature/cloud with a bounded fixed point, and the existing `solveThermalState` is where it would belong. **Recommend doing (1) now and raising (2) as its own item** — but note that (1) alone will make every authored Earth-like world warm regardless of where it orbits, which is wrong in the other direction, so whoever does it should say so plainly rather than declare the class fixed. **SHARPENED 2026-08-03, and the real diagnosis is the DUPLICATION RULE, not a tight boundary.** Asked whether the importer is missing a calculation or the physics boundaries are too tight: neither. **The same rule pack defines "Earth-like atmosphere" TWICE and the two disagree.** `Nitrogen-Oxygen (Earth-like)` carries `H2O`; `Traveller-6 (Standard - Earth-like)` does not. One concept, two entries, drifted — which is exactly the test the standing rule sets ("could these two answer the same question differently"), and the importer happens to select the one that is wrong. **UNDERNEATH IT IS A GENUINE MODEL GAP, and this is the part worth fixing properly:** Terra has 70% surface water and ZERO vapour above it, which is impossible at any temperature above absolute zero. The engine KNOWS there is an ocean — it renders the coverage and tags the frozen surface — but the greenhouse never asks, because vapour is only ever AUTHORED and never DERIVED from available water. So this is another instance of the recurring shape: a fact the engine holds does not reach the calculation that needs it. The boundaries are not too tight; the wiring is missing. Related: [[B1]], [[B4]], [[B5]], [[D2]], [[G11]]. | captured |
| E1 | `src/routes/page.spec.ts` — 4 failures (jsdom cannot fetch a relative URL). Verified failing at 255768a via a detached worktree, so NOT from the v2.1.277 work despite being attributed there. Likely origin `371f649`. **RETIRED v2.1.411-beta, superseded by [[E3]] - and its diagnosis was never the cause.** jsdom-plus-Node-fetch really does throw `TypeError: Invalid URL` on a root-relative fetch, and `/temporal/calendars.json` really did print a stack on every mount. But the app catches it, and no test ever failed because of it: the four failures were a stale storage mock and a heading that had been removed. The noise is gone anyway - `src/setup.ts` now answers a root-relative fetch with a plain 404, which is the truth (there is no server under test), is what callers already handle, and opens no socket.
