Note the shape of the fix is already argued in `applyKnobBias`'s own comment — a star and its planets condense from one disc, so they start ALIGNED and misalignment is evidence of a violent past rather than a die-roll to make worlds look varied. Planetary obliquity is a different question again (giant impacts, Uranus), so it wants its own thinking rather than reusing the stellar spread. **SCOPED 2026-08-02, NOT BUILT — stopped deliberately with context left rather than pushing an unverified change to every generated system. Read this before starting; it makes the job smaller than the entry implies.** **THE TWO-GENERATORS WARNING DOES NOT APPLY TO PLANETS, which is the whole of the difficulty removed.** `_generatePlanetaryBody` (`generation/planet.ts:20`) is imported by BOTH paths — `planet-generation.ts:69` and `:183` for the legacy `generateSystem`, and `generateFromConfig.ts:312` for the wizard — and it recurses into itself for moons (`planet.ts:421`). So planetary AND lunar tilt is ONE site, and it is the site that already invents `rotation_period_hours` (`planet.ts:205-210`) and already pushes `origin/…` tags (`:194`, `:214-215`). A tilt set there reaches every generated planet and moon on every path. **The duplication warning applies to STARS only:** `applyKnobBias` (`generateFromConfig.ts:48-58`) is the sole stellar-tilt site and runs only `if (config.knobs && !config.emptyPlanets)`, while the legacy path's star creation in `generation/star.ts` sets none. Follow [[B9a]]'s precedent exactly — it hit this same wall and fixed it by extracting `starFieldFromPack` into `star.ts` and calling it from both. Extract, do not copy the block. **Reuse the physics argument already written in `applyKnobBias`'s comment rather than re-deriving it:** a star and its planets condense from one disc so they start aligned and stay aligned, the Sun is 7 deg off after 4.5 Gyr, and misalignment is evidence of a violent past — which is why stellar tilt hangs off the dynamical-history knob and not a die-roll. **That argument does NOT transfer to planetary obliquity and must not be reused for it**, as this entry already says: Uranus at 97.77 deg and Venus's retrograde spin are per-body accidents (giant impacts), not a system-wide property, so the planetary distribution wants its own shape — most bodies near the disc plane with a long tail, not a uniform spread. **The tag, per D2a's constraint: no "inferred" convention exists yet.** `tags/tagPresentation.ts` title-cases an unregistered key as a fallback, so a bare key renders but with no hover description — it wants a `TAG_INFO` entry. `origin` is an existing namespace (group "Origin", `#8a8a9a`), so a key under it needs no new namespace metadata. **And note the THIRD route, which will make the fix look broken if you verify through the UI:** `SystemView.svelte:478-505` sets planetary tilt by its own die-roll and bypasses `bodyFactory` entirely (`generation-duplication-map.md` §2), so anyone checking this through the manual "add a body" button would conclude it had not worked — the same trap that entry records for B9a. **What remains: the two edits above, a spec asserting non-zero tilt across three seeds through BOTH entry points (the original measurement was 0 of 40, so the test is the same measurement inverted), and a `tests/output/solar-system-derived.json` diff — tilt feeds the seasonal term in `surfaceTempProfile`, so that diff is the proof the change is physics-visible rather than cosmetic.** Related: [[B9a]], [[D2a]], [[C5]]. **BUILT v2.1.363-beta — 0 of 40 became 149 of 149 across eight seeds and both generators.** **The scoping above held: planets and moons were one site and stars were two.** `_generatePlanetaryBody` got the planetary/lunar block beside the rotation period it already invents, and `starTiltFromPack` was extracted into `star.ts` and called from `_generateStar` AND `starSeedToBody` — extracted, not copied, following [[B9a]] exactly. **TWO POPULATIONS, because obliquity has two causes that do not blend and averaging them gives a distribution matching neither.** The DISC population is drawn Rayleigh (what a random walk of the spin vector in a plane gives) — a planet condenses from its star's disc, starts near the normal, and is nudged. The CATASTROPHE population is drawn ISOTROPICALLY, uniform in cos(obliquity) rather than in the angle, because a late giant impact does not nudge an axis, it re-points it — and that is what produces Uranus and Venus at the right rate instead of a smear of 90-degree worlds. **Rolled per body on purpose: an impact is an accident, not a system-wide property, so it deliberately does NOT reuse the star's dynamical-history spread** (which is system-wide and correctly stays that way). **Measured across 149 bodies:** median 18.3 deg, p75 28.0, p90 45.1, 11.4% tipped past the disc against a 10% target. Earth 23.4, Mars 25.2, Saturn 26.7 and Neptune 28.3 all land in the meat; Uranus and Venus are the tail. **The honest weakness: almost nothing is drawn below 3 degrees, where Mercury and Jupiter sit** — Mercury is tidally despun and Jupiter is too massive to tip, and this model has neither mechanism. A despun body should probably have its obliquity collapsed by the processor, which owns locking; not done. **PHYSICS-VISIBLE, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** `obliquity_deg` feeds `surfaceTempProfile`'s seasonal component. Over 75 generated bodies — before: 20 with any seasonal swing, mean 8.3 K, max 13.0, and **that residue is orbital eccentricity, not tilt**, because the component is "tilt + orbit"; after: **64 of 75, mean 24.9 K, max 148.0 K**. Three times as many worlds have a year that means anything. The `climate/monsoon` gate (`cloudDecks.ts:369`, tilt > 12 deg) is unlocked but NOT exercised by these seeds — none produced a world with both rain and a surface ocean, so that half is untested. **`tests/output/solar-system-derived.json` is UNCHANGED, and that is the other half of the claim, not an absence of evidence:** the block is guarded on `axial_tilt_deg == null`, so Sol's authored obliquities are untouched and generator inference does not leak into hand-authored systems. **D2a's constraint met:** every inferred tilt carries `spin/axis-inferred` and the impact cases carry `spin/tipped` — the interesting half as a tag rather than a float the reader must interpret. New `spin` namespace, chosen deliberately to be OUTSIDE `importFixup`'s `DERIVED_TAG_PREFIXES` so the tags survive a save and reload instead of being stripped as processor-owned. **Two traps found in the building that the scoping did not predict.** (1) **The draw must use its own RNG stream keyed on the body id.** Adding a draw to the shared system stream shifts every subsequent draw, so every planet in every saved seed would silently re-roll and a written-down seed would stop reproducing its system. B9a separated its field draw for the same reason; that precedent is the only thing that flagged it. (2) **`applyKnobBias`'s stellar branch had to become UNCONDITIONAL.** It was guarded on `!b.axial_tilt_deg`, which was right while nothing else set one — with a baseline in place the guard would never fire again and the dynamical-history knob would have quietly stopped doing anything, with its own spec still passing because that spec only checks calm-versus-violent through the same dead branch. The knob is the more specific statement, so it overrides the baseline. A regression test now covers exactly that. **The test proves itself in both directions:** it passes on the fix, and reverting the two sites fails it naming the bodies ("HD06715 A (star) has no axial_tilt_deg", "HD06715 A I states a tilt with nothing saying it was inferred"). It also guards its own sample size, since a run that quietly produced two bodies would satisfy every other assertion. **Constants are in pack data** (`starter-sf/generation.json`: `axial_tilt_disc_sigma_deg` 15, `axial_tilt_catastrophe_chance` 0.1, `star_axial_tilt_baseline_deg` 8), not code. **NOT done, deliberately:** the third body-creation route, `SystemView.svelte:478-505`, still die-rolls its own tilt and bypasses `bodyFactory` — left alone because unifying it is the V2.3 generation tidy-up, but note that a body added through the manual button gets a tilt with no `spin/axis-inferred` tag, so the provenance claim is not yet true of every route. **CLOSED PROPERLY v2.1.366-beta — and the manual route was not a missing tag, it was A HOLE IN AN EXISTING TEST.** `axialTilt.spec.ts` already asserted "states a tilt with nothing saying it was inferred"; the invariant EXISTED and simply did not reach `SystemView.svelte`, which builds its body inline and touches neither `BodyFactory` nor `_generatePlanetaryBody`. A rule enforced on two of three routes reads as enforced — that is how [[B9a]] shipped, and leaving it as "recorded, not done" would have repeated it. **WHICH ROLLED VALUES NEED PROVENANCE, decided on evidence rather than symmetry — the test is whether the PROCESSOR RE-DERIVES the value, because one it replaces was never a claim the generator made:** `axial_tilt_deg` — nothing re-derives it, the roll IS what the reader sees → **tagged**. `rotation_period_hours` — re-derived ONLY for a tidally locked body (`SystemProcessor` `lockedSpin`), so an unlocked body keeps the roll → **tagged unless locked, and this was missing from the GENERATOR too**, which is what checking the manual route turned up. `magneticField` — **NOT tagged, and this is the interesting one**: `SystemProcessor:704` always overwrites it from the derived model unless it is `manual`, and `:707` strips every `magnetic/*` tag and re-derives it alongside, so a provenance tag there would be both untrue and deleted on the next pass. The roll still stays, because pass 2 reads the field for atmospheric escape and shielding before pass 3 overwrites it ([[B13]]) — which is why the duplication map lists removing these rolls as unsafe until B13 lands. **So the DECISION moved to `generation/spinProvenance.ts` and all three routes call it**, rather than the manual block growing its own copy — the duplication map's own worked example, and the same move C3(c) made by importing `laplaceRadiusAU` instead of reimplementing it. **TESTING THE ROUTE NO TEST CAN DRIVE:** SystemView's block lives in an event handler and is not callable until the V2.3 tidy-up folds it into `BodyFactory`, and "not testable yet" is precisely the gap that let B9a ship. So the extracted decision is unit-tested, and both sites are checked STRUCTURALLY for calling it — **a weaker guarantee, and the file says so**: it cannot tell you the tag came out right, only that the site has not quietly regrown the rule. Proven by breaking both halves: reverting the manual route to a direct tag push fails with "SystemView.svelte pushes the tag directly instead of using the helper". **One thing this exposed and did NOT fix:** `spinProvenanceTags` had to be typed structurally rather than as `Pick<CelestialBody, …>`, because **`axial_tilt_deg` is not declared on `CelestialBody` at all** — every site reading or writing it does so untyped, the same drift that left `Orbit.frame` untyped until C3(c) needed it. Appended unnumbered below. Related: [[C3]], [[C5]], [[B9a]], [[D2a]], [[B13]]. | fixed v2.1.363-364-beta |
| B11 | **Belts and rings get no derived physics at all, because `processClassification` returns early for anything that is not a planet or a moon.** Found while doing [[B9]]: the early return at `SystemProcessor.ts:539` gates the whole third pass, which is where magnetism, rotational deformation, geology, surface age and the reasons-to-visit hooks live. scale of the problem in both directions.** **"No derived physics at all" is not true:** a belt and a ring already carry equilibrium and surface temperature, albedo, ALL FIVE radiation figures, classes, the ascent Δv budget, orbital period and the temperature profile. **And the reasons-to-visit hooks are NOT gated** — `annotateReasonsToVisit` runs over the SYSTEM, not inside `processClassification`, so the Kuiper Belt already carried `resource/ore-belt`, `frontier/ice-mining` and `intrigue/anomalous-signal`. **What was genuinely missing was ONE thing, and it was the one that mattered: `hazard/radiation`.** Jupiter's Rings carry the loudest dose in the Solar System — 360 Sv/day, above Io — and had no tag to filter or warn on, because the radiation tags sat inside `processClassification`'s GEOLOGY branch: two gates, neither of them about radiation. **THE PILE-SORT.** *Applies as-is, now runs:* `hazard/radiation` and `hazard/orbital-radiation` — six bodies gain a word (Jupiter's Rings **hours**, Saturn's Rings weeks, Uranus' and Neptune's Rings months, The Main Belt chronic, Kuiper Belt background). *Genuinely meaningless, still gated and deliberately so:* no dynamo or fluid layers (`processInterior`), no tectonic regime, **no `surface/irradiation` — `deriveIrradiationDose` takes `surfaceAgeGyr`, which is a cratering-sense age a debris field has not got**, no rotational deformation, no habitability ([[B18]]'s axis is a surface score and a field has no single surface), no fingerprint classification. **NO PARALLEL PATH:** the tags moved into ONE private method called ahead of the gate, and the eligibility test is `radiationPlace` — [[B26]]'s decision reused, not restated. **`radiationPlace` and `hasSolidSurface` MOVED from `catalogue/bodyFacts.ts` to `physics/radiation.ts`**, because the processor and the info block must not answer "is this a real place" differently; bodyFacts re-exports them so no caller changed. **AND MOVING IT EXPOSED TWO REAL BUGS INSIDE IT, both found by removing the gate and watching what slipped through — the same category error [[B18]]/[[B22]]/[[B25]]/[[B33]] keep hitting, living in the helper meant to prevent it.** (1) It read the STORED `makeup.gas`, which is normally absent because makeup is INFERRED from density, so its composition test almost never fired. (2) Its class regex listed `gas-giant` but not `ice-giant` — **so URANUS AND NEPTUNE read as having solid ground, and their dose has been labelled "Radiation (surface)" in the info block for as long as that label has existed.** Both now use `makeupFractions(body).gas <= 0.5`, the SAME boundary B18/B25/B33 use, and the regex is deleted rather than extended (a derived class is never a physics input, and B33 found bundled bodies carrying a giant class at gas 0.00). A STAR is excluded outright: without it Sol picked up a `background` tag derived from an undefined dose. **Derived diff: SIX bodies gain a tag, ZERO lost, ZERO numeric changes; the rest of the 264 lines is tag ORDER on 24 bodies** (the hazard tag is emitted before geology now, same key, same value). Idempotence holds. **STILL OPEN and unchanged: the star half.** `deriveMagnetism` still never runs on a star; that becomes live with [[B9]](b), exactly as this entry warned. | fixed v2.1.382-beta (star half still open) |
| B12 | **The dynamo's slow-rotator cut-off has no anchor between Venus and Earth, and [[B7]] just made it load-bearing for ~40 bundled planets.** `magnetism.ts:122` kills an ordered dynamo below `rot < 0.12`, i.e. a day longer than about 200 h, calibrated on Venus (5832 h, effectively no field) with Earth (24 h, 0.5 G) at the other end. There is no measured body anywhere between, and the whole habitable-zone population of an M dwarf lands in the gap: tidally locked worlds rotating once every 9 to 50 days sit at 0.02 to 0.11, just under the cut. Before [[B7]] they had no rotation at all and took the `unknown -> 0.6` default, so the threshold never fired on them; now 41 of them read `magnetic/unshielded` and 12 `magnetic/tenuous`. **Deliberately NOT retuned** — there is no measurement to retune it against, and re-shaping a curve to satisfy an intuition is what [[B2]] warns about. But the real literature question is that dynamo strength scales with the ROSSBY number (rotation against the convective overturn time), not the period alone, so a slowly-rotating world with a vigorously convecting core can still sustain a field. If an anchor or a Rossby formulation is wanted, this is the item. Consequence to weigh first: magnetic shielding feeds atmospheric retention and the particle dose, so moving this moves habitability across a whole class of world. **NO CHANGE TO THE CONSTANT, and the anchor hunt is why. This is a B2-style wont-fix, not an unfinished item.** **Every Solar System body with a measured field and a known spin, against the gate (`rot < 0.12`, i.e. a day longer than ~200 h):** | body | period h | rotFactor | clamped | vs gate | measured | ours | |---|---|---|---|---|---|---| | Venus | 5832 | 0.0041 | **0.040** | suppressed | ~0 (<3e-5 G) | 0 | | Mercury | 1408 | 0.0170 | **0.040** | suppressed | **0.003 G — a real, dipolar, axially-aligned dynamo** | 0.003 | | Ganymede | 171.5 | 0.1399 | 0.140 | ok | **0.007 G — a real dynamo** | **0** | | Earth | 24 | 1.0 | 1.000 | ok | 0.5 | 0.5014 | | Mars | 24.6 | 0.976 | 0.976 | ok | 0 | 0 | | Jupiter / Saturn / Uranus / Neptune | 9.9-17.2 | — | 1.4-1.6 | ok | 4.3 / 0.2 / 0.23 / 0.14 | 4.32 / 0.24 / 0.21 / 0.22 | **TUNING 0.12 IS POINTLESS AND THAT IS THE ANSWER.** `rotationFactor` clamps to a floor of 0.04, and **BOTH bodies below the gate land ON that floor** — Venus and Mercury are IDENTICAL on rotation in this model and have opposite outcomes. What separates them is already `bigIronCore = mk.metal > 0.5`, i.e. COMPOSITION. So there is no measured body whose result would change if the threshold moved anywhere in (0.04, 1.0). **No anchor pins the constant, and moving it could not be validated against anything.** Rule 4 applied: left alone. **But the STRUCTURE is falsified by measurement, which is a different claim from "the number looks wrong".** Mercury turns once per 58.6 days — a raw rotation factor of 0.017, three times below the gate — and sustains a genuine self-sustained core dynamo. "Slow rotation prevents a dynamo" is contradicted by a Solar System body we can measure. Venus, the sole body the gate was ever calibrated on, is explained in the literature by a lack of CORE CONVECTION (stagnant lid -> poor mantle cooling -> no inner-core nucleation), not by its spin. And the field-strength scaling in `nominalGauss = 0.5 * rot * sizeF` makes strength LINEAR in rotation, where the standard numerical-dynamo result (Christensen & Aubert 2006) is that strength scales with buoyancy flux and is essentially INDEPENDENT of rotation rate. Rotation properly sets GEOMETRY — dipolar at low Rossby number, multipolar at high — which the model already half-knows, since it flips `geometry` to multipolar in this very branch and then also zeroes the strength. **The engine already derives what a better model needs, and the ordering is easy** — unlike [[B5]]. `deriveMagnetism` is at `SystemProcessor.ts:788` and `deriveGeoActivity` at `:833`: same pass, 45 lines apart, no thermal-solve boundary between them, and geology reads nothing from magnetism (grep: 0 hits). `geoActivity.vigor` is the buoyancy-flux proxy and `geoActivity.regime` is the stagnant-vs-mobile-lid distinction that is the actual Venus story. **DO NOT SELL THAT REBUILD AS "GIVE THE 41 WORLDS THEIR FIELDS BACK" — measured, it probably would not.** Of the 43 bodies the model currently calls `suppressed`, **41 have a molten-iron core in our own fluid-layer model** and their geology is vigorous (24 episodic, 12 plutonic, 1 plate-tectonics, only 6 inactive; vigor up to 2.43). But **24 of them are `episodic`-lid, exactly like Venus** — so a regime-based gate would suppress most of them too, for a better reason. The honest expected outcome is a similar answer with a defensible mechanism, not a mass restoration. **One concrete anchored MISS, separately actionable and NOT about rotation: Ganymede.** Measured 0.007 G, ours 0. It sits ABOVE the gate at 0.140, so rotation is not the cause — it fails the molten-iron branch's `massMe > 0.3` test (Ganymede is 0.025 Me) and our fluid-layer model gives it no conductive interior layer at all. That is a CORE model defect and it has a real measurement behind it, which makes it the best available starting anchor for any rework. | wont-fix (the constant) — structure falsified by Mercury + Ganymede, rebuild scoped |
| B13 | **`process()` is not idempotent: surface radiation changes on a second pass, by up to 18%.** Measured on the Local Neighbourhood map, 156 bodies, comparing one pass against two: **37 bodies drift on `surfaceRadiation` (worst 17.5%) and 4 on `habitabilityScore` (worst 19.7%)**. Pre-existing, NOT caused by [[B7]] — verified against a detached worktree at the parent commit, where it is worse (55 bodies, 18.5%). **Root cause: `calculateSurfaceRadiation` reads `body.magneticField` in pass 2 (`processEnvironment`), and `deriveMagnetism` WRITES `body.magneticField` in pass 3 (`processClassification:774`).** So the first pass shields with whatever field was authored or left over, and every later pass shields with the derived one. Since `process()` runs on load AND after every edit, the dose a GM reads depends on how many times the system has been through — a freshly imported system and the same system after one nudge print different radiation. This is exactly the one-pass lag the v2.1.282 thermal unification removed, in a different quantity, and the standing rule that anything the solve reads must be committed before it runs applies here too. Likely fix is to derive magnetism BEFORE the environment pass (it needs makeup, mass and the fluid layers, plus `equilibriumTempK` for the giant helium-rain factor — so the same ordering question [[B5]] has, and possibly the same answer). An idempotence test belongs with the fix. **ESCALATED 2026-08-01: THIS IS NOT AN 18% CURIOSITY, IT IS A 100x ERROR ON EARTH IN A LIVE CAMPAIGN.** Reported as "we seem to have killed atmo protection" — Earth's card reads **Radiation (surface) high, 230 mSv/y**, against a real background of about 2.4. **Confirmed as this bug and NOT a regression in the radiation work:** the derived fixture was regenerated from scratch and Earth still reads `surfaceRadiation = 2.298990432085668`, `radiationShieldingAtmo = 0.99937`, unchanged — and the fixture's Earth input is IDENTICAL to the campaign's, same composition, same 1.013 bar, same 0.5 G. Same code, same data, two answers a hundredfold apart. The only difference is HOW MANY TIMES `process()` has run. **AND THIS IS THE ANSWER TO "why did no test catch it?" — the physics baseline test runs `process()` EXACTLY ONCE on a fresh input, so it pins the pass-1 value and can never see a bug that only exists in the repetition.** Nothing in the suite runs the processor twice. That gap is more important than any single number: every derived figure the engine produces is currently verified only in its pass-1 form, while every figure a GM actually reads is a pass-N form. **UNRESOLVED AND FOR THE WORKER TO MEASURE, NOT GUESS:** B13's original measurement was a worst case of 17.5% for one extra pass on the Local Neighbourhood map, and this is 100x. Either the drift COMPOUNDS across many passes rather than converging after the second, or Sol exposes it far worse than that map did — plausibly because Sol's bodies carry AUTHORED magnetic fields while the 22 rebuilt systems mostly do not ([[D2a]]: 0% have atmosphere or rotation period), so the authored-versus-derived gap is much wider on Sol. Measure the value as a function of pass count on SOL specifically before fixing, and report the curve. **THE TEST THAT MUST COME WITH THE FIX:** assert that a second and third `process()` pass change nothing, on Sol AND on the Local Neighbourhood. That single assertion closes the whole class, and its absence is why a 100x error reached a card. **MEASURED 2026-08-02, and the curve settles the two open questions: it CONVERGES AT PASS 2, and the 100x on Earth is a DIFFERENT ORDERING FAULT from the one recorded above.** Ten passes, every derived leaf field on every body swept (not just the two named), on Sol and on the Local Neighbourhood. **(a) IT CONVERGES, IT DOES NOT COMPOUND.** Sol: 216 numeric (body, field) pairs move, **216 of them converge at pass 2** and never move again. Local Neighbourhood: 495 pairs, 483 converge at pass 2. So "17.5% per pass compounding to 100x in 29 passes" is refuted — pass 1 is simply a different answer from the fixed point, and every later pass agrees. That means idempotence here has to mean "pass 1 equals pass 2", not "the iteration settles". **(b) THE DRIFT IS FAR BIGGER THAN 18%, because [[B17]]/[[B22]]'s belt term landed in between.** The original 17.5% was measured before the belt existed. Sol's worst is now **360%** (Neptune's Rings, `particleRadiation` 5,735 -> 26,380) and the Local Neighbourhood's worst is **22,530%** (TRAPPIST-1 b, `particleRadiation` 549 -> 124,158). The belt reads the HOST's field and spin, so the pass-2/pass-3 lag on one body is amplified through an exponential on all of its moons. **(c) EARTH AT 230 mSv/yr IS `atmosphere.scaleHeightKm`, NOT `magneticField` — REPRODUCED EXACTLY.** The magnetic lag moves Earth by 0.0007% (2.2989904 -> 2.2989748), so it cannot be the reported fault. The real one: `beltInnerEdgeRadii` (`radiation.ts:111`) reads `body.atmosphere.scaleHeightKm`, and `recalculateAtmosphereDerivedProperties` WRITES it at `SystemProcessor.ts:517` — **58 lines AFTER `calculateSurfaceRadiation` at `:459`, in the SAME pass.** With no scale height the function returns 1 by design ("no atmosphere, no absorber: the belt reaches the surface"), so a world with an atmosphere is handed **its own belt peak at ground level**. Run on the bundled Sol with `scaleHeightKm` absent from the input: **Earth 228.25 mSv/yr** against 2.299 on the next pass — the reported 229.6 to within 0.6% — and **Jupiter 1.93e7 against 11.46, a factor of 1.68 million**; Saturn 70,325 against 5.086. **AND THE INPUT IS ABSENT ON EXACTLY THE PATH THE CAMPAIGN TOOK: `importFixup.ts:124` DELETES `scaleHeightKm`** as stale derived data, correctly, and `ImportModal`, `GenerationWizard` and `SystemView` all then call `process()` ONCE. So every imported or generated system prints the pass-1 figure until something edits it. **This is also why the fixture never saw it: `stripDerivedData` in `physics-baseline.test.ts` does not strip `scaleHeightKm`,** so the fixture's Earth is a world that has already been through the atmosphere pass, and the test's one pass is really a pass 2 for that quantity. **(d) THE FULL READ-BEFORE-WRITE SET, swept rather than assumed.** Four edges, not one: own `magneticField` (pass 2 reads, pass 3 writes); own `atmosphere.scaleHeightKm` (same pass, 58 lines late); the HOST's `magneticField` / `rotation_period_hours` / `scaleHeightKm` via the belt term, which additionally depends on NODE ORDER since a moon can be processed before its host; and `insideHostMagnetosphere` reading the host's derived field inside pass 3 with the same order dependence. **(e) A SECOND, UNRELATED NON-IDEMPOTENCE FOUND IN THE SWEEP, and it never converges at all: `processBarycenters`' binary coupling has a LIMIT CYCLE of period 2 in `omega_deg`.** `SystemProcessor.ts:277-278` assigns `m0.omega = ref.omega` and `m1.omega = ref.omega + 180` — but when `m1` IS the reference (it is heavier, or more recently edited) the reference member flips its OWN argument of periapsis by 180 degrees every pass, forever. Zeta Reticuli oscillates 0 / 180 / 0 / 180 through all twelve passes measured. It is genuine `process()` non-idempotence and it blocks any idempotence test on the Local Neighbourhood, so it is fixed here rather than deferred. **FIXED v2.1.356-beta.** **SEVEN read-before-write edges, not one, and the entry above named only the smallest of them.** Each is the same shape — a value read in one pass and written in a later one — and they are listed in the order they bite. **(1) RADIATION IS NOW ITS OWN PASS, 2c**, after every body has its field, its spin and its scale height. It used to sit in the middle of pass 2. **(2) FLUID LAYERS AND MAGNETISM ARE NOW PASS 2b**, moved out of `processClassification`, and iterated **PARENT BEFORE CHILD** — a moon's induced field asks its host whether it sits inside a magnetosphere, and the belt term asks the host for its field and spin, so in node order both answers depended on where the host happened to sit in the file. **(3) TIDAL LOCKING MOVES ABOVE THE THERMAL SOLVE.** The solve's temperature profile reads `tidallyLocked` and the rotation period and they were written after it; nothing in the lock assessment is thermal (despinning is mass, radius, distance and age), so this is a stale read removed with no circularity created. **(4) GEOLOGY MOVES ABOVE THE WEATHER.** `deriveWeather`'s lightning term reads `geology/plate-tectonics` and `tidal/volcanism` as its convection driver, and the geology block emitted them *afterwards* — so Earth's sky read "frequent lightning" on a fresh load and "constant" on every pass after. **(5) `estimateInternalHeatK` ASKED `body.classes` FOR THE WORD "ice-giant".** The classifier runs a whole pass later and reads the temperature this figure produces — a class name used as a physics input, which is the architecture rule inverted. It now uses the same mass split the interior model already uses (`GIANT_METALLIC_HYDROGEN_MIN_MASS_ME`, exported from `fluidLayers.ts` so there is one home for it). **(6) BARYCENTRE EFFECTIVE MASSES ARE COMPUTED DEEPEST-FIRST**, in their own pre-pass. Alpha Centauri lists its OUTER barycentre first, so on a fresh load the system barycentre summed Proxima plus a stale AB total — **2.43e29 against the true 4.20e30** — which moved every orbit in the system and flipped both primaries from no stability verdict at all to "Very Unstable" on the second pass. The pre-pass also breaks the genuine circularity in doing it in one loop: an inner barycentre needs its parent's mass for its own orbit while the parent needs the inner one's mass for its total. **(7) THE `omega_deg` LIMIT CYCLE**, above. Plus a `settled()` guard on the barycentre split: separation is the SUM of the members' semi-major axes and each axis is then re-derived from that sum, and in double precision that round trip lands one unit in the last place from where it started on every pass, for ever. Nothing physical moves and the number never settles, which is enough on its own. One duplicate writer was removed rather than guarded: a barycentre that is a MEMBER of another had its mean motion written twice, once from its own semi-major axis and once from the pair's separation, two formulas one ulp apart with whichever ran last winning (Algol). **WHY THIS DOES NOT REINTRODUCE THE v2.1.282 LAG, stated plainly because it was the gate.** Nothing was dragged backwards across the thermal solve. Magnetism moved DOWN the pipeline (pass 3 -> 2b), not up; `teqK` did not move at all; the lock moved up but it is not thermal in either direction. The solve still runs before everything that reads its output. **THE ONE EDGE LEFT, AND WHY IT COULD NOT BE ORDERED AWAY.** `applyAtmosphericEscape` reads `magneticField` for its shielding term, and it runs inside pass 2a — before magnetism is derived in 2b. Magnetism cannot move ahead of it, because `deriveFluidLayers` reads the composed surface temperature and `deriveMagnetism` reads `equilibriumTempK`, both of which the solve produces, and escape rewrites the atmosphere the solve reads. That is a real circle: field -> escape -> atmosphere -> thermal -> temperature -> fluid layers -> field. It is NOT closed here. It is opt-in (`evolveAtmosphere`), no bundled body in Sol, either starmap or any example carries the flag, and the physics baseline's own Mars-aging idempotence check still passes because Mars's field is 0 on both passes. Worth knowing: the field's thermal dependency is only the giant helium-rain factor and the induced-ocean case, so for a rocky world with air to lose the field is a pure function of makeup, mass and spin — but a magnetism that is derived early for some bodies and late for others would be two evaluations of one question, which is precisely what the architecture rule forbids. Left as a single evaluation with the residual documented. **THE TEST: `src/lib/system/idempotence.test.ts`.** Process, process the result, process that — and require that nothing anywhere on any body changed, comparing pass 1 against 2 AND 2 against 3 (one comparison alone would have called the `omega_deg` oscillation stable). Five cases: bundled Sol, **bundled Sol through `fixUpImportedSystem`** (the path the campaign actually took), both bundled starmaps, and every shipped example system. It went in RED, on 231 fields, in its own commit ahead of the fix. **THE ANCHORS, AFTER THE FIX.** Earth **2.29897 mSv/yr** (real background ~2.4) — unmoved to five significant figures; Mars **213.692** (Curiosity RAD ~210) — unmoved exactly; Venus 2.000, Luna 512.38, Titan 2.0002, Triton 2.566 — all unmoved. **Io 13,139 Sv/yr = 36.00 Sv/day against B17's 36 anchor, where it read 34.9 before — the fix put B17's own calibration back on its target.** The 3% was the belt evaluating **Jupiter's AUTHORED 4.28 G** while B17 calibrated against the **DERIVED 4.32**: dose goes as B² and the scale length as B^(1/3), which compounds to +3.05%. **Ganymede 45.05 Sv/yr = 0.1234 Sv/day, exactly the pure-law figure B17 quotes and exactly the 1.54x over-prediction it designed for** — which CLOSES B17's open item (a), "Ganymede's authored 0.007 G is overwritten by `deriveMagnetism`'s 0, yet `radiationShieldingMag` reads 0.0768... Left for B13". Europa 1,403.7 Sv/yr (3.85 Sv/day against a measured 5.4, ratio 0.71 where B17 had 0.76); Callisto 38.81 mSv/yr of belt = 1.06e-4 Sv/day against its 1e-4 anchor, 6% high — the one anchor that moved AWAY, and by the same B_ref correction, so B17's two-point fit should be re-derived at 4.32 if that 6% is ever worth closing. Mercury 3,483.6, Enceladus 2,027.1, Jupiter 11.46 / Saturn 5.086 / Uranus 2.665 / Neptune 2.275 at their 1-bar levels — sane ordering throughout. **THE DERIVED DIFF: 26 bodies in Sol, radiation figures and their habitability knock-ons, plus FOUR tag values. No temperature, no classification, no makeup, no orbit and no magnetic field moved anywhere in Sol.** **TAGS CHASED, as the standing rule requires.** Checked: `surface/irradiation` (buckets `irradiationDose`, which moved on 2 Sol bodies — neither crossed a bucket edge); `aurora/*` (reads the field and the dose — the TIER held on all three giants, only the strength VALUE moved, Saturn 0.25->0.26, Uranus 0.26->0.25, Neptune 0.22->0.26); `magnetic/*` (emitted in 2b now, and nothing after strips it); `habitability/*` (radiation is a habitability factor — Ariel is the only score to move, 0 -> 1.48); `weather/lightning` (Earth frequent -> constant, which is the CORRECTION: Earth has plate tectonics and the driver was simply being read too early). Deliberately left: `geology/*`, `surface/age`, `structure/cloud-deck`, `volatiles/*` in Sol — none moved. **THE ONE PLACE PHYSICS GENUINELY CHANGED, measured against a reverted build rather than reasoned: the ice-giant split moves 12 bodies, ALL of them in `static/examples`, NONE in Sol and none in either bundled starmap.** They are mini-Neptunes, hycean and sub-Neptune worlds between 1.3 and 35.6 Earth masses that were being handed **Jupiter's formation heat** — 52 K against the ice giant's 24 K — because their class string did not happen to contain the characters "ice-giant". Internal heat falls by 54% and surface temperature by 4 to 28 K (TRAPPIST-1 g 230.4 -> 208.4 K, Proxima Centauri c 86.8 -> 59.5 K, Testion's "ice giant" 124.0 -> 103.1 K). **No class moved and exactly one tag moved** — Proxima Centauri c gains nitrogen to its retained ices, correctly, being 27 K colder. **NOT DONE, said plainly.** (a) The `evolveAtmosphere` escape edge above. (b) `processEnvironment` still ends with a dead `retainsAtmosphere` local that reads the field; it is unused so it cannot cause drift, and it was left rather than widen the diff. (c) [[B5]] is still gated — this establishes the pattern it asked for (a derivation moved across a pass boundary with an idempotence test to prove it), but B5's move is UP across the thermal solve and this one was DOWN, which is the easy direction. | fixed v2.1.356-beta |
| B14 | **The `Testion-System.json` per-class fixture no longer demonstrates four of its classes, because their authored atmospheres put their surfaces far outside the band they are named for.** Fallout from [[B6]], and the fixture is the messenger rather than the victim. `t-21-ammonia-planet` has a 346 K surface (ammonia boils at ~250 K at its 1.5 bar); `t-23-earth-like` 346 K, i.e. 73 °C, from 1% CO2 — twenty-five times Earth's — against a body labelled habitable; `t-24-superhabitable` 371 K, 98 °C, with 80% ocean; `t-5-coreless` loses to `desert` on base-class exclusivity. These were only ever their named class because the fingerprints read equilibrium temperature and their equilibrium temperatures ARE in band. **The fix is to re-author the four bodies' atmospheres so their SURFACES land where intended — not to widen the bands**, which would put the contradiction back. Worth doing because a one-body-per-class fixture that silently stopped covering four classes is a test that has quietly stopped testing. Check the rest of the file at the same time: only these four were caught by a class CHANGE, and a body could be sitting mid-band for the wrong reason without moving. **FIXED v2.1.337-beta. In four of the five the AUTHORED DATA is wrong and the band is right; no band was touched.** - **ammonia planet — DATA WRONG.** 1.5 bar of pure NH3 is a strong greenhouse and put the surface at 346 K; ammonia boils near 250 K at that pressure, so the body had no ammonia hydrosphere to be named for. The 150-280 K band is right. Pressure to 0.2 bar -> **249.2 K -> `planet/ammonia-planet`**. - **earth like — DATA WRONG.** 1% CO2, twenty-five times Earth's, for +92 K and a 73 °C surface. The 250-315 K band is right — Earth's 287 K sits mid-band. CO2 to 400 ppm -> **289.6 K -> `planet/earth-like`**. - **superhabitable — DATA WRONG**, same cause: 371.4 K (98 °C) with 80% ocean that would be near boiling. CO2 to 400 ppm -> **316.4 K -> `planet/superhabitable`**. - **swamp — DATA WRONG, and the mirror of [[B15]].** The body is 1.00 Me, 1.00 Re, Earth's exact N2/O2 at 1.1 bar with a biosphere — it IS Earth, so earth-analogue beating it was correct. A swamp world should be WETTER than Earth: coverage 0.8 -> 0.90, inside swamp's 0.6-0.95 and outside earth-analogue's 0.5-0.85 cap. Now `planet/swamp`, and it no longer competes with the Earth twin. - **coreless — NEITHER.** Its 268 K dry surface is legitimately a desert AND its interior is legitimately coreless; both are `kind: base` and bases are mutually exclusive. Not a band error and not a data error, but a taxonomy question — a STRUCTURAL claim competing in the same pool as surface claims. Left alone deliberately. **A FIFTH case found, diagnosed and deliberately LEFT, because the diagnosis is the deliverable.** `forest` classifies as `planet/silicate` and has done since before this session: at 268.9 K its water is frozen, so `liquidCoverage` is 0 and the fingerprint is disqualified outright. **It is not fixable with the CO2 knob** — no CO2 gives 268.9 K, 100 ppm gives 295.1 K and 400 ppm gives 306.8 K, jumping clean over the 273-292 K window it needs. That is the **ice-albedo bistability**: bright ice and no vapour below freezing, dark water and vapour feedback above — the standing "a bright condensate makes the solve bistable" rule showing up in a fixture. It needs an ORBIT change, not an atmosphere nudge. Reverted to as authored. **Sol's derived output is unchanged, correctly** — Testion is a separate example file and no bundled starmap body is involved. **The wider finding, captured not fixed: the fixture is broken far past the four named.** At least eight more bodies do not classify as the class they are named for, nearly all pre-existing — `ocean` (416 K, boiled off), `earth analogue` (244.8 K, too cold), `barren`, `crater`, all three `eyeball` variants, `hot neptune`, `puffy` and `brown dwarf`. **A one-body-per-class fixture with no assertion per body will keep silently ceasing to cover things**; it wants a pass of its own that asserts each body classifies as its own name, which would have caught all of this the day it broke. | fixed v2.1.337-beta (4 of 5) — coreless is a taxonomy question, forest diagnosed and left |
| B15 | **`planet/swamp` out-scores `planet/earth-analogue` on Earth's own numbers.** Noticed while measuring [[B6]]. Earth's classification explanation now reads base `planet/swamp` at 2.10 with `planet/jungle` runner-up at 2.06 — a clean 1.0 on all four swamp bands (biosphere, surface 286.9 K in 278-300, water, liquidCoverage 0.71 in 0.6-0.95). It does not reach the screen, because Earth is hand-authored and keeps `planet/earth-analogue`; but a GENERATED Earth twin with a biosphere would come out a swamp world. The cause is weight, not bands: the biosphere types carry `weight: 1.5` and score `mean x (1 + 0.1n) x weight`, so four perfect bands at 1.5 beats five perfect bands at 1.0. Note the existing test 'classifies an Earth twin as the specific type' passes, so the synthetic twin in that fixture does NOT have a biosphere — which is why this has never shown up. Decide whether earth-analogue/earth-like should out-weigh the biome types (they are the more specific claim: a named Earth twin rather than a generic living world) or whether a living Earth genuinely IS a swamp world by this taxonomy. Cheap to change, so worth being deliberate about rather than nudging a weight. **FIXED v2.1.336-beta. NEITHER band is at fault — it is the WEIGHT, and that is the finding.** earth-analogue's five bands are the most demanding in the pack (0.8-1.3 Me, 0.9-1.15 Re, 10-35% O2, 255-300 K, 50-85% liquid water) and Earth matches all five perfectly. swamp's four are not generous either: Earth genuinely is a warm, waterlogged, living world. Score is `(mean fit) x (1 + 0.1 x bands) x weight`, and **the specificity bonus was working correctly** — earth-analogue 1.5 against swamp 1.4. The weight overturned it. **Against the pack's own scale the omission is obvious**: subsurface-ocean 1.8, forest/jungle/swamp and cold-eyeball 1.5, carbon/sulfur 1.4, the exotics 1.3, and the generic types pushed DOWN (silicate 0.95, barren 0.85, crater 0.8) — while **earth-analogue, earth-like and superhabitable were the only distinctive world types in the set carrying no weight at all**, i.e. level with `planet/terrestrial`. Earth scored swamp 2.10 against earth-analogue 1.50. **Weight 1.45, and the value encodes a rule that can be stated in one sentence: an Earth twin outranks a biome label only when it matches Earth EXACTLY; miss any of the five and the biome describes it better.** 1.5 was tried first and is WRONG — it let a PARTIAL earth-analogue beat a PERFECT jungle. Testion's jungle body sits at 314 K against the 255-300 band (fit 0.689) and scored 2.11 against jungle's 2.10, which is exactly the pathology the scoring header says it was redesigned to prevent. 1.45 puts a perfect match at 2.175 (beats 2.10) and that partial at 2.040 (loses to 2.10). **Only earth-analogue is weighted** — earth-like and superhabitable are GENERIC habitables that a biome type legitimately refines, so they stay at 1.0. **Four bodies move, all correct.** Earth in all three copies of Sol (best-fit swamp 2.10 -> earth-analogue 2.17; its DISPLAYED class was already earth-analogue, being hand-authored, so this was only ever visible in the "why this type" block). Testion's "swamp" body -> earth-analogue, which is RIGHT and belongs to [[B14]]: it is 1.00 Me, 1.00 Re, Earth's exact N2/O2 at 1.1 bar with a biosphere — Earth with more ocean, not a swamp world. **Swept the other bundled Earth-likes as asked:** Diamedes, Reach and Uggi's main world unaffected; Pandora stays `planet/earth-like`; TRAPPIST-1 e/f keep authored classes. Derived diff is ONE body, ONE field — Earth's `classification` block. Raised while measuring: [[B16]]. | fixed v2.1.336-beta |
| B16 | **A PARTIAL match of a more-specific type can still out-score a PERFECT match of a less-specific one.** Found while fixing [[B15]], and it is the reason that fix uses weight 1.45 rather than 1.5. `fingerprintScore` is `(mean fit) x (1 + 0.1 x bands) x weight`, and the header comment says it was deliberately reshaped so "partial fits now drag the score down instead of padding it up" and so "a band-rich catch-all whose extra bands are barely-true edge slivers can no longer out-score a perfect match on fewer bands". That holds only while weights are equal. At weight 1.5, `planet/earth-analogue` with ONE band at fit 0.689 (Testion's jungle body, 314 K against a 255-300 K band) scored 2.11 against a PERFECT `planet/jungle` at 2.10 — the exact pathology, reintroduced by a weight rather than by a band count. Worked around per-type for now by choosing a weight that cannot do it, which is fragile: every future weight change has to re-derive the same inequality by hand. **The candidate rule is that no fingerprint with any band below full fit may out-score one with every band at full fit** — a tier ordering rather than a scalar comparison. Cheap to state, needs a sweep of all 70 fingerprints to confirm it does not reshuffle anything else, so it wants its own pass. **FIXED v2.1.368-beta, and the candidate rule in this entry is exactly what shipped:** a type the body falls INSIDE every band of outranks a type it falls outside of, and score decides only within a tier. Weights keep ranking types that all genuinely fit and can no longer buy a type past a better-fitting rival. Modifiers are unaffected — they are chosen by a score threshold, not by rank. **One comparator, both call sites:** `classifyByFingerprint` and `explainClassification` sorted independently, so left alone the explanation would have named a different winner than the class the body carries. **THE SWEEP THIS ENTRY ASKED FOR: all 366 bundled bodies, seven move, four distinct, and every one is an improvement — in each case the losing type was outside one of its own bands.** Wolf 1061 d ice-giant→sub-neptune (mass 7.70 Me against [8-50], fit 0.75); TRAPPIST-1 d cold-eyeball→sub-earth (288 K against [120-255] — "icy except the substellar point" at 15 C); Proxima Centauri d hot-eyeball→sub-earth (below the 320 K floor); **Pandora earth-like→silicate (318.03 K against [250-315], fit 0.94).** **PANDORA IS THE ONE TO ARGUE ABOUT and the evidence is here rather than buried:** it misses by 3 K on a 65 K band and `silicate` is a duller label for a lush moon — but the BAND is making that call, not this rule. earth-like stops at 315 K = 42 C, Pandora's derived surface is 45 C, and under the old behaviour it won anyway by matching MORE BANDS while sitting outside one of them, which is precisely the pathology this item is about. **If Pandora should read earth-like the argument is over where that band ends, and it should be made deliberately rather than by leaving a scoring hole open to hide it.** **`tests/output/solar-system-derived.json`: 190 lines and ZERO class changes** — every line is the "why this type" candidate list re-sorting for Earth, Saturn, Enceladus and Tethys. Saturn's mini-jupiter (1.14, partial) now sits BELOW gas-giant (1.08, clean); Earth's partial jungle drops out of the top five. The panel now shows the types the body actually fits ahead of the ones that merely score well. **A process note worth keeping:** the proof-by-removal was wrong the first time — a multi-line string replacement silently failed to apply, the suite came back green, and that looked like evidence the tests did not exercise the rule. Re-run against a single-line edit it fails correctly. Verify that a disabling edit APPLIED before believing what the suite says about it. Related: [[B15]], [[B25]]. | fixed v2.1.368-beta |
| B24 | **A stability verdict can print a reason that contradicts it.** Found while doing [[B19]]. Several bodies read, in one string, "...but a locked mean-motion resonance keeps their conjunctions away from the crossing point, so it stays stable" followed by "Predicted outcome: flung out". Both halves are correct in isolation: the resonance note comes from the ORBIT-CROSSING test, which correctly spared the pair, while the fate came from a DIFFERENT driver on the same body (usually `assessHostBindingStability` — "Orbit exceeds host's stable Hill sphere"). `mergeAssessment` collects every reason and lets the most severe driver own the fate, so reasons and fate can end up describing different mechanisms with nothing marking which reason produced the verdict. Seen on Bergamen and Hades in the Uggi example. **The fix is presentational rather than physical** — carry the reason that OWNS the fate, or group reasons by driver so the verdict sits with its own cause. Do not resolve it by dropping the resonance note; that note is doing real work and is the more interesting half. **FIXED v2.1.368-beta. WHICH HALF IS RIGHT: both, about their own mechanism** — so presentational exactly as this entry judged. The orbit-crossing test genuinely spared the pair (the resonance does keep conjunctions off the crossing point); the host-binding test genuinely failed the body (it is outside its host's Hill sphere). **Two faults in the one string, so two changes.** (1) **The resonance note claimed more than its test had measured.** "so it stays stable" is a verdict on the whole body when the crossing test only examined the crossing — now "so this crossing on its own is survivable". The note is kept in full, as instructed, and no longer contradicts anything. (2) **The fate now travels with the reason that produced it**, so the verdict prints beside its own cause: "Predicted outcome: flung out … Driven by: Orbit exceeds host's stable Hill sphere". Only when there is more than one driver — with a single reason the cause is unambiguous and attribution would be noise. **Verified on the two bodies named: Bergamen and Hades in Uggi each read as one argument, and across that system 8 bodies carry a fate, 6 are attributed, 0 unattributed contradictions remain.** **The test fixture is MOONS, not planets, and that is the point:** a body orbiting a star has no meaningful outer binding limit, so the first version produced the sparing reason and NO fate — the assertion sits inside an `if` and was passing while proving nothing. Caught by adding a guard that the fixture still reproduces the shape, which is now its own test. **`tests/output/solar-system-derived.json`: 2 lines, 2 bodies — Pluto and Charon, wording only. Neither gains an attribution, which is the single-driver rule behaving: their crossing IS their only driver.** | fixed v2.1.368-beta |
| B25 | **Fifteen gas-dominated bodies classify as eyeballs.** Found while sweeping [[B18]]: of the 59 bundled bodies with `makeup.gas > 0.5`, ten are `planet/cold-eyeball` and five are `planet/hot-eyeball`. The three eyeball fingerprints key on `starTidallyLocked` plus a `SurfaceTemp_K` band and nothing else, so they never test whether the body HAS a surface to have a permanent dayside on — a tidally locked gas giant matches as readily as a tidally locked rock. The eyeball notes are explicitly about ground ("molten/dry dayside", "icy except the substellar point", "temperate oasis"), which is what [[B3]] and [[B6]] established when they moved the family onto surface temperature, so a makeup gate is consistent with the reasoning already applied to them. Cheap to fix — a `makeup.gas` band on the three fingerprints, as rule-pack data — but it WILL move classifications, so it wants the usual before/after diff rather than a blind edit. **FIXED v2.1.368-beta. ALL NINE DISTINCT BODIES MOVE TO A BETTER CLASS, and three land on the name their own author gave them** — GJ 674 b (0.60 gas, 488 K) hot-eyeball→sub-neptune; GJ 687 b→super-neptune; GJ 876 c→puffy; GJ 876 b→water-clouds-gas-giant; GJ 876 e and GJ 682 c→ice-giant; and Testion's "hot neptune", "puffy" and "alkali metal clouds gas giant" now classify as hot-neptune, puffy and alkali-metal-clouds-gas-giant respectively. Fifteen rows across the two starmaps and the examples, nothing else. **THE FIRST ATTEMPT WAS WRONG IN AN INSTRUCTIVE WAY AND THE MECHANISM IS THE REAL FINDING.** Adding `makeup.gas` as a MATCH band did remove all fifteen — and turned SIX temperate rocky worlds at 278-303 K into eyeballs. Ross 128 b at 292 K became a "hot eyeball" (band starts at 320); GJ 1061 c at 289 K became a "cold eyeball" ("icy except the substellar point"). **The cause is arithmetic, not tuning: a gate written as a band is always-true for every body that survives it, and averaging an always-1 band in RAISES a poor defining band. Fit 0.11 gains 37%; a perfect fit gains 8%. It rewards the worst matches most** — the exact pathology the scoring redesign exists to prevent. A weight adjustment cannot undo it either, because the distortion is fit-dependent and a weight is a flat multiplier. **So `Fingerprint` gained `gate`: preconditions that must hold or the type scores 0, contributing NOTHING — no fit, no band count.** Eligibility and definition are different questions and the schema now says so. With the gate the diff is exactly the fifteen intended bodies. **A missing feature fails a gate**, as it fails a match band; the three existing eyeball fixtures now state `makeup.gas: 0`, making the precondition explicit. **The overlap audit builds each type's prototype from its bands and now includes gates** — otherwise a gated type scores 0 against its own prototype and reports as shadowed, which it did the moment the gate landed. Caught by that audit, which is what it is for. **Docs: two were stale from [[B15]]'s scoring change rather than from this one** — the Newton trace called the score "Σ band fits × weight" in its output label and "the sum of its band fits" in its runner-up note, and `classification-and-tags.md` said "sum of band fits"; all three now say mean fit × specificity × weight. The physics page listed eyeball as a MODIFIER; it is a base type. **`tests/output/solar-system-derived.json` UNCHANGED** — Sol has no tidally locked giants. Related: [[B18]], [[B16]]. | fixed v2.1.368-beta |
| B26 | **A ring carries a "surface radiation" dose.** Noticed while doing [[B17]], and it predates it: `Jupiter's Rings`, `Saturn's Rings`, `Uranus' Rings` and `Neptune's Rings` all carry a `surfaceRadiation` figure, and the belt term has now made them the loudest numbers in the system (Jupiter's Rings 352 Sv/day, above Io). The number is not WRONG — a probe in the ring plane at 1.8 R_J really would take that — but a ring is distributed debris with no surface to stand on, so "surface dose" is the same category question [[B18]] answered for gas giants: does this axis mean anything for this kind of body? Note the two differ in an important way, though, and that is the reason this is captured rather than fixed by analogy: a giant's envelope has no place to stand at ALL, whereas a ring is made of countless small bodies that each DO have a surface, so the honest answer may be that the figure is meaningful and only the LABEL is wrong. Decide which before changing anything. **DECIDED AND FIXED v2.1.358-beta: THE FIGURE IS MEANINGFUL, THE LABEL WAS WRONG — the second of the two readings this entry set out, not the first.** The distinction the entry drew is the one that settles it. [[B18]] DELETED a giant's habitability score because the AXIS meant nothing for that kind of body — there is no place to stand in an envelope, so there is nothing for a surface score to be about. A ring is not that: it is countless small bodies that each have a surface, and the number is the dose at the ring's orbital distance, which is simultaneously **what a fragment's surface takes and what a ship crossing the ring takes**. A GM planning a ring transit wants that figure. So it stays, and it is named for where it is: **`Radiation (in the ring plane)`**. **ONE HELPER, THREE KINDS OF BODY.** `radiationPlace()` in `bodyFacts.ts` now answers "which place does this figure describe" once — `surface`, `at 1 bar` (a giant, [[B22]]'s wording, unchanged) or `in the ring plane`. Jupiter's Rings read **360 Sv/day in the ring plane**, above Io, which is correct rather than alarming: the main ring sits near 1.8 R_J, inside Io and close to the belt peak. **AND THE RING CORRECTLY GETS ONE ROW, NOT TWO.** Its surface and orbital figures are the SAME number to the last digit (131,496,772.635 mSv/yr) because for a ring the two places coincide — no field to deflect the stellar particle channel, no atmosphere to attenuate anything, no terrestrial background term. B22's existing gate (an orbital row only when the two genuinely differ by half again) already suppressed the duplicate, so nothing needed adding. Pinned by a test. **THE GM PANEL WAS STILL SAYING "Surface Radiation" FOR EVERY NON-STAR, which means B22's fix had only ever reached the player-facing block.** `BodyTechnicalDetails.svelte` hardcoded that label, so a GM looking at Jupiter — or at a ring — read "Surface Radiation" on a body with no surface. It now uses the same helper, so both sides of the app name the same place with the same words. That was not this item's scope but it was this item's exact fault, one panel over. **NO TAG.** A ring gets no `hazard/radiation` tag from [[B28]] — `processClassification` returns early for anything that is not a planet or a moon, so rings carry no derived tags at all, and a "surface hazard" tag would reintroduce precisely the claim this item removes. **DERIVED DIFF: EMPTY.** Nothing in the physics changed; this is a label and it was verified by the fixture NOT moving. | fixed v2.1.358-beta |
| A8 | **The rest of the GM starmap's scaling text.** A4 fixed the system names, the depth annotations and the placement ghost. Still inside the world transform and still growing with zoom: the ROUTE labels (`.route-label` / `.route-name`, positioned along a line, so they need the same treatment applied to a transform rather than an x/y pair), the measure read-out, and the `+` indicator on a system with more than four stars — that last is arguably part of the marker glyph rather than a label and may be right as it is. Same one-line fix per label; `labelK` already exists in `Starmap.svelte`. Left out of A4 to keep that change to what was reported. **Done for the route distance, the route name and the measure read-out; the `+` is deliberately NOT done.** The route name needed no special handling in the end: its `rotate()` is about the midpoint, so scaling the y-offset keeps the name the same distance off the line at every zoom and the transform is left untouched — the worry in the entry above about "a transform rather than an x/y pair" did not materialise. **The `+` call, with the reason:** the star discs it annotates are plain `r={5}` circles at fixed world offsets with no counter-scale, so the whole marker glyph grows with the map. The `+` is typography belonging to that glyph, not an annotation of the map, and pinning it to a constant screen size would make it drift off its own marker at high zoom and swamp it at low zoom. It scales because the thing it belongs to scales. Verified by measuring on-screen bounding boxes across a 4.9x zoom range (scale 0.397 → 1.94 → 0.397): route label, route name and measure read-out are pixel-identical at every step while the route line goes 189 → 706 px and the measure line 144 → 706. | fixed v2.1.310-beta |
| A9 | **True scale cannot be both visible and in proportion, and the dial should probably say which it means.** Raised by F2/F3. At true scale Earth's rendered radius at whole-system framing is about 0.05 px — genuinely true is genuinely invisible, so some floor must exist, and any floor puts bodies out of proportion with their orbits. v2.1.288 keeps a floor at 2% of readable, which stops the belt burying the planets without making the planets honest. If a real "true proportions" reading is wanted, the options are (a) a SCREEN-space floor — a body renders at its true size whenever that exceeds about 1 px and never vanishes below it, costing a per-frame rescale, which is what the construct glyphs already do; or (b) floor the SYSTEM rather than each body, scaling everything by one common factor so relative sizes survive the magnification. Neither is a bug fix; both are a decision about what the dial promises. **Resolved by taking option (a), across v2.1.297-303.** Commit trail: a screen-space visibility floor so planets are drawn at all (297); framing a body at true scale no longer clips it through the near plane (299); "true scale means true" — real radii, pixel floors, camera unclamped (300); the focus ease now expires on arrival rather than after a fixed frame count (301); the planet sits ON its orbit line, rocks are planet-sized and moons are reachable (302); and the Spread and Body-size dials carry a green "actual" pip marking where the setting is physically honest (303). The pip is the answer to "the dial should say which it means" — rather than renaming the ends, it marks the one point on the travel that is true and lets the rest be readable. Verdicts here are from commit subjects and the live session, not a diff read. | fixed v2.1.297-303-beta |
| A10 | **The legacy Field Guide skins read a GM-local store.** From the A3 investigation. The old console skin renders `SystemVisualizer`, whose promotion to the feature-rich `PlanetDisc` overlay is gated on `get(trueColorMode)` — a store written only by the GM's own system view. It defaults to true so it usually looks right, but a GM who turns true colour off in their orrery silently strips surface features from every player view on that browser. Also from that dig: `DISC_OVERLAY_MIN_R = 11` px means nothing is promoted at whole-system zoom even in the GM view, so the surface-feature work is invisible in the most-used view — the threshold guards real cost (an SVG overlay per body), so do not lower it without measuring frame rate on a busy system. Lower priority than it reads: the Field Guide rail entry sits behind `PLAYER_VIEWS_ENABLED` as of v2.1.287, so on beta these paths are reachable only by URL. **DITCHED 2026-08-03, SUPERSEDED BY [[A42]]** — the Field Guide and Projector are being REMOVED outright rather than repaired, so a GM-local store read by a skin that will not exist is not worth fixing. Fold any surviving insight into A42's removal check. | wont-fix — superseded by the [[A42]] removal |
| A11 | **The picker should float, on every screen, GM and player alike — the way the time control already does.** It currently sits as a fixed strip at the top of the view, which is why A6 calls it "sometimes ineffective": it takes permanent space and is not where the eye is. The model to copy is the collapsed time puck in `src/routes/catalogue/+page.svelte:1067` — a `.time-controls` element with an `expanded` flag and `use:clickOutside` to close, i.e. a puck that opens on demand and gets out of the way. **One component, six mount sites**, all of `BodyPicker.svelte`: GM starmap (`Starmap.svelte:1062`), GM system view (`SystemView.svelte:2193`), transit planner (`TransitPlannerPanel.svelte:942`), the main route twice (`routes/+page.svelte:1589` and `:1747`) and the player catalogue (`routes/catalogue/+page.svelte:1054`). Do the floating behaviour INSIDE `BodyPicker` behind an opt-in prop rather than at six call sites, or the next surface reintroduces the strip. Note the transit-planner mount is inside a panel, not over a map — it likely wants to stay inline, so the prop needs to default to the current behaviour. `SystemView.svelte:2722` already has `position: relative` to "anchor the BodyPicker + time overlays", and `:2738` documents the z-order (the eye sits above the picker at 60) — that stacking is the existing constraint to respect. **Built as an opt-in `floating` prop on `BodyPicker`, default OFF — and only THREE of the six mounts wanted it.** The other three are already `inline`: the transit planner is a form field, and `routes/+page.svelte:1589`/`:1747` are `inline startOpen` inside the dedicated "All bodies & constructs" and "Constructs" dialogs, where the picker IS the modal's content. None of those is a strip over a map, so the complaint does not apply to them and they are untouched (verified: the find-a-body dialog still renders the same 495 px inline strip, list open). The three that float are the GM starmap, the GM system view and the PLAYER catalogue. The puck keeps the strip's own anchor rather than inventing a position, so every host's existing placement CSS still works — the catalogue's `.holo-picker-left` override puts its puck at left 10, the two GM mounts stay top-centre at `top`. One trap worth recording: the collapsed container must be `width: auto`, or a 420 px transparent box goes on sitting over the map swallowing clicks. Dismissal is outside-click, Escape, a pick, or the host changing focus; the outside-click listener has to survive the dropdown being shut, since that listener is what puts the puck away. Verified live on all three: puck 42 px → 420 px strip with a working list, no overlap with the SystemView eye button (strip right 723, eye left 813), and the player mount exercised in a real live player view over the broadcast. **Does NOT share a root with A13**, despite both being "floating chrome on the starmap": A13 was an ancestor `overflow: hidden` throwing away growth the panel had already done, A11 is a shape change in a different component with no persisted state at all. They were done in one session but they are two unrelated fixes. **FOLLOW-UP v2.1.311 on review: "floating" was only half of it — the picker and the time transport had to become the SAME control, not two that resemble each other.** The behaviour now lives in `src/lib/ui/floatingControl.ts` with two shared chrome components (`FloatGrip`, `FloatPin`), and both the transport and the picker consume it: slim move handle left, lock right, collapse to a puck, put itself away on the first pointerdown anywhere else unless locked. The lock REPLACED the transport's minimise button and had to keep both meanings, so unlocking also puts the control away — otherwise the one button a GM already knew stopped doing what it did. Defaults chosen to change nothing unasked: the transport starts locked open (its previous always-on state), the picker starts as a puck. **Three things only the live pass found.** (1) `didDrag()` was STICKY — set on a grip drag and never cleared, so a later click that fired no pointerdown (keyboard activation, or the same flag read by a different grip) inherited the old verdict and refused to open the puck; it consumes the flag now. (2) The picker's drag offset sits on an INNER element, because the outer box is what each host anchors (the catalogue pins it left, the GM mounts centre it) — and a parent's `getBoundingClientRect()` is unaffected by a transformed child, so the on-screen clamp measured the wrong rectangle and did nothing. `float.root` had to move onto the shifted element. (3) Nothing clamped on LOAD, only during a drag: an offset dragged on a desktop opened a phone with no puck on screen to grab. Now clamped on mount and on resize — verified by storing dx 900/1100 and reloading at 375x812, both pulled fully inside. The grip hides while locked (nothing to drag, and 15 px of pill back on a narrow phone); a locked control is repositioned by unlocking, dragging the puck, and locking again. **SECOND follow-up v2.1.312, from the next look.** (a) The picker GREW SYMMETRICALLY: the host centres it with `translateX(-50%)`, so expanding from a 41 px puck to a 420 px strip moved the left edge 190 px left and the puck slid out from under the pointer into the middle of the bar. The time transport never had this because it is `inline-block` anchored bottom-left and simply grows right. Fixed with one rule — a floating picker anchors its LEFT edge (`transform: none`) — which also matches the catalogue's existing left-anchored override instead of fighting it. The lone puck now sits a puck's-half right of true centre, which reads as centred. (b) Nothing re-clamped when a control CHANGED SIZE, only on mount, resize and mid-drag — so opening near an edge ran straight off it. Now re-clamped on open, on lock and on drag release. **Two traps inside that:** the clamp was scheduled on `requestAnimationFrame`, which is SUSPENDED while the page is not painting (minimised, background tab) — so a control opened off-screen while hidden was still off-screen when you came back; it is a macrotask now, which also made it testable in the automated pane, where rAF is exactly as unreliable. And the in-drag clamp reads a rect one frame behind, which converges over a slow drag but lets a FLICK that ends at the edge stop just past it — hence the extra clamp on release. Validated at 375x812 by flicking both controls into the bottom-right corner and opening them: puck 330-371 opening to a 351 px strip at 20-371, clock 343-371 opening to a pill at 52-371, all wholly on screen. | fixed v2.1.308-beta, v2.1.311-312-beta |
| A12 | **When "Ignore depth when measuring distances" is on, the GM starmap should stop showing z-axis information.** If depth does not count, the depth annotations are noise on the map. The setting is a single campaign flag — `Starmap.ignoreZForDistances`, written by the checkbox at `SettingsModal.svelte:345`, and there is already ONE predicate for reading it: `zCounts()` at `src/lib/map/systemDistance.ts:22`, whose comment says depth counts unless the campaign opts out. Use that, do not read the flag directly. What to hide in `Starmap.svelte`: the `.depth-label` WS7 depth cue on each system marker (~1382-1397, the signed "above/below the plane" text) and the same cue on the placement ghost (~1406). **Judgement call to make explicitly, not silently:** the "Set Depth…" context-menu item (~1470) should probably STAY — the depth data still exists and the campaign can turn the setting back on, so hiding the editor would make the value uneditable rather than merely uncounted. Say which way it went in the commit. Check the 3D starmap too: this is stated as a GM-starmap item, and a genuine 3D view showing depth it is not counting is a different question — do not change it without asking. **Done as written: both cues gated on `zCounts(starmap)`, nothing reads the flag directly.** Verified live by round-tripping the checkbox on the Science Fiction map — 41 depth cues, 0, 41 — with the system names untouched at 42 throughout. **The judgement call, made explicitly: the depth EDITORS stay.** "Set Depth…" is still on the star context menu with the setting on (verified), and so is the elevation control in the "Add System near here…" placement dialogue, which is the same class of thing and was not in the original write-up. The reasoning is the one the entry suggested: the z value still exists and the campaign can turn counting back on, so hiding the editor would make depth uneditable rather than merely uncounted — and an editor is not an annotation, which is what the observation was about. The 3D starmap is untouched; see A14 for why that is a separate question rather than an oversight. | fixed v2.1.309-beta |
| A13 | **The starmap Description box shows about 20 words and its resize grabber does not genuinely resize.** `StarmapInfoPanel.svelte` — the desktop draggable panel holding Description + GM Notes. Editing in situ is right and should stay (no pencil-icon mode was wanted); the default panel size is right and should stay too. The ask is that the grabber at the bottom of the box actually makes the box bigger and shows more description. **Verified mechanism, three CSS rules fighting each other.** The textarea already has `resize: vertical; min-height: 56px` (~172-175) so the browser DOES draw the native grabber and the element does grow — but `.info-panel` is `max-height: 70%` with `overflow: hidden` (110-121) and `.info-body` is `overflow-y: auto` (152-158). So dragging the grabber makes the textarea taller INSIDE a fixed-height panel and the body simply becomes a scroll region: the visible window never changes, which is exactly "it doesn't resize". Compounding it, the panel is `width: min(320px, calc(100% - 24px))` and the description is `rows="3"` — 320 px x 3 rows is where the ~20 words comes from. **Fix direction:** let the PANEL follow its content instead of clipping it — grow with the resized textarea up to a viewport-relative ceiling — or give the panel its own real resize handle. Either way the size should persist the way the position already does (`pos.x/pos.y` are clamped into view and saved, ~26; size belongs in the same store, with the same clamp so a size dragged on a desktop cannot strand the panel on a phone). GM Notes stay always-editable and get the same treatment for free, being the same field pattern. **ROOT CAUSE, measured live: the panel is anchored by its TOP and defaults to the BOTTOM of the canvas, so the grabber grew it into CLIPPED space.** The triage above named `max-height: 70%` as the clip. That rule is real, but it is the SECOND clip and at the default position it never fires — so "the panel is a fixed height" was the wrong picture. Measured: canvas 956 px tall, panel top 648 (the default position is `parentH - panelHeight - 64`, deliberately leaving only the scale-bar clearance below it), and BOTH `.area.canvas` and `.app-shell` are `overflow: hidden` at 956. Dragging the description grabber out to 260 px put the panel bottom at 1093 — 137 px, including the whole GM Notes field, cut off — while `max-height: 70%` = 669 px was never reached. So the textarea WAS growing and the panel WAS following it; the result was thrown away by an ancestor. Fixed by giving the panel a real corner grip that sets a persisted `{w, h}` (`sse-starmap-info-size-v1`): the fields flex to fill it (Description grow 3, GM Notes 2, so the field that was too small takes most of the extra), the grip clamps the size to the canvas and pulls `pos` up/left so every pixel it adds is on screen, and size and position now share ONE clamp on mount — a 644 px panel dragged on a 956 px canvas reopens at 536 px, wholly inside, on a 560 px one. The per-textarea grabbers are gone (`resize: none`): two grabbers with different meanings was half the confusion, and the panel grip also widens, which the native one could not — 320 px was the other half of "about 20 words". Default panel is byte-for-byte unchanged; no size is stored until the grip is used. | fixed v2.1.307-beta |
| A18 | **Player view: a selected body's framing gradually retreated while time was running.** Reported 2026-07-31 — "the camera drifts away", "only if time is running", which is the whole diagnosis in five words: the body was moving and the camera was not. **Root cause, one line in `driveFocus` (`lib/holo/scene.ts`).** Once the focus ease expired, the FREE-HEADING (3D) branch did only `controls.target.lerp(desiredTarget, 0.08)` — it re-aimed at the body and left `camera.position` exactly where it stood, with a comment calling that "the natural hologram feel". Turning to track reads well for a second and then fails: a body orbiting AWAY from a fixed camera is further away every frame, so the shot silently retreated until the planet was a dot. Rotating in place cannot hold a distance; only travelling with the body can. Note the LOCKED-heading branch was always correct — it re-seats the camera at `target + heading * d` every frame — so this only ever affected rotatable 3D presets, which is every Holo Table. **Fix:** carry the target→camera offset with the body (`followOffset = camera.position - target`, copy the target onto the body, replace the camera at `target + followOffset`). Preserving the OFFSET is what keeps everything else working: dragging still orbits the body, the wheel still changes distance, the turntable still spins, and a re-click still re-frames through the ladder because that arms `focusDrive` and eases to the new distance above. The target is copied rather than lerped, matching the locked branch — a lerp lags a fast-moving moon off centre. **Verified live** on a broadcast player view (Altair IV focused, clock at 1 month/s): centre-frame ink held at 1131-1774 over ~11 simulated months with no downward trend, where the old behaviour collapsed it to ~12 as the body left frame. Confirmed visually by Alex on the same running view. | fixed v2.1.313-beta |
| A19 | **Player view at 1:1 scale AND 1:1 distances: small bodies far out jitter — the orbit line vibrates and a moon does not sit on it.** Reported 2026-07-31, using Pluto/Charon as the example, and explicitly flagged as edge-of-the-envelope: it needs BOTH dials at their honest end, which is the regime A9 opened up (v2.1.297-303). Not diagnosed yet. **Where to start.** The 2D orrery solved exactly this and the fix is documented — `SystemVisualizer` uses a **Floating Origin (relative camera)** pattern, doing its arithmetic in 64-bit JS relative to the current camera focus before handing coordinates to the renderer, precisely because large absolute distances lose precision in a 32-bit rendering matrix (see `docs/dev/ARCHITECTURE.md`). The holo scene has no equivalent: it compresses map/AU distances into scene units and then leaves three.js to build float32 matrices, so a body 40 AU out drawn at its true radius is a tiny object at a huge coordinate — the worst case for float32, and it would show up as exactly this, a vibrating orbit line and a satellite that will not stay glued to its primary. **Two candidates, and they are separable:** (a) float32 precision in the world/view matrices, which the floating-origin treatment fixes; (b) the true-scale screen-space floor (`updateTrueScaleFloor`, A9/v2.1.297+) resizing a sub-pixel body per frame, which could make the body and its orbit line disagree about where the centre is. Check (b) first — it is cheap to rule out by pausing the floor — before taking on (a), which is a real piece of work.  **CHEAP CANDIDATE RULED OUT, v2.1.316-beta — and by construction, not by sampling.** The A9 screen-space floor (`updateTrueScaleFloor`, `holo/scene.ts:1086`) writes `b.mesh.scale` and NOTHING else. Scaling a mesh about its own origin cannot translate its centre, so it cannot make a body and its orbit line disagree about where that centre is. Confirmed by checking every write to a body's position in the scene: they are all in `updatePositions` (:1866/:1868/:1871) and `updateSurfaceConstructs` (:1992), none of them in the floor. Nor can the floor cause the vibration: `k = minPx / pxR` sets the rendered radius to exactly `minPx`, so the pinned size is stable by construction rather than a feedback loop that could hunt. **So it is the expensive one.** The remaining candidate is float32 precision in the GPU's world/view matrices, which is exactly what a body 40 AU out drawn at its true radius provokes: a tiny object at a huge coordinate, where neighbouring vertices differ in the last bits a float32 has. That fits both symptoms at once — an orbit line whose vertices round differently frame to frame VIBRATES, and a moon placed by a separate per-frame path rounds differently again, so it will not sit on the line. It also fits the trigger: it needs BOTH dials at their honest end, because that is when scene coordinates get large while radii get tiny. **What the fix involves.** A Floating Origin, which the 2D orrery already does and documents — do the arithmetic in 64-bit JS RELATIVE TO THE CAMERA, and hand the renderer only small numbers (`ARCHITECTURE.md`, `SystemVisualizer`).
