In the holo that means rebasing the scene on the current focus: body positions, the static orbit-ring vertex buffers (which are built once in absolute scene units and would need rebuilding, or a per-object offset, when the origin moves), the belts, and anything else holding absolute coordinates. It is a real piece of work with a re-render cost, and it wants its own scoped session — NOT this batch.  **QUANTIFIED 2026-07-31 — the numbers say it outright.** At TRUE scale `compressScalar` is linear: `scene = GRID_RADIUS * r / rMax` = `12 * r / 39.5` for Sol, so Pluto sits at scene coordinate 12. float32 has a 24-bit mantissa, so the gap between representable values at magnitude 12 is `2^-20` = **9.54e-7 scene units**. Against that: Charon's orbit radius is 3.98e-5 scene units = **42 ULP** — a whole orbit quantised to about 42 steps per axis, which is why it will not sit smoothly on its line — and Pluto's own RADIUS is 2.41e-6 = **2.5 ULP**, i.e. the body is essentially inside the rounding error before the screen-space floor inflates it for display. Every camera nudge re-rounds, which is the vibration. It gets worse, not better, on a map whose outermost body is further out. **Rebasing fixes it by orders of magnitude:** with the origin on Pluto, Charon's coordinates are ~4e-5, where the ULP is 3.6e-12 — about **1e7 ULP of headroom** instead of 42. **Design notes for whoever takes it.** (1) There is ONE choke point: every absolute→scene conversion goes through `positionToScene` (`holo/scene.ts:725`, 8 call sites). (2) Rebase AFTER the compression, not before — `compressScalar` is a RADIAL, nonlinear map, so a translation does not commute with it; subtracting the focus's COMPRESSED scene position is a pure translation of the rendered space and composes cleanly. (3) Moon orbit rings are ALREADY safe: `buildMoonOrbitRing` stores vertices relative to the parent and the loop is positioned at the parent, so its numbers are small by construction. The absolute holders that would need re-emitting on a rebase are `buildOrbitRing` (heliocentric), the belts, and the grid. (4) The camera and `controls.target` rebase with everything else. **Verification it should be held to:** assert that Charon's RENDERED offset from Pluto matches the propagated offset at true scale — today it will not — and measure frame-to-frame stability of the orbit-line vertices across a slow camera move. Both are numeric, like C3's. **FIXED v2.1.319-beta — a floating origin, rebased on the camera's focus.** Root cause in a sentence: the heliocentric orbit rings and the grid are static float32 vertex buffers holding ABSOLUTE scene coordinates, and at true scale those are magnitude 12, where a float32 step is 9.5e-7 — so the line's SHAPE is quantised at that step, and the shader then adds that large vertex to a large, oppositely-signed modelView translation, a cancellation that re-rounds every time the camera moves. **A correction to the earlier diagnosis, and it changes what you should look at.** The MOON was never misplaced. three.js builds `modelViewMatrix` on the CPU in float64 and only then hands it to the GPU, so a body's centre arrives as a SMALL camera-space translation, accurate to ~1e-12; every body position in this scene is a float64 JS number all the way. So "a moon will not sit on its line" is the LINE being wrong, not the moon — which is also why the moon reads as steady while the line vibrates against it. The cheap candidate stays ruled out by construction: `updateTrueScaleFloor` writes `b.mesh.scale` and nothing else, re-confirmed in one pass over every write to a body's position. **Measured, against the prediction.** The float32 step at Pluto's scene coordinate 11.99 is 9.5367e-7 (2^-20), exactly as predicted. The Pluto-Charon SEPARATION is 3.98e-5 scene units = **41.7 ULP** — the predicted "42" is CONFIRMED, and it was quoting the pair separation; Charon's own orbit about the barycentre is 3.52e-5 = **36.9 ULP**. Pluto's radius is 2.41e-6 = **2.5 ULP**, also as predicted. Rebased, that same separation sits where the step is 3.64e-12: **1.09e7 ULP**, confirming "about 1e7". End to end: a vertex on the orbit line beside Pluto is drawn **3.8e-7 scene units** from where it belongs — 1.1% of Charon's whole orbit — and rebased it lands within **2.8e-7 OF** Charon's orbit. Over a slow 600-frame pan the absolute vertex holds only **148 distinct positions** and jumps a full ULP when it moves; that staircase IS the vibration. Rebased it holds 600, moving one camera-step per frame. **Where the notes left a choice — rebuild vs per-object offset — the answer is NEITHER.** An offset is the wrong instrument: it moves the object in float64 but leaves magnitude-12 numbers in the vertex buffer, so both the shape quantisation and the shader cancellation survive it untouched. A full rebuild re-propagates 1024 samples per ring, and for a belt would re-randomise the rubble field (`buildBeltBand` calls `Math.random`) — i.e. it would change how the scene LOOKS. The absolute holders instead keep a float64 MASTER copy of their vertices and re-emit from it: one array pass, the subtraction done in float64 and rounded once. The grid gets the same treatment — its outermost ring sits at rMax, which for Sol is Pluto's own distance, so it is as much a reference line as the orbits are — with its AU labels translated rather than re-made, since they are canvas textures. **Belts are the exception and stay ABSOLUTE.** Their rocks are baked host-relative and the group is re-anchored to the host's rendered position every frame, so they follow the rebase for free; putting them through the rebased choke point would count the origin twice. Moon rings, planetary rings and surface-locked constructs likewise need nothing — all three are placed from a parent's rendered position. **The policy.** Rebase once the camera's target has drifted more than 2000x the working distance from the origin. That multiplier holds the float32 error to 1.5e-4 of the view width (a fifth of a pixel at 1000 px), and it is also what keeps the whole thing invisible at readable scale: there the camera never comes closer than the controls' 0.05 floor, which puts the threshold at 100 scene units against an outermost body at 12 — so the origin never leaves zero and that end of both dials is bit-identical. Suppressed while a focus ease is in flight, so a fly-in re-emits once on arrival instead of on 48 consecutive frames. **What is NOT done.** The on-screen pass. The numbers live in `src/lib/holo/floatingOrigin.spec.ts` (15 assertions) and `npm run build` plus the full suite are green (only E1 fails), but the browser pane in the session that did the work could not composite frames, so nobody has yet LOOKED at Pluto/Charon at 1:1 scale and 1:1 distances, or panned slowly across the outer system. Read A22 before doing that — it explains a second reason the pair may not appear to sit on a line. | fixed v2.1.319-beta |
| A20 | **QUESTION, not a bug: the in-system ruler reports a PLAN-VIEW distance. Does that agree with what transit charges for the same trip?** The ruler has two modes and the rail button says so — `RailNav.svelte:129` reads "tap two bodies for the distance between them in AU" in the system view and "tap two stars or ships" on the starmap. A17 is the starmap half. This is the system half. **First written up as "the same shape as A17". It is NOT, and the correction matters.** A17 was a genuine amputation: the code intended a 3D answer, wired `zCounts()` correctly, and then lost z at the endpoint, so it returned a number nothing in the system agreed with. Here the planar answer is CONSISTENT with its surroundings. **Verified:** `SystemVisualizer.svelte:65-71` computes `rulerDistanceAU` as `Math.hypot(a.x - b.x, a.y - b.y)` from a `Map<string, { x, y }>` filled by `computeWorldPositions` — but that component references inclination NOWHERE AT ALL (zero occurrences), so the 2D orrery is a plan view uniformly and by design, not a 3D view that dropped a coordinate. `computeWorldPositions3D` (`physics/worldPositions.ts:111`, "inclination-aware… what the holo view uses") has exactly one consumer, `holo/scene.ts`, and the holo view has no ruler. Note also that orbital inclination has existed since day one, unlike starmap z which arrived with WS7 on 2026-07-30 — so a planar in-system ruler is far more likely to be a deliberate plan-view choice than an oversight. **The one thing that would make it a real bug is unresolved and needs checking:** whether the AU figure the ruler shows matches the distance the transit planner and journey durations actually charge for that pair. Neither transit nor journeys go through `computeWorldPositions` at all, so they derive distance somewhere else — find where, and compare on a genuinely inclined pair. If they disagree, the map contradicts itself the way A17 did and this becomes a fix; if they agree, this is correct as it stands and should be closed wont-fix with the evidence. **Do NOT wire this to `ignoreZForDistances`** — that is a STARMAP field described in interstellar terms in `systemDistance.ts`, and in-system inclination is not the same quantity as starmap depth. **Belongs with the in-system transit work, not the starmap batch.** **CLOSED 2026-08-03 — checked by the project owner directly, and it behaves.** Units were changed and depth was toggled off; the numbers moved and matched. So the ruler is consistent with the rest of the engine, and the "does transit agree" question this item was really asking is answered in the affirmative. The plan-view reading stands as designed. | verified by observation — no change needed |
| A21 | **Interstellar space has no depth: a ship stranded away from a system cannot record one.** Residual from A17, and a DATA-MODEL gap rather than a display one, which is why it was not fixed there. Two places store an interstellar position and both are 2D: a journey's POINT origin/destination (`ActiveJourney.fromX/fromY`, `toX/toY` — used when a course is replotted from where an adrift ship sits, or aimed at a stranded one) has no `fromZ/toZ`; and `AdriftConstruct` (`types.ts:644`) stores `x, y, vx, vy` and no z. So a ship stranded between the stars reads as being ON the reference plane, however deep the systems either side of it are, and a rescue course plotted to it measures to the plane. Everything downstream is already ready for the answer — `posZ`, `mapSeparation` and `ConstructPlacement` all handle z — so this is purely about recording it: add the fields, write them where the strand/replot happens, and carry them through serialisation. **Also open, and the reason a coasting drift has no `vz` today:** the fly-by slingshot deflects a coasting ship by rotating `vx/vy` through a planar angle (`flybyTurn`, `interstellar.ts`). Giving the drift an out-of-plane velocity means deciding what that deflection does in 3D, which is a transit-physics question. Until then a coasting ship holds the depth it had when it was stranded, which is at least continuous. **BANKED 2026-08-03, EXPLICITLY FOR THE INTERSTELLAR AUTOPILOT WORK.** The data-model gap is real but the case it serves — a ship adrift between stars, replotting from where it sits — only bites once ships routinely fly interstellar routes under their own control. Pick this up as part of that feature, not before; building a 3D interstellar position with nothing flying through it is a schema change in search of a user. **ONE HALF OF IT IS NOT BANKED AND HAS MOVED TO [[A41]]:** a ship shown ON an interstellar route should sit in the correct 3D place along that route. That is a RENDERING question rather than a data-model one — the route's endpoints have z, so a ship's position along it can be interpolated in 3D today without any schema change — and it is the natural companion to A41's work making routes run to the stars rather than to their plane projections. | captured |
| A22 | **A barycentre member's orbit ring is drawn about the scene ORIGIN, not about its barycentre.** Noticed while reading `buildOrbitRing` for A19; deliberately NOT fixed there (out of scope). `propagateState3D` returns a PARENT-relative position and `buildOrbitRing` hands it straight to the projector — right for a planet, whose parent is the star at the origin, but Pluto and Charon are members of a root-level barycentre, so their rings come out as loops of radius 4e-6 and 3.5e-5 scene units AT THE SUN, inside the star's own globe, instead of around their barycentre out at 39.5 AU. At the eyepiece that means the only line near Pluto is the BARYCENTRE's ring, which the pair straddle by design — so part of "a moon will not sit on its line" is a ring that was never drawn where it belongs. The moon rings already solve exactly this: `buildMoonOrbitRing` stores vertices in the parent's local frame and `updateOrbitRings` positions the loop at the parent each frame via `trackParentId`. A barycentre member wants the same, tracked against its barycentre's rendered point. **FIXED v2.1.320-beta, and it was the answer to the reported symptom too.** Each member now gets a ring about its barycentre's rendered point, stored in that point's local frame and tracked to it every frame. The two local-frame builders (moon, member) are now ONE function taking a per-sample radial rule: a ring has to be laid down with exactly the transform the body's own placement uses or the body will not sit on it, and keeping the two rules side by side is what stops them drifting. Barycentres carry a ring but no mesh, so their scene points are now kept alongside the bodies in `updatePositions` — which also means they move with a floating-origin rebase like everything else. Nested pairs fall out for free (no partner radius, so no clearance, so pure physics). | fixed v2.1.320-beta |
| A23 | **An orbit ring is a 1024-gon, and past a certain zoom you are inside one of its facets.** Measured while closing A19/A22, and the reason a framed Pluto appeared to sit off its line even after the floating origin removed the jitter. `ORBIT_SAMPLES` is 1024 for every heliocentric ring whatever its radius, so at Pluto's 12 scene units a single chord sags **5.6e-5 scene units** off the true ellipse — **1.4x the entire Pluto-Charon separation**. It is a RESOLUTION limit, not a precision one: the vertices are exactly right, the straight line between them is not. A22 makes it moot for the pair (their own rings are 3e5 times smaller, where 1024 samples sag 1.6e-10), but it still applies to ANY body zoomed in far enough against its own heliocentric ring — Earth framed at Luna's orbit is the next case up. Options, none costed: raise the sample count globally (4096 brings Pluto's sag to 0.09x the pair separation, at 4x the buffer and 4x the propagation on every `setSystem`, which is already the prime suspect for the random slowdowns); scale the count per ring from its radius; or re-emit the ring non-uniformly around the floating origin on a rebase, which now has somewhere natural to live since the rings already keep a float64 master and are re-emitted there. **FIXED v2.1.321-beta by the THIRD option, and confirmed on screen first — the kink was visible in the outer line beside Pluto.** Diagnosis sharpened: the visible artefact is the 0.35 degree TURN at each vertex, not the sag, because the line spans the whole screen; that is why raising the count cannot win (the kink alone wants ~16k samples at that zoom, and deeper zoom wants more again). The same 1024 samples are now redistributed by a monotonic cubic warp of the orbit parameter, packed into the arc nearest the floating origin. The centre density is SOLVED from the tolerance — chord sag within camDist/2000, sub-pixel at any zoom — rather than picked, so it is correct at 1e-5 and at 1e-2 alike. Costs: the ring to refine is found by scanning the existing float64 master (no propagation), and only a ring passing within 8 camera-distances is refined, so in practice exactly ONE re-propagates per rebase. Re-emitted on rebase and on any zoom past 1.6x, and never called at all while the origin is at zero, so readable scale is untouched. The far side degrades to ~1 degree per vertex at the deepest zooms, which is only ever when it is also 24 scene units away and subtends nothing. **REOPENED AND ACTUALLY FIXED v2.1.322-beta — the v2.1.321 refinement was correct and unreachable.** Its gate asked whether the ring passed within 8 camera-distances of the focus and answered with the distance to the nearest VERTEX of the uniform master. On a 1024-gon at 12 scene units the vertices are 0.074 apart, so a ring passing exactly THROUGH the focus reports 0.037 — **46x over the threshold** — and every ring silently took the uniform path. It now measures the distance to the nearest SEGMENT (the two meeting at that vertex), which is what the question meant; the vertex scan stays, because it fixes the orbit parameter the refinement is centred on. A LESSON worth keeping: a proximity test against a sampled curve must be against its segments, never its samples, or the sampling pitch becomes the floor. Second fault found while proving the first: the dense arc was centred on the floating ORIGIN, which only re-latches after the target has drifted 2000 camera-distances (0.2 scene units at camDist 1e-4) against a dense arc 0.15 wide — so a followed body walked out of its own refinement. The refinement now follows the CAMERA and the rebase follows precision, and it re-emits once the focus crosses a quarter of the arc. | fixed v2.1.322-beta |
| A24 | **Constructs had no orbit lines on the player view.** Reported 2026-07-31 while checking A19/A22, with the ask that they be a lighter colour so they do not read as worlds. They were excluded from the ring branch outright. **FIXED v2.1.321-beta**: a construct now takes whichever branch fits it (heliocentric, parent-local, barycentre-local) in its own `icon_color` lightened 45 percent toward white — and neutral grey under the `white` body-colour selection, which exists so a screen filter sees one palette. Two exclusions remain, both deliberate: a construct carrying `scheduled_journeys` is placed by the transit sampler rather than by its orbit, so a ring from that orbit would be a line it is not on; and a surface-locked one has no orbit to draw, tested per frame rather than at build time because the lock is live. A construct also contributes no radius to a moon ring's clearance, matching its own placement (`radiusScene` is 0 for one). | fixed v2.1.321-beta |
| A25 | **A companion STAR is listed under a heading that says "Moons".** Found by the A6 sweep (v2.1.324); pre-existing, deliberately NOT fixed there to keep that change to what was reported. On a barycentre's page `guideDocument.ts` builds `moonRow = [...companions, ...moons]` and heads the whole thing "Moons" — so the Alpha Centauri AB Barycentre page reads "Moons: ★ Toliman (Alpha Centauri B)", a main-sequence K star filed as a moon, glyph and all. The same block also catches CIRCUMBINARY PLANETS, because `membersOf` matches `n.parentId === bary.id` as well as `memberIds` — confirmed on a synthetic P-type fixture, where both planets land under "Moons" too. **Note it is only wrong for STAR barycentres: for Pluto-Charon the companion genuinely is a moon and the heading is right**, which is why this wants a content-derived heading rather than a rename. Same shape as the fix A6 already applied to its own new block (heading chosen from what is in the list), so the pattern to copy is three lines above it. Related: [[A6]]. **FIXED v2.1.327-beta. DECIDED: two blocks with honest headings, not one heading covering both** — `Companion star` / `Companion stars`, then `Moons`. **The reason one shared heading was rejected is not tidiness, it is that no honest name exists for that list.** It can hold a main-sequence star, a genuine moon (Charon) and a circumbinary PLANET all at once, because `membersOf` matches `parentId === bary.id` as well as `memberIds`. Any single heading would be wrong for two of the three. **So the split is by WHAT EACH NODE IS, not by which query produced it:** a star goes to Companions, a moon joins the Moons list, and a planet is left out of both so the A6 orbiters block files it under Planets. That one change fixes the circumbinary half of this entry as a side effect rather than needing its own pass. **Verified on all three shapes:** the Alpha Centauri AB Barycentre now reads "Companion star: Toliman (Alpha Centauri B)"; Pluto-Charon still reads "Moons: Charon", which is the case the entry flagged as already correct and which a blanket rename would have broken; and on the synthetic P-type fixture both circumbinary planets move out of Moons and into Planets. Also re-checked live on the fixed Struve 2398 and Groombridge 34, where the barycentre pages now read "Companion star" instead of listing a planet as a member ([[D3]]). Related: [[A6]], [[A26]]. | fixed v2.1.327-beta |
| A26 | **A ringed planet's RINGS appear in no drill-in list.** Found by the A6 sweep (v2.1.324). A ring is `roleHint: 'ring'`, so `moonsOf` (which requires `roleHint === 'moon'`) excludes it, and the planet is not a star or a barycentre so A6's new orbiters block does not run for it either — Jupiter's page lists Io, Europa, Ganymede and Callisto and never mentions Jupiter's Rings, although the ring is a real node with its own facts page and IS reachable from the star's list, since `listBodiesOf` includes belts and rings. So the ring is orphaned only from its own parent's page. Low value on its own; it is really the same question as A25 — what a body's satellite list should CONTAIN and be CALLED — and the two should be scoped together. Related: [[A6]], [[A25]]. **FIXED v2.1.327-beta, alongside A25 as that note suggested. CORRECTION to this entry: the ring was NOT reachable from the star's list either.** That claim assumed `listBodiesOf` would pick it up, but `orbiters()` matches on the HOST, and all four Solar System ring nodes are parented to their PLANET, not to the Sun — checked in the shipped data. So a ring was reachable from nowhere in the document at all, which makes this worth more than the entry credited it with. **DECIDED: a ring ROW, in its own `Rings` block.** The entry's hesitation was fair — a ring is not somewhere you drill INTO the way a moon is — but it is a real node with its own facts page (radii, temperature, composition), and the star's list has always offered BELTS on exactly that basis. Treating a ring differently from a belt would be the inconsistency, not the row. **Implementation is a widening, not a new block:** A6's orbiters query now runs for ANY subject rather than only stars and barycentres, since `orbiters()` already excludes moons and stars and never returns constructs — so for a planet it yields precisely its rings. The heading is composed from content (`Planets`, `Belts`, `Rings`, or a joined pair), so a page never announces something it is not showing. **Verified:** Jupiter reads "Moons: Io, Europa, Ganymede, Callisto" then "Rings: Jupiter's Rings"; Mars, which has no rings, is unchanged. Related: [[A6]], [[A25]]. | fixed v2.1.327-beta |
| A27 | **QUESTION, raised by A2 (v2.1.325) and deliberately not answered: is `cargoDescription` player-safe?** It is a free-text manifest the GM writes — Blip-A's reads "Xenonite hull stock, tools for every conceivable emergency, a large supply of hot high-pressure ammonia, and one grieving, brilliant engineer" — and 24 of the campaign's 26 constructs carry one. **The reason it is a question and not an obvious yes:** every other narrative field has its own redaction lever and this one has none. `description` is player-visible but can be withheld per node via `description_playerhidden`; `gmNotes` is stripped outright. `cargoDescription` has neither, so including it would make a ship's manifest unconditionally public the moment the construct itself is visible — and "what is in that hold" is exactly the sort of thing a GM reveals on a roll rather than on a panel. The A2 block therefore prints the cargo TONNAGE (observable from outside, and already reaching players) and leaves the prose out. **Three ways it could go, and it is a design call rather than a bug:** (a) show it, treating a manifest as public; (b) give it a `cargoDescription_playerhidden` flag of its own and default to showing it; (c) treat it as GM-only and strip it in `computePlayerSnapshot` alongside `gmNotes` — worth noting the snapshot does NOT strip it today, so it is already crossing the wire even though nothing renders it. **DECIDED 2026-07-31: option (c), GM-ONLY — strip it.** Reasoning, and it is worth keeping because it generalises: a STAR CATALOGUE would not know a ship's cargo, fuel or supply reserves — only its CAPACITY. The catalogue is a reference work, not a live telemetry feed. Strip `cargoDescription` in `computePlayerSnapshot` alongside `gmNotes`, which also closes the existing wire leak. "We can always complicate later" — if a GM later wants to reveal one, that is a `cargoDescription_playerhidden` flag added then, not now. **See A29: the same principle indicts three rows that already shipped.** Related: [[A2]], [[A29]]. **DONE v2.1.328-beta, option (c) as decided.** `cargoDescription` is now deleted in `computePlayerSnapshot` (`system/utils.ts`, re-exported from `api.ts`) on the same line as `gmNotes`, so it never leaves the GM's machine. **Note what this actually closed:** the field was already crossing the wire — nothing rendered it, so it read as a display question, but the prose for all 24 constructs that carry one was in every broadcast. This is a leak fix, not only a policy decision. **No `cargoDescription_playerhidden` flag was added**, per the decision: a GM who later wants to reveal one gets a flag then. Related: [[A2]], [[A29]]. | fixed v2.1.328-beta |
| A29 | **The construct block prints CURRENT LEVELS, not just capacity — and by A27's own reasoning it should not.** Raised 2026-07-31 when deciding A27, and it applies to code that SHIPPED in v2.1.325, so it is a live player-visibility question rather than a design musing. A27 was settled with the principle "a star catalogue would not know cargo or fuel or supply reserves, only capacity". That indicts three rows of the new construct block, not just the cargo prose. **Verified in `catalogue/bodyFacts.ts`:** `Crew` prints "current of max" (`:106`), `Cargo` prints "current of capacity t" (`:121-123`), and `Fuel` prints "current volume of capacity m³" (`:134-137`). Each shows a live reading a reference work would not hold — how loaded a hold is, how much range a ship has left, how many hands are aboard. **The counter-argument to weigh before stripping:** a player looking at their OWN ship would reasonably know all three, so a blanket strip may be too blunt; the honest split might be that capacity is catalogue data and current level is telemetry, with telemetry shown only for constructs the viewing player owns or is aboard. That needs the ownership/visibility model checked before it can be built — find out what `computePlayerSnapshot` already knows about who is looking. **Do not guess: get the decision before changing shipped behaviour**, and note that whichever way it goes, `Total mass` (`:124`) is derived FROM the current fuel and cargo, so it leaks the same reading indirectly and has to move with them. **DIRECTION SET 2026-07-31: gate them behind a Player View preset toggle, not a strip.** Proposed name "Show temp info", rejected because "temp" reads as TEMPERATURE in an app full of temperatures. **Use "Live readings"**, with the sub-line "Fuel, cargo and crew as they are now, not just capacity" — a *reading* is taken now, a *specification* is permanent, and the name extends cleanly to anything transient added later (position, heading, damage). It belongs with the other presentation booleans in `player/presetTypes.ts` (`auroras`, `unlit`, `starmapRouteGlow` etc.), so a "star catalogue" preset has it off and a "ship's console" preset has it on — same data, different document. **KNOW WHAT THIS BUYS, because two different boundaries are in play.** The SNAPSHOT decides what LEAVES (`computePlayerSnapshot`, `src/lib/api.ts`) — it already deletes `gmNotes` (:75/:86/:99), honours `description_playerhidden` (:78) and `object_playerhidden` (:43), and strips `scheduled_journeys`, `draft_transit_plan`, `classification` and `aiContext` outright (:113-116), so the codebase already treats forward-looking tactical data as never-leaves. The PRESET decides only what is SHOWN, on data already sitting on the player's machine. A preset toggle is therefore COSMETIC, not secure: the figures still cross the wire and anyone reading the raw broadcast has them. That is acceptable here because the stated intent is "a star catalogue would not ASSERT this", not "a player must never obtain it" — but it should be a knowing choice, and if the answer ever changes to the latter, current fuel and cargo belong beside `draft_transit_plan` in the snapshot strip rather than behind a checkbox. **CONFIRMED 2026-07-31, and the trade-off was accepted knowingly: build it as a PRESENTATION toggle.** The figures continuing to cross the wire is a KNOWN and ACCEPTED consequence, not an oversight — "if they hack the data stream, well, that is live role playing, well done them". **So do not harden this into a snapshot strip without being asked.** A future session finding live readings in the broadcast will be tempted to treat it as the same fault A27 closed; it is not, and that would silently take a decision that has already been taken the other way. `Total mass` (`:124`) is derived from current fuel and cargo, so it moves with them either way. Related: [[A27]], [[A2]]. **BUILT v2.1.329-beta as specified — a `liveReadings` boolean beside `auroras` and `starmapRouteGlow` in `presetTypes.ts`, labelled "Live readings" with the sub-line "Fuel, cargo and crew as they are now, not just capacity."** **The LABEL changes with the setting, not just the value.** Off reads `Crew capacity` / `Cargo capacity` / `Fuel capacity`; on reads `Crew` / `Cargo` / `Fuel` as current-of-capacity. Printing a bare number under an unchanged label would have left the reader guessing which figure they were looking at — the same reasoning that produced `Power surplus` vs `Power output` in A2. **Two more rows had to follow than the direction named, and this is the one judgement call worth reviewing.** `Total mass` was already identified. But `Max acceleration` divides thrust by the CURRENT wet mass, and `Δv (vacuum)` is the log of the wet/dry ratio — i.e. literally how much fuel is left. Leaving them on would have meant the block withheld the fuel reading in one row and restated it in the next, which is the "quietly wrong" pattern A5 and A2 both turned on. Both are therefore gated too, and `Supplies remaining` with them. **The cost is real and is not hidden:** with the setting off there is no Δv figure at all, where a catalogue would reasonably want the RATED Δv at full tanks. That figure is not derived anywhere today and inventing it here would break the read-don't-recompute rule — see [[A31]]. **Defaults, chosen by what each preset IS:** ON for `datapad` ("a hand-held data terminal readout") and `console` ("a ship-console orbital plot") — the two that are instruments; OFF for `guide`, `crt`, `holo` and `projection`, and OFF as the schema default so a stored preset picks it up. This DOES change shipped behaviour: v2.1.325 showed live readings everywhere, and The Guide now shows capacity. That is the correction this item asked for. **The accepted trade-off was honoured, not quietly re-litigated: NOTHING was added to `computePlayerSnapshot`.** Current fuel, cargo and crew still cross the wire, by decision. (`cargoDescription` IS now stripped, but that is [[A27]] and a different question — prose with no hide flag, versus figures with an accepted one.) **Verified on both consumers, off and on:** `FilteredDocumentView` with it off shows Rocinante as Cargo capacity 200 t / Fuel capacity 3,500 m³, and `DocPanel` with it on shows Crew 4 of 6, Cargo 40 of 200 t, Total mass 1,259 t, Fuel 2,820 of 3,500 m³, Max acceleration 3.24 g, Δv 15,842.7 km/s. Blip-A checked the same way against the bundled map. Related: [[A27]], [[A2]], [[A31]]. | fixed v2.1.329-beta |
| A30 | **What SHOULD a construct look like in the info block?** Raised by A28 (v2.1.328), which removed the wrong picture — a ship was being illustrated with a planet's sphere — and deliberately did not choose a right one. So a construct now has a blank where every body has a graphic, which is honest but plain. **The material already exists, which is why this is a decision rather than an art job:** `bodyGlyph` gives a construct `◆`; every construct carries an authored `icon_type` and `icon_color` that the 2D starmap and the holo scene BOTH already draw it with; and the class string is structured enough to distinguish a station from a frigate from a derelict ("Station/Colony/Spin", "Ship/Frigate/Corvette"). **Options:** (a) leave it blank — a stat block needs no portrait; (b) draw the construct's own `icon_type`/`icon_color` at info-block size, which makes the panel agree with the marker the player is looking at on the map, and costs nothing new in data; (c) silhouettes by hull class, which is real artwork and a rule-pack asset question. (b) is the obvious middle and the only one that reuses what is already authored. **Constraint, and it has come back twice:** whatever is chosen belongs to the INFO BLOCK only and must never reach the system map. A GM-uploaded `image` must keep winning over any of it. Related: [[A28]], [[A2]]. **G3's design investigation (2026-08-01, `docs/dev/ship-appearance-design.md`) ANSWERS the long-term question but does NOT close this: the cheap interim should proceed NOW, independent of whether the 3D work ever happens.** Verdict from that doc: build option (b) — the `icon_type`/`icon_color` glyph at info-block size, the exact `getConstructIconTexture` vocabulary, so the panel agrees with the marker on the map — as a small self-contained block. It is not throwaway: it remains the permanent fallback tier under the eventual priority chain photo > 3D model > glyph > nothing. Option (c), silhouettes by hull class, is REJECTED in that doc with evidence (class strings are free-form — "Sensor Platform", "Warship w/Fighter Wing" — so the mapping is a fragile keyword heuristic feeding bespoke artwork). Related: [[G3]]. **BUILT v2.1.343-beta — option (b), exactly as `docs/dev/ship-appearance-design.md` §8 scoped it.** A construct now draws its own authored `icon_type` in its `icon_color` at info-block size, so the panel shows the same mark as the marker on the map. New `constructGlyph` block, pushed by `guideDocument` for a construct under the same imagery modes that give a world its disc — so 'none' still means none, and a GM-uploaded photo still wins (that branch sits above and was left alone). **It is drawn INTO the document canvas rather than reserving a gap like `bodyDisc` does,** because a flat vector shape needs no live renderer overlaid: one case in `renderDocument` and BOTH consumers get it with no plumbing of their own, and it picks up the page's filter and colouration like every other mark. Under a mono theme it takes the page ink instead of its authored colour, as the bleached page requires. **DECIDED, and it is a decision the doc left open: the glyph is capped in absolute px (84) as well as by its band.** Uncapped it filled the document's body-picture slot at ~120 px, and a flat grey square that size reads as a MISSING IMAGE rather than as an emblem — the opposite of the honesty A28 was after. **The duplicate-code rule was honoured as far as the session's bounds allowed:** the shape vocabulary is now a shared `src/lib/constructs/constructIcon.ts` (`constructIconShape` + `traceConstructIcon`) rather than a fourth private copy. THREE OLDER COPIES REMAIN — `holo/scene.ts` (`getConstructIconTexture`), `components/Starmap.svelte` (`iconPath`, as SVG) and `components/SystemVisualizer.svelte` — and all three were explicitly out of bounds for this session. Written up unnumbered at the bottom for the coordinator. **The standing rule was respected and checked, not assumed: nothing was added to any map surface.** The new block exists only in the document builder; `DocPanel`'s body-graphic overlay is keyed on the `__bodygfx` region id, which the glyph deliberately does NOT carry, so no construct graphic can reach the map through it. **G3 was NOT built toward** — no model loading, no `ModelRef`, no new data. The glyph is the permanent bottom tier of that doc's ladder (photo > model > glyph > nothing), so it is not thrown away when models arrive. **Verified live on both consumers with the bundled Sol:** `DocPanel` beside the 2D map and `FilteredDocumentView` as a full document both draw the International Space Station's grey square, and Earth still draws its own sphere. Unit test added covering shape, colour, the 'triangle' fallback for an unset `icon_type`, 'none' drawing nothing and a photo outranking it. Related: [[A28]], [[A2]], [[G3]]. | fixed v2.1.343-beta |
| A31 | **A construct has no RATED performance figure — only its performance right now.** Raised by A29 (v2.1.329). `calculateFullConstructSpecs` computes `maxVacuumG` from the CURRENT wet mass and `totalVacuumDeltaV_ms` from the current wet/dry ratio, so both are readings of how loaded and how fuelled the ship is at this instant. With "Live readings" off there is therefore no acceleration or Δv row at all — which is honest, but a catalogue entry for a ship would certainly quote a Δv, and quoting the current one under a capacity heading would be exactly the fault A29 exists to fix. **What is missing is a FULL-TANKS figure:** Δv at `fuelCapacity`, and the acceleration band from full to empty. **Neither would be new physics** — `ConstructDerivedSpecs.svelte` (~:89-92) already derives an accel range across `fuelCapacity_tonnes` for the GM panel, so the arithmetic exists; it is not on `ConstructSpecs`, so the document layer cannot read it without recomputing, and A2's standing rule is that this layer READS derived values rather than deriving its own. **The fix is therefore in `construct-logic.ts`, not in the document:** add rated `deltaV`/`accel` fields to `ConstructSpecs` next to the current ones, then the capacity view can print a rated Δv and the GM panel can stop computing its range inline. Small, but it crosses out of the document layer, which is why A29 stopped short. Related: [[A29]], [[A2]]. **FIXED v2.1.345-beta, in `construct-logic.ts` as the entry directed — not in the document.** `ConstructSpecs` now carries `ratedVacuumDeltaV_ms`, `ratedAccelFullG` and `ratedAccelEmptyG`, computed inside `calculateFullConstructSpecs` beside the current-state pair, so ONE function owns the quantity. With "Live readings" OFF the block prints `Δv (rated, full tanks)` and `Acceleration (rated) — a-b g, full to empty`; with it ON the current `Δv (vacuum)` and `Max acceleration` return, unchanged. The LABEL says which is on show, per A29's own precedent. **THE DECISION WORTH REVIEWING: the rated figures use an EMPTY HOLD, not the current cargo.** Two reasons, and the first is a leak. Capacity mode already prints Dry mass AND Fuel capacity, so a rated Δv computed with the current cargo aboard lets a reader solve straight back to the cargo A29 deliberately withheld — the block would be withholding a reading in one row and handing it over in the next, which is exactly the "quietly wrong" pattern A29 exists to prevent. Second, it is simply what rated MEANS: a property of the ship, not of today's manifest. There is a test that pins it — loading the hold to capacity must not move the rated Δv by a digit. **THE GM PANEL NOW READS THE FIGURE INSTEAD OF COMPUTING IT.** `ConstructDerivedSpecs.svelte` was scaling `maxVacuumG` by mass inline to build its full→empty band; that was a second derivation of what the spec sheet now publishes, and two sums of one quantity drifting apart is precisely how [[B8]] happened. **KNOW THIS BEFORE COMPARING SCREENSHOTS: the GM's band CHANGED MEANING slightly** — it used to carry the current cargo in both ends and is now the ship's rated envelope with an empty hold, so a laden ship's band reads a little higher than before. That is deliberate; a "rated" prefix was added to the panel, and a `Rated Δv (full tanks)` row beside the current one. **A formatting fault surfaced and was fixed with it:** `toFixed(2)` printed the whole band of an ion-drive station as "0.00–0.00 g". Accelerations under 1 g now use two significant figures keyed on the SMALLER end, so the Lunar Gateway reads "0.000048–0.0011 g, full to empty" rather than two zeroes. **Verified live in capacity mode on the holo preset panel and in the full document:** Lunar Gateway shows Acceleration (rated) 0.000048–0.0011 g and Δv (rated, full tanks) 78.5 km/s; the ISS, which has no thrust, correctly shows neither. Two tests added. Related: [[A29]], [[A2]], [[B8]]. **FOLLOW-UP v2.1.346-beta, on direct instruction: the whole construct block was re-gated by ONE rule — a row is a live reading if it changes minute to minute, and a specification if it does not.** **CONFIRMED, not changed: capacity mode already showed `Cargo capacity` and `Fuel capacity` as capacities** — checked on screen and now pinned by a test, because it was the first thing asked about. **`Status` is now live-only.** `flight_state` is literally the ship's state; a reference work does not say where a vessel is this afternoon. **`Location` follows the same rule but only bites when it has to:** a berth — an orbit, a dock, a landing — stays in capacity mode, because a standing assignment is the kind of thing a catalogue lists. A construct UNDER WAY has no berth and its `orbit_string` is telemetry ("Deep Space (v=12.3 km/s)"), so the row is absent rather than quoting a speed under a capacity heading. **`Manifest` (`cargoDescription`) is back, live-only — and this REVERSES a shipped [[A27]] decision, so read it before re-stripping anything.** A27 deleted the prose from `computePlayerSnapshot` outright, reasoning that a star catalogue would not know what is in a hold. That reasoning turned out to be the exact statement the "Live readings" toggle now makes, so the question was a display one after all. The delete is gone from `system/utils.ts` and the row rides beside the cargo TONNAGE it describes. **The consequence is A29's, taken knowingly on the same terms: the prose crosses the wire whatever the preset says.** Do not reinstate the strip without being asked. **`Route` is new, live-only.** A ship's route is a reading of its intent. Only the autopilot PLAN survives to a player — the starmap snapshot strips `scheduled_journeys` and `draft_transit_plan` — so this is the route it flies, not the schedule it flies it on, which is what a watcher could infer anyway. Legs render as verbs with named places ("Mine water ice → Luna (looping)"); `FactContext` gained an optional `system` so a leg can name its target instead of printing an id, and a leg it cannot name is described by its verb rather than dropped. **Ruled out as specifications, so they stay in both modes:** Type, Dimensions, Dry mass, Spin gravity, Power surplus/output, Aerobraking, Orbital period (a property of the orbit, not of the moment), Tags. **NOT SCREEN-VERIFIED, and this is the gap to close: no construct in either bundled map carries a `cargoDescription` OR an autopilot route,** so the Manifest and Route rows are covered by tests with exact expected strings but have not been seen live. To check them: give a construct a cargo description in its Basics tab and a one-leg route in its Autopilot tab, then open a `datapad` or `console` player view. The capacity-side gating (capacities present, Status absent, berth kept) WAS confirmed on screen. Six more tests. | fixed v2.1.345-346-beta |
| A28 | **A construct is drawn as a planet's disc in the info block.** Noticed while verifying A2 (v2.1.325). The body-graphics setting ('3D sphere' / 'Simple disc' / 'Flat shape') is applied to whatever is selected, so Blip-A — a 110 m ship — gets a featureless orange sphere above its stat block, the same graphic a rocky world would get. `guideDocument` pushes a `bodyDisc` block for any subject when imagery is disc/sphere/flat, with no test for `kind === 'construct'`. Pre-existing and NOT touched by A2, which changed only the facts. **Note the constraint before fixing it:** the construct already has a glyph (`bodyGlyph` gives it `◆`) and an `icon_type`/`icon_color` pair the starmap and the holo scene both use to draw it, so the honest picture already exists in data — this is about the info block reaching for the wrong one, not about inventing artwork. A GM-uploaded `image` on the construct should still win, as it does now. Related: [[A2]]. **FIXED v2.1.328-beta. DECIDED: draw NOTHING for a construct, and do not invent a graphic for it here.** Root cause exactly as scoped — the `bodyDisc` branch tested only the imagery mode, never the kind, so a ship took a world's illustration. One added condition. **The reasoning for stopping at nothing rather than substituting a glyph:** the wrong picture is worse than no picture, so removing it is the whole of the reported fault; choosing what a construct SHOULD look like is a design question with real options (the `◆` glyph, the `icon_type`/`icon_color` pair the starmap and holo scene already use, a silhouette by hull class) and inventing one mid-fix is how a look ends up in code instead of in data. Raised as [[A30]]. **The GM-uploaded photo path is untouched and was checked, not assumed:** it is a separate branch above, gated on a loaded image, so a construct with a picture still shows it and only the procedural-body branch is refused. **The standing rule was respected:** this REMOVES a block from the info-block builder and adds nothing anywhere, so no construct graphic goes near the system map. **Verified on both consumers and both ways round:** Blip-A produces no `bodyDisc` block with imagery set to 'disc' while Jupiter still does, and live in `DocPanel` the Rocinante panel runs heading straight into facts with no sphere. Related: [[A2]], [[A30]]. | fixed v2.1.328-beta |
| A17 | **THE MEASURE TOOL HAS NEVER COUNTED DEPTH. It reports the planar distance whatever the campaign setting says.** Found by the A15 regression check, NOT caused by it — this predates A8 and A12 and has been wrong since WS7 introduced z on 2026-07-30. **Evidence:** Vega (depth +16 ly) to Zeta Reticuli (depth -35 ly) reads **34.17 ly**, identical with "ignore depth" on and off. The pair is 51 ly apart in depth ALONE, so no 3D separation between them can be below 51; the honest figure is about `sqrt(34.17² + 51²) ≈ 61 ly`. A GM measuring that pair is being told roughly HALF the distance the routes and journey durations for the same pair would use. **Root cause, and it is not where you would look.** The plumbing is right: `measureDist` (`Starmap.svelte:692`) already calls `systemSeparation(mA, mB, pixelsPerUnit, !zCounts(starmap))`, so the flag is wired correctly and the 3D branch does run. The endpoints are what is wrong — `measurePick(x, y, label, constructId)` (`:673`) takes only x and y, and the call site passes `s.position.x, s.position.y` (`:704`), dropping `s.position.z`. `posZ()` then reads both endpoints' absent z as 0, so the "3D" calculation returns the planar answer and the flag can change nothing. This is why the setting appears to work everywhere else: routes and journeys pass whole `position` objects. **Fix is small but not one line, and it needs its own verification:** add z to `MeasureEnd`, pass `s.position.z` at `:704`, and carry z through `resolveMeasure` (`:681`) for construct endpoints — which means `constructDisplayPlacement` has to supply a z for transit/adrift points too, or ship measurements stay planar while system measurements go 3D, which would be worse than the honest bug. Deliberately NOT fixed inside the A15 check: changing what a measurement REPORTS changes what the campaign counts, so it wants its own commit and its own before/after. **FIXED v2.1.314-beta. Four numbers, same pair, both zooms: depth counting 60.99 / 60.99 ly, depth ignored 34.17 / 34.17 ly.** The arithmetic checks out independently — `60.99² − 34.17² = 2552`, so Δz ≈ 50.5, which is what the two labels round to at ±1 (they print 16 and −35 via `Math.round`). **What was ruled out:** the flag, the predicate and the module were all innocent. `measureDist` already passed `!zCounts(starmap)`, `systemSeparation`'s 3D branch always ran, and `posZ` did exactly what it documents. The coordinate was amputated one level above all of them, at the pick. **The second caller, decided:** a CONSTRUCT endpoint needs no z at pick time, because `resolveMeasure` re-derives its whole position from the clock every frame — anything stored at the pick is overwritten immediately. So the ship's depth had to come from the placement instead, and `ConstructPlacement` now carries it wherever it is EXACTLY derivable: resting in a system it is that system's z; in transit it is the same lerp as x and y, because an interstellar hop is a straight line and `frac` was already on the placement. **Two honest gaps left, deliberately, and both are data-model not display:** a POINT origin/destination (`fromX/fromY`, `toX/toY`) has no `fromZ/toZ` in the journey schema, and `AdriftConstruct` stores only x/y — so a ship stranded in interstellar space has no recorded depth and reads as being on the plane. Also NOT invented: a `vz` for a coasting drift, because the slingshot deflection is modelled as a planar rotation of `vx/vy` and giving it an out-of-plane component is a transit-physics decision, not a display one. A coasting ship holds the depth it had when stranded. See A21. **Pinned by a test at the level the bug lived** (`Starmap.spec.ts`, "measure tool depth (A17)"): it drives the real path — ruler on, tap two stars, read the label — on a fixture 40 apart in plan and 9 in depth, so the answers are exactly 41 and 40. A unit test of `systemSeparation` would NOT have caught this, since that module was never wrong; confirmed by reverting the call site, which fails the test with `expected '40 ly' to be '41 ly'`. | fixed v2.1.314-beta |
| A14 | **What should the 3D starmap do when the campaign ignores depth?** Raised by A12, deliberately left unanswered there. A12 drops the depth cues from the 2D GM starmap on the grounds that annotating a number which affects no distance is noise. The same argument does not obviously carry to the 3D view, because in 3D the depth is not an annotation — it is the geometry, and the whole reason for the view. Three coherent answers: (a) leave it, and treat "ignore depth" as a MEASUREMENT setting only, which is exactly what `systemDistance.ts` says it is ("`ignoreZ` is the campaign's own choice… so an existing map keeps the distances it always had") — the 3D view then honestly shows a layout whose separations are not what the campaign counts; (b) flatten the 3D view to the plane when the flag is on, which makes the two views agree but throws away the placement work and arguably makes the 3D view pointless for that campaign; (c) keep the geometry but suppress the numeric read-outs, the same line A12 drew in 2D. Not a bug; a decision about what the setting means. Worth deciding before someone "fixes" the 3D view to match the 2D one. **DECIDED 2026-07-31: option (c).** Keep the geometry — bodies stay where they were placed, because that is the entire point of the 3D view — and suppress the numeric depth READ-OUTS, which is exactly the line A12 drew in 2D. The reasoning is then consistent across both views: the placement is real and stays visible, the number that affects no distance is not asserted. Option (b) is explicitly rejected: flattening throws away placement work. Implement via `zCounts()` (`src/lib/map/systemDistance.ts:22`), never by reading the flag directly. **BUILT 2026-07-31 at v2.1.312 — and the decision turned out to need NO behaviour change, which is a result rather than a dodge.** Both halves of option (c) were already true, and this is a proof rather than a sample. **Geometry is kept:** `Starmap3DView.svelte:53` passes `z` through unconditionally, and nothing in the 3D path can do otherwise — `src/lib/starmap/**` and the catalogue route never import `systemDistance`, never call `zCounts()` and never reference `ignoreZForDistances`; the single mention in `starmapScene.ts:201` is a comment saying distances live elsewhere. The flag cannot reach the scene, so option (b) was never a risk here. **There is no numeric depth read-out to suppress:** the scene draws exactly three kinds of text — system name (`starmapScene.ts:586`), route name (`:603`) and the grid distance rings (`:384`), and the rings come from `spanMap = max(maxX-minX, maxY-minY)` (`:532`), which is planar and never sees z. A search of the whole tree for a signed depth figure finds exactly two, both the 2D GM cues A12 already handles; `position.z` has only three consumers in `src/` (`Starmap.svelte`, `map/rebase.ts`, `map/systemDistance.ts`), so no document, panel or player surface prints a depth anywhere. **NO `zCounts()` call was added, deliberately:** a guard on a read-out that does not exist would be dead code asserting a rule it does not enforce. What WAS added is the durable part — a comment at the `z` pass-through recording the A14 decision and its reasoning, at the one line where a future session could break it by "making 3D match 2D". Note also that there is no GM 3D starmap at all: `Starmap3DView` is mounted only by the player catalogue and the preset-editor preview. | verified v2.1.312-beta |
| A16 | **LANDED — one shared handling for the floating chrome (picker + time controls).** Sanctioned follow-on from A11, not drift: A11 gave the body picker a floating puck, and the time control already had one, so the two were doing the same job twice. Being unified into shared components — `FloatGrip.svelte`, `FloatPin.svelte` and a new `src/lib/ui/` — with `TimeControls.svelte` losing the bulk of its own implementation and `BodyPicker.svelte` adopting the shared one. Recorded here because at the time of writing it is UNCOMMITTED and partly UNTRACKED, so it exists only in a working tree; a future session finding `FloatGrip` with no board entry would have no idea what it was for. **Note the blast radius before finishing it:** `TimeControls` is mounted on the GM starmap, the GM system view and the player catalogue, so this is a shared-component change reaching a player surface — the same trap A11 carried. Land it as its own commit with `npm run build` green, and verify the picker AND the time control on both a GM view and a player view before moving on. Related: [[A11]], [[A13]] — the third floating panel is `StarmapInfoPanel`, which has its own drag/persist implementation and is the obvious next thing this should absorb, if it does not already. **Shipped: the picker and the time transport are now ONE floating control (v2.1.311), and a control that would open off an edge pushes back on rather than overflowing (v2.1.312).** The entry is KEPT rather than deleted now that it is done, because it is the only place that says why `FloatGrip.svelte`, `FloatPin.svelte` and `src/lib/ui/` exist — a future session finding them with no board entry is exactly the confusion this was written to prevent. **CORRECTION to the blast radius above: `TimeControls.svelte` is NOT mounted on the player catalogue.** It has exactly TWO mounts, both GM — `Starmap.svelte:1449` and `SystemView.svelte:2278`. The player catalogue has its own separate, much smaller time puck (`.time-controls` at `catalogue/+page.svelte:1068`: a play/pause button, a rate slider and `use:clickOutside`), which this change did not touch and which still works. The player surface IS reached by this batch, but through `BodyPicker`, not `TimeControls` — so the A11 trap is real and the verification still had to happen, just on the picker. **Verification done on every surface (2026-07-31, at v2.1.312).** GM starmap: both controls, drag / lock / auto-close / open-rightwards. GM SYSTEM view (the mount that had not been exercised): unlock puts the pill away to its clock, dragging the clock moves it 180/-60 without counting as the tap that opens it, tapping reopens with the grip back and wholly on screen, and touching the orrery canvas closes it again. Player catalogue: puck at left 10, strip opens rightwards from exactly 10 to 430 with grip + pin + a working list, auto-closes to the puck on touching the scene, and the catalogue's own time puck still expands. **`StarmapInfoPanel` is deliberately NOT absorbed, and this is the reason.** It is not the same kind of object. The floating control models a puck that hides itself and remembers a POSITION; the info panel is a persistent notes panel that remembers a position AND A SIZE (A13), anchors by absolute `left/top` rather than an offset from a host anchor, is dragged by its whole header rather than a grip, and must NOT auto-close — auto-hiding notes you are typing into would be a bug. Absorbing it would mean either extending the shared control with a size model only one consumer wants, or leaving it half-converted with position shared and size bespoke. The second is worse than what is there now, and the first risks re-breaking A13, which was fixed hours earlier and is the panel that was actually complained about. Left alone on purpose; revisit only if a THIRD panel wants a persisted size, at which point the size model earns its place. | fixed v2.1.311-312-beta |
| A15 | **Regression check: does the MEASURE TOOL still work after A8 and A12?** Raised while deciding A14, not a reported fault — a check that has to be done before the batch is trusted. Both changes touched it from different directions. A8 (v2.1.310) made the measure read-out hold a constant screen size under zoom, so it now divides by `labelK` like the other labels — a read-out that renders at the wrong size, in the wrong place, or not at all would be that change. A12 (v2.1.309) hides depth cues when the campaign ignores depth, and the measure tool is precisely where depth is meant to STOP counting rather than stop being shown: `zCounts()` governs whether a measured distance is planar or 3D, and A12 must not have altered the arithmetic — only the annotation. **Verify both readings:** measure between two systems with the flag OFF (depth counts, the figure should be the 3D separation) and ON (planar), at more than one zoom level, and confirm the read-out is legible and correctly placed at each. If the figure moved when only the ANNOTATION should have, that is a real bug in A12 and it is more serious than anything A12 fixed. **DONE 2026-07-31 at v2.1.312. A8 and A12 are both CLEAN — and the check found a much older bug underneath, now A17.** Measured Vega (depth +16 ly) to Zeta Reticuli (depth -35 ly) on the Science Fiction map, four readings: flag OFF at zoom 0.397 → **34.17 ly**; flag OFF at zoom 1.234 → **34.17 ly**; flag ON at zoom 1.234 → **34.17 ly**; flag ON at zoom 0.397 → **34.17 ly**. **A8 verdict: clean.** Across a 3.1x zoom range the read-out is pixel-identical (17.33 x 56.21), stays centred on the line midpoint, and holds the same 5 px gap above it — legible and correctly placed at both ends. **A12 verdict: clean, and exonerated by the same numbers.** The figure did not move when the flag was toggled, which is what A12 promised. The depth ANNOTATIONS behaved: 41 cues → 0 → 41 across the toggle, with the measure read-out unaffected throughout. **But the figure not moving is itself the finding.** It should have moved, and 34.17 ly is impossible for that pair: the two systems are 51 ly apart in depth alone, so no 3D separation between them can be less than 51. The tool has never counted depth — see A17. Neither A8 nor A12 caused it: `measurePick` and its call site last changed on 2026-06-16 (`f626123`, `f5c6e72`), before WS7 added z at all, and neither of this batch's commits touches them. | verified v2.1.312-beta |
| A32 | **Changing the info-panel width does not take effect unless the player window is ALREADY OPEN when you save — and the top third of the slider does nothing at all.** Reported 2026-08-01: "it ONLY updates if I have the window open already and edit and save it — maybe it is scaling of old window rather than current size". The instinct is right, and there are THREE faults in five lines, only one of which was reported. **VERIFIED, all in `src/routes/catalogue/+page.svelte`.** **(1) THE CONVERSION IS ONE-SHOT.** `applyPlayerPreset(p)` (`:350`) converts the stored fraction to pixels at `:357` — `inspectorWidth = Math.max(200, Math.min(640, Math.round(p.inspectorWidthPct * window.innerWidth)))` — and that is the ONLY place it happens. It is never recomputed on resize, so the pixel width is frozen against whatever the viewport was when the preset was last applied. That is precisely why editing and saving with the window open works: it re-runs `applyPlayerPreset` against the current size. A window opened later, or resized after, keeps a stale figure. **(2) THE CLAMP KILLS THE TOP OF THE SLIDER, and this is [[F10]] RECURRING IN MIRROR IMAGE.** The slider's range is 0.15 to 0.50 (`PlayerPresetEditor.svelte:541`) but the live view clamps to `min(640, ...)`. On a 1920 px display 50% is 960 px, clamped to 640 px = 33%, so roughly the top third of the travel moves nothing. F10 fixed exactly this fault in the PREVIEW, which was drawing a raw pixel figure capped at 340 — nobody checked the LIVE view, which caps at 640. Whatever is decided, the editor's range and the live clamp must agree, or the slider is lying again. **(3) TWO SOURCES OF TRUTH FOR ONE NUMBER.** `inspectorWidth` is ALSO seeded from `localStorage['holo-insp-width']` (`:186-187`) and written by the drag handle (`:191-196`) as a PIXEL value, while the preset stores a FRACTION. Last writer wins and the two do not mean the same thing — a width dragged on one screen is a different proportion on another, which is the very problem F10 moved to fractions to solve. Decide which owns the value; the F10 reasoning says the FRACTION should, with the drag writing back a fraction rather than pixels. **The proper fix makes the pixel width a DERIVED, REACTIVE quantity** — a function of the stored fraction and the CURRENT viewport, recomputed on resize — rather than a variable assigned once at apply time. Related: [[F10]], [[A13]]. **FIXED v2.1.342-beta — all three, plus a fourth the verification turned up, and there was a FIFTH mechanism nobody had named.** Root cause exactly as scoped for (1)(2)(3), and the proper fix was taken: `inspectorWidth` is now a REACTIVE DERIVATION of a stored fraction and the live viewport (`$: inspectorWidth = clamp(pct × viewportW)`), with `<svelte:window bind:innerWidth>` feeding it, so it is right on a window opened later and tracks a resize. **THE FIFTH MECHANISM, and it is the one worth reading: making it reactive was not enough, because SVELTE ORDERS `$:` STATEMENTS BY THE ASSIGNMENTS IT CAN SEE.** `applyPlayerPreset` is called FROM a reactive block, so a write to `inspectorWidthPct` buried inside that function cannot order the derivation that reads it — the derivation had already run for that cycle and was never marked dirty again. Measured live: the panel sat at the local default (342 px = 0.18 × 1898) while the preset asked for 0.26, i.e. the preset's width reached nothing at all. The adoption therefore happens IN the reactive block, in plain sight, with a comment saying why it must stay there. This is A32's own fault — a one-way write nobody re-reads — in a second costume, and it would have shipped looking fixed. **(2) The clamp is gone and the ranges agree:** bounds are `INSP_MIN_PCT`/`INSP_MAX_PCT` = 0.15/0.50, character-for-character the editor's slider range, with a 200 px readability floor that is itself capped at half the viewport so it can never exceed the maximum on a narrow screen. Confirmed at the top of the travel: 50% on an 1898 px display gives **949 px**, where the old `min(640, ...)` gave 640 (34%). **(3) DECIDED: the FRACTION owns the value, and the drag writes a fraction.** New key `holo-insp-width-pct` (the old `holo-insp-width` pixel key is abandoned, not migrated — beta). **The tie-break needed one more distinction than the triage names, and this is the judgement call to review:** a stored drag wins on the FIRST application in a window, so a width the reader dragged survives a reload; every LATER application — the GM editing or swapping the preset live — wins over it, because that is what deploying a preset is for. Preset-always-wins would have meant a dragged width never surviving a reload; drag-always-wins would have meant a GM could never set it. **(4) FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, fixed in the same commit: the drag handle was `display: none` at every width.** `.insp-resize { display: none }` sits AFTER the `@media (min-width: 720px)` rule that un-hides it, at equal specificity, so the later rule won everywhere — the panel edge could not be dragged at all. Now `.inspector .insp-resize`. Without it fault (3) has no way to be exercised, which is presumably why it went unnoticed. **Verified live on a 1898 px display, GM + player window side by side, every case:** width changed with the player window SHUT then opened → 949 px (50%); resize → tracks (1500 → 390 px = 26%, 760 → 200 px floor); drag → stores `0.3462` (a proportion, not pixels) and reload restores 759 px = 40%; GM edits the preset to 20% with the window OPEN → 380 px, so the deploy still lands. Note the harness caveat for the next session: a CDP viewport override does NOT emit a `resize` event, so the resize case has to be driven by dispatching one. **RULED OUT:** nothing was changed in `PlayerPresetEditor` — its slider and preview were already right after F10, and the live view was the only side that disagreed. Related: [[F10]], [[A13]]. | fixed v2.1.342-beta |
| A33 | **Two differently-scaled radiation numbers with similar names sit near each other, and the small one reads as a bug.** Reported 2026-08-01 as "radiation is super high on Io but lowish on Mars". **Mars is not low — it is very nearly exact.** `surfaceRadiation` for Mars is **213.69 mSv/yr** against the ~210 mSv/yr Curiosity's RAD instrument measures on the martian surface, i.e. within about 2%. The figure that looks wrong is `irradiationDose: 0.574`, which is a DIFFERENT QUANTITY — `radiation.ts:8` describes it as the cumulative high-energy dose a surface has taken, RELATIVE, with Earth-unshielded-young about 1. It is a surface-ageing ratio, not an annual dose, and it has no business being read on the same scale as a mSv/yr figure. **The rest of the column is right too, which is why the pairing is the fault rather than the physics:** Earth 2.30 mSv/yr against ~2.4 background, Venus 2.0 under 92 bar, Mercury 3,500 unshielded and close in, Phobos 223.9 consistent with Mars beside it. **This is the same fault as [[A5]]** — two numbers describing related but different things, adjacent, with nothing in the presentation to say they are on different scales. A5 was a mean sitting outside its own range; this is a ratio sitting beside a dose. Fix it the same way: make the block say what each number IS. Options are to label the units explicitly, to move the relative figure away from the absolute one, or to drop `irradiationDose` from the reader-facing block entirely if its only real consumer is the ageing model. **Check what else in that block is a ratio rather than a measurement while you are there** — the A5 sweep found [[B8]] by doing exactly that. Related: [[A5]], [[B8]], [[B20]]. **FIXED v2.1.344-beta as a LABELLING change. NEITHER NUMBER WAS TOUCHED, and the triage's premise turned out to be half wrong in a way that matters.** **`irradiationDose` IS NOT IN THE READER-FACING BLOCK AT ALL, and never was** — checked before removing anything, as the entry asked. Its only consumers are `planetAppearance.ts` (tholins and regolith greying) and the GM's physics trace. So there was nothing to drop. **What the reader actually sees of it is its BUCKET, as the `surface/irradiation` tag, in the same block as the dose rows, under the label "Irradiation".** That is the fault the report describes, one layer down from where it was looked for. **Io is the proof the two are different quantities:** constantly resurfaced, so its `irradiationDose` is 0.000 and the tag reads LOW, printed inches from a surface-radiation row of 36 Sv/DAY. **DECIDED: rename the label to "Space weathering"** (the tag KEY `surface/irradiation` is untouched — it is serialised) and say in its description that it is a cumulative total relative to a young unshielded Earth, not a dose per year, so it does not compare with the rows above. **THE SWEEP FOUND TWO MORE, and the first is worse than the reported one.** **(a) The radiation rows themselves were on two scales, side by side.** The mean-only path auto-scaled to Sv/y or Sv/day, but the range path printed raw mSv/y whatever the magnitude AND SHOWED NO MEAN — so Earth read "high (229.6–229.6 mSv/y)" on one line and "high (843 Sv/y)" on the next: two doses a thousandfold apart, told apart by a suffix on one of them, with no absolute figure on the first row. Io's range came out as an eight-digit millisievert figure. Now one helper picks the unit ONCE from the mean, prints the mean WITH that unit, and prints the range in the same unit and precision — Mars "high · 214 mSv/y (178–258)", Io "high · 36 Sv/day (35–37)", Earth "high · 230 mSv/y" (a range whose ends round alike is suppressed rather than printed as "230–230"). **(b) An INDEX printed as a percentage one row above a real coverage percentage:** "Habitability 83%" against "Native life present (cover 80%)", with nothing to say the first was a score rather than a fraction of the surface. Now "Habitability score — 83 / 100"; the biosphere coverage keeps its % because it IS one. **RULED OUT, with reasons, so the next sweep need not redo it:** Density (`×Earth` in the value), Mass (`M⊕`), Gravity (`g`) — all ratios, all wearing their unit; Eccentricity (dimensionless and named); Air mix and Surface liquid/ice (genuine fractions of a real total); Magnetosphere, Ascent Δv, Orbital period, Radius (measurements with units). **B27 CAN LAND CLEANLY: the two radiation LABELS were deliberately left alone** — `Radiation (surface)` / `Radiation (at 1 bar)` / `Radiation (in orbit)` are B22's wording and B27's decision is about which place the second one names. Only the VALUE formatting changed, and it is one helper (`radRow`) both rows call, so renaming or re-pointing either row is a label edit with nothing else to follow. **Verified on both consumers** (`DocPanel` beside the 2D map and `FilteredDocumentView` as a full document), on Earth, Mars and Io. Six tests added in a new `bodyFacts.spec.ts`. **New, unnumbered at the bottom: the bundled Sol's Earth reads 229.6 mSv/yr at the surface where the generated fixture asserts 2.0–3.0 for the same world.** That is a data or physics divergence, not a display one, and it is not this session's to chase. Related: [[A5]], [[B8]], [[B20]], [[B27]]. | fixed v2.1.344-beta |
| A36 | **The player view's collapsed time button LOOKS like pause but only opens the speed window.** Reported 2026-08-01: "hitting the pause opens the speed window — it should also pause". **VERIFIED, one line.** `src/routes/catalogue/+page.svelte:1133`, the COLLAPSED state of the time control: the button renders `{isPlaying ? '❚❚' : '▶'}` — a pause glyph while running — but its handler is `on:click={() => (timeExpanded = true)}` and nothing else. The EXPANDED state's button (`:1129`) does toggle `isPlaying` correctly. So the control shows a pause icon that does not pause, which is worse than showing no icon. **The ask is that it does both: toggle AND expand.** That stays consistent, because the expanded button then toggles back. The alternative — leaving the behaviour and changing the collapsed glyph to something that does not promise pausing — is worse, since pausing from collapsed is one tap and is obviously what a player wants. Note the control is hidden while following the GM and on non-interactive presets (`presetInteractive && !followGMActive`), so this only affects a player driving their own clock. **FIXED v2.1.367-beta.** One line, exactly as scoped: the collapsed button now toggles isPlaying AND sets timeExpanded, so it does what its glyph promises and the expanded button beside it toggles back. Its aria-label now says which it will do rather than the generic "Time controls". | fixed v2.1.367-beta |
| A37 | **The SQUARES grid does not work at all on the player 3D starmap.** Reported 2026-08-01 alongside [[G4]]. Hex and traveller-hex render; squares do not. **IDENTIFY THE SURFACE FIRST — there are at least two grid implementations and possibly three, and this is the same trap [[A1]] set.** (1) `components/Grid.svelte` is the 2D SVG grid, typed `'grid' | 'hex' | 'traveller-hex' | 'none'`, with the square branch at `:19` and `:173`. (2) `holo/scene.ts:974` carries its own WS3 lattice overlays described as "square / hex / traveller-hex on the system's GROUND PLANE" — a different surface again. (3) The report is specifically about Player Views → starmap → 3D map, which may be neither of those. Work out which one the player 3D starmap actually draws before touching any of them; fixing the wrong square branch will look like a fix and change nothing. Note also that the preset editor's preview builds its grid type at `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte:19` from `travellerMode ? 'traveller-hex' : gridType`, so a preview that works while the live view does not would point at that line rather than at the renderer. **SURFACE CONFIRMED 2026-08-01: it is the 3D view of the STARMAP** — `src/lib/starmap/Starmap3DView.svelte` over `src/lib/starmap/starmapScene.ts`. Neither `components/Grid.svelte` nor the `holo/scene.ts` ground-plane lattice is involved; ignore both. **And the squares ARE implemented, so this is a plumbing fault rather than a missing renderer.** `renderMapGrid` handles `cfg.type === 'grid'` at `starmapScene.ts:317`, hex and traveller-hex at `:325`, and traveller hex labels at `:351`. **TWO SUSPECTS, both visible from the outside.** (1) **A NAMING MISMATCH:** `:380` reads `renderMapGrid(base, gridMode === 'square' ? 'grid' : gridMode, ...)` — two vocabularies for one concept, `'square'` in `MapOverlay` and `'grid'` in the map-grid config, translated in one place and therefore untranslated everywhere else. That is the classic shape for a type that silently falls through. (2) **TWO OVERLAPPING GRID SYSTEMS ON ONE VIEW:** `Starmap3DView.svelte` takes BOTH `grid: 'off' | 'plain' | 'scaled' | 'hex'` (`:13`, the preset's own dial, which has NO square option at all) AND `mapGrid: { type: 'grid' | 'hex' | 'traveller-hex' | 'none' }` (`:16`, the GM's snap-grid, fed to `controller.setMapGrid`). Establish which one the user is actually setting when they choose squares, and whether the two are fighting. The right end state is probably ONE vocabulary rather than a translation at `:380`. **PLUS A SECOND, LARGER REQUIREMENT ADDED 2026-08-01: every grid style must exist on the 2D starmap too.** `src/lib/starmap/Starmap2DView.svelte` has NO grid handling of any kind — the search for `mapGrid` or `grid` in it returns nothing. So this is not a styling gap on 2D, it is an absent feature, and it is the larger half of this item. Same styles, same vocabulary, same data driving both, so a GM switching between the two views sees the same lattice. **Do this BEFORE [[G4]]** - there is no point styling a grid that does not draw, and G4's falloff is meant to apply to squares. **FIXED v2.1.371-beta. ROOT CAUSE IN A SENTENCE: the square branch ran, built its geometry and drew nothing, because `addLattice` fades PER VERTEX and every square line spanned the whole lattice - so BOTH of its endpoints lay outside the fade radius and every single line was culled.** The arithmetic is exact and needs no browser: GRID_RADIUS is 12, the lattice half-extent is 12 x 2.4 = 28.8, the fade ends at 12 x 1.9 = 22.8. A square line runs the full width at some z, so its endpoints sit at a distance of at least 28.8 - beyond 22.8 - and `addLattice` drops any segment whose both ends have faded out. Hex edges are ONE HEX WIDE and always have an endpoint near their own centre, which is exactly why hex looked fine and squares looked unimplemented. **WHAT WAS RULED OUT, both of the item's own suspects.** (a) The naming mismatch at `:380` was REAL and is fixed, but it was not the fault - `gridMode === 'square' ? 'grid' : gridMode` translated correctly, so the square branch was being reached every time. (b) The two grid systems are NOT fighting: `grid` is the overlay CHOICE and `mapGrid` is the GM's snap-grid supplying cell SIZE and alignment only. Both now say so at the prop. Also ruled out, as the brief instructed: `components/Grid.svelte` and `holo/scene.ts`'s ground lattice were never touched. **2a - WHICH VOCABULARY WON: `MapOverlay`, which already existed.** WS3 built it (`src/lib/map/mapOverlay.ts`) with `normaliseOverlay()` folding every persisted spelling - `grid`, `none`, `travellerHex` - into the canonical set. `renderMapGrid` now speaks it and the inline translation is gone. `Starmap3DView`'s own `grid` prop was typed as the legacy four (`off|plain|scaled|hex`) and so did not admit `square` or `traveller-hex` AT ALL, while the preset field feeding it is typed `MapOverlay`, which has both. Also closed in passing: the else-branch drew hexes for ANY non-square type, including `none`. **2b - AND THE GEOMETRY IS NOW ONE GENERATOR: `src/lib/map/latticeGeometry.ts`.** Square and hex, in map space, unit-agnostic, with `travellerHexLabel` and `hexCentres` alongside so a label can never sit in a hex the lattice did not draw. It takes a `maxSegment` option documented with the reason it exists: a caller that fades per vertex MUST segment, or it judges a whole line by its far ends. **Proved numerically rather than looked at** - `latticeGeometry.spec.ts` asserts that an unsegmented square lattice has ZERO edges surviving the real fade radius and a segmented one has many, with hex unaffected either way. Seven tests. **HEX AND TRAVELLER-HEX RENDER UNCHANGED, and that took care:** squares take the THINNED cell (drawn per grid line, so a dense map can be skipped through) while hexes keep the true cell, because thinning a hex lattice moves the centres a system is snapped to. The density bail and the label offset are preserved deliberately. **2c - THE PREMISE OF THE 2D HALF IS WRONG, AND CHECKING IT WAS THE WHOLE OF THAT STAGE. `Starmap2DView.svelte` has no grid handling because NOTHING MOUNTS IT.** It is imported once, in `catalogue/+page.svelte`, and rendered nowhere. **The player's 2D starmap is `Starmap3DView` with `flat` set** - the same renderer locked overhead at `angleDeg` 0 - and the page says so in as many words at `:1021`: "3D (or 2D = the same renderer LOCKED OVERHEAD)". `flat` pins the tilt and re-runs `setData`; it does not touch `rebuildGrid`. **So the two views are not two implementations agreeing by luck, they are ONE implementation, and 2b fixed both at once** - a stronger parity than the item asked for. Adding grid styles to `Starmap2DView` would have been work that could never appear on screen, which is the trap this very item warned about one level up. Dead import removed, component annotated so the next reader is not misled the same way; NOT deleted, because whether it is a discarded prototype or an intended lighter renderer is not this session's call.
