Recorded unnumbered. **THE HOOK FOR [[G4]], since 2a left an obvious one and the brief asked me to say so:** every G4 feature is now a property of ONE generator rather than three. Brightness falloff already exists as `addLattice`'s `fadeFrom`/`fadeTo` and is the mechanism this bug turned on - it just needs exposing. Thicker subsector edges want a per-edge WEIGHT on `LatticeEdge`, which is one field on the returned tuple. Hex numbering is already factored out as `travellerHexLabel` + `hexCentres`. And "the 3D hex look for all types" is now literally a matter of routing `square` through the same styling as `hex`, since both come from one function. **NOT BUILT - G4 remains open.** **SEEN ON SCREEN 2026-08-02, AND THREE THINGS CAME BACK - all fixed at v2.1.373-beta.** **(1) Traveller hex was indistinguishable from plain hex.** Two causes, and the second is the one that mattered. The numbering gate was `hd >= 0.5` SCENE units, and with GRID_RADIUS 12 that means a hex has to be about a twenty-fourth of the whole field before a number appears - above a typical bundled map, so the numbers simply never drew. Lowered to 0.22: still a legibility gate, set where a number is readable rather than where it is comfortable. **But numbering alone was never going to be enough, because it only shows when the hexes are large.** What makes a Traveller map READ as one at any zoom is the SUBSECTOR GRID, and that did not exist in the 3D renderer at all - only in the GM's 2D `Grid.svelte`. Added to the shared generator (`subsectorLattice`) on Grid.svelte's own rule, `absCol % 8` and `absRow % 10`, **including the ZIG-ZAG vertical edge**: a hex lattice has no straight vertical line, and drawing one is the giveaway that a map was made by somebody who had not looked at a real sector. Drawn as RIBBONS with a true world-space width, because THREE ignores line thickness on nearly every platform - "thicker" has to be geometry, not a parameter. **(2) THE LATTICE IS NOW FLAT BY DEFAULT.** Every edge dropped a depth curtain ALWAYS. That reads as depth on a tilted 3D map and as fuzz on a flat one - and since the "2D" starmap is this same renderer locked overhead (see 2c), it was paying for a cue it can never show. **(3) The curtain is now a CHECKBOX, "Grid depth", offered only on the 3D starmap view** (`starmapGridDepth`, default off): the grid line at full intensity with the curtain fading away beneath it. Suppressed automatically in `flat` mode as well as by the preset, so the overhead view cannot get it by accident. **This is the part of [[G4]] that the reported faults forced - said plainly rather than pretended otherwise.** G4 lists "thicker subsector edges" and "hex numbering" among its wants, and both are now built, because without them the two hex types are the same picture and the item's own "verify hex and traveller-hex still render unchanged" could not be answered. **What remains G4 and was NOT built: brightness falloff as a user control** (the mechanism exists as `addLattice`'s `fadeFrom`/`fadeTo` and is what this bug turned on - it needs exposing, not writing), **the 3D hex look offered for all types**, and **hex numbering styling**. **CONFIRMED ON SCREEN 2026-08-02.** Squares, hex and Traveller hex all render as intended, the subsector boundaries read as continuous lines, and the numbering fault found in that pass is recorded under [[G4]]. Nothing outstanding on this item. And a finding for whoever picks up the preset editor, recorded unnumbered: the starmap overlay picker and the system overlay picker both `bind:value={draft.grid}` - one preset field, two dropdowns, offering different option sets. | fixed v2.1.371-beta - needs a human eye |
| A38 | **The 3D body graphic in the DOCUMENT ignores the view filter — a plain white sphere on a green CRT page.** Reported 2026-08-02 with a screenshot of Epsilon Eridani III in the console/CRT preset: every glyph is green phosphor with scanlines and noise, and the body renders as an unfiltered white low-poly globe sitting on top of it. **The mechanism is visible in the code's own reasoning.** `catalogue/document/bodyImage.ts:3-4` restricts photos to same-origin URLs because "a cross-origin image would taint **the WebGL surface the filter reads from**" — so the document is drawn into a canvas which WebGL then filters, and ANYTHING DRAWN INTO THAT CANVAS IS FILTERED BY CONSTRUCTION. The sphere is not, which points at it being rendered SEPARATELY and composited OVER the filtered result rather than into it. Confirm that before fixing: find where the `bodyDisc` block in `mode: 'sphere'` is actually produced and whether its output joins the document canvas or is layered above it. **THE FIX IS TO GET IT INSIDE THE PASS, NOT TO RE-TINT IT.** Do not approximate the filter on the sphere with a CSS filter or a colour multiply — that is a second implementation of the filter which will drift from the real one, and the preview-versus-live drift that shared resolvers exist to prevent has already bitten in [[F9]]. **ALSO REQUESTED: CHECK THE OTHER MODES DO RESPECT IT.** Photo, simple disc and flat shape are all 2D draws into the document canvas and should therefore be filtered already — verify rather than assume, and say which you checked. Note the side PANEL takes a different route from the full document: `catalogue/+page.svelte:903` applies `cssFilterApprox(...)` as a CSS `filter` on the `.inspector` aside plus tint and scanline overlay divs (`:904-906`), i.e. an APPROXIMATION, while the full `FilteredDocumentView` uses the real shader. So a body graphic may be filtered on one surface and not the other, and both need checking — that pair has produced two separate faults already ([[F7]], [[F9]]). Related: [[F9]], [[A30]]. **FIXED v2.1.367-beta, and the item's assumption about the other modes was WRONG - which is why it was right to ask.** **Mechanism confirmed before touching anything, exactly where the item predicted.** renderDocument.ts:292 (case 'bodyDisc') reserves a TRANSPARENT GAP and says so in its own comment: "It is NOT drawn into the filtered canvas - the caller overlays the REAL renderer here". FilteredDocumentView then positions BodyGraphic as an absolutely-placed SIBLING of the filtered canvas (.fd-bodygfx), so it was composited over the filtered result and never saw the shader. **THE OTHER MODES, checked rather than assumed, and the answer splits three ways.** **PHOTO is genuinely inside the pass** - it is drawn into the canvas as an image block, and bodyImage.ts's same-origin restriction exists precisely because WebGL reads that canvas. **SPHERE, FLAT and SIMPLE-DISC are ALL overlays**, not just the sphere. Flat and disc merely LOOKED right because BodyGraphic already receives the document's mono/theme colours and the page background, so they were tinted to match without ever being filtered - no scanlines, no barrel warp, no noise on any of them. The reporter noticed the sphere only because a 3D render with its own portrait lighting comes out white instead of ink-coloured. **THE FIX GETS THEM INSIDE THE PASS, and re-tints nothing.** holo/scene.ts gains preserveDrawingBuffer: true - without it a WebGL canvas captured outside its own render callback comes back blank; BodyGraphic exposes getCanvas(); and FilteredDocumentView composites document + graphic into the texture the filter reads, EVERY FRAME, because the body spins and a single upload would freeze it mid-turn. The DOM copy stays mounted at opacity 0 - still rendering, still hit-testable, so drag-to-spin survives - which is the same trick the holo HUD card already uses to keep the inspector's buttons while the shader draws the card. **NOT CAPTURED, and recorded rather than bodged: the SIMPLE-DISC mode is an inline SVG with no canvas to capture**, so it keeps the overlay. A CSS filter on it would be a second implementation of the shader, which is exactly the [[F9]] failure this item warns against. **BOTH SURFACES:** the full document (FilteredDocumentView) is fixed. The side PANEL takes the other route entirely and is unchanged - see [[A39]], which establishes that its canvas is CSS-approximated along with its chrome on the non-holo tiers, and takes the REAL shader on the holo tier via the HUD card. **NOT VERIFIED ON SCREEN - see the handback list.** The Browser pane does not composite in this environment, so requestAnimationFrame never fires, and a per-frame capture is precisely what cannot be tested without compositing. The strongest available evidence is internal precedent: filteredCanvas.ts:24 already relies on the same preserveDrawingBuffer capture, deliberately and with a comment, so the technique is already in production in this repo. | fixed v2.1.367-beta - needs a human eye |
| A39 | **Does the info panel still need the CSS filter approximation now that its content is a canvas?** Asked 2026-08-02 while scoping [[A38]] — "nothing should be using the CSS approximation any more, are they?" **The answer is no, it is not obsolete, but the question lands on something real.** `player/cssFilterApprox.ts:1-4` states its purpose: a CSS approximation of the GPU filters for DOM-RENDERED surfaces, driven by the SAME `filterParams` as the shaders so one preset reads consistently, and explicitly not pixel-identical (barrel warp and the signal-artifact effects have no cheap CSS analogue and are skipped). That is legitimate and not debt: a shader can only filter what is rendered into a texture, and DOM chrome cannot go through GLSL without a capture step. It is also ONE source of truth rendered two ways rather than a rival implementation, which is what distinguishes it from the [[F9]] failure. **Two things in it ARE worth chasing.** (1) The comment marks cover and starmap as INTERIM, "until their true-GLSL capture path lands" — that path has not landed and nothing tracks it. (2) **The better question: the INFO PANEL is listed as a DOM surface, but since D6 unified the info panels onto the document engine, `DocPanel` renders its content to a CANVAS.** `catalogue/+page.svelte:607` and `:903` still apply `cssFilterApprox` to the wrapping `.inspector` aside with tint and scanline overlay divs at `:904-906`, i.e. treating the whole thing as DOM. If its interior is now a canvas, it may be able to take the REAL shader and stop approximating — which would also close the panel-versus-document split that has produced [[F7]], [[F9]] and now [[A38]]. Establish what is actually DOM in that aside (chrome and controls, presumably) versus what is canvas, and whether the two can be filtered separately. **Do this WITH or BEFORE [[A38]]** — both are about which surfaces reach the real filter pass, and answering them separately risks two different answers. Related: [[A38]], [[F9]], [[F7]]. **ANSWERED v2.1.367-beta. NO, IT IS NOT OBSOLETE - AND THE REAL-SHADER PATH FOR THE PANEL ALREADY EXISTS, GATED.** **What is actually in that aside, established rather than assumed:** DOM chrome (the title button, the resize handle, the action buttons) AND a canvas - DocPanel renders the whole document body to one canvas (DocPanel.svelte:132), and it carries no filter of its own. The CSS filter on the .inspector aside therefore covers both, which is why the approximation works and why it cannot simply be deleted: a shader can only wreck what is in a texture, and the chrome is not. **But the panel interior CAN take the real shader, and on one tier it already does.** catalogue/+page.svelte:644 computes hudCardOn = effectiveSystemTier === 'holo' && selectedBody && presetFilterActive && hudW >= 720 && !hideInfoPanel, and when it is true the card is drawn to a canvas and composited into the holo render as a HUD quad - the REAL GLSL - while inspFx is bypassed (!hudCardOn guards the filter, the tint div and the scanline div) and the DOM inspector is kept underneath, invisible, purely for its buttons. So the pattern is built, proven and shipping; what is missing is the same route on the NON-holo tiers (text-list / 2D), where DocPanel's canvas is still CSS-approximated along with its chrome. **That is the honest state, and it is not debt in the way the question implied:** one source of truth (filterParams) rendered two ways, with a real-shader path already taken wherever the surface can support it. **Extending it to the other tiers is real work, deliberately not started here** - it needs a compositing target on tiers that have no holo render to composite into. **The INTERIM note is now tracked rather than orphaned:** cssFilterApprox.ts marks cover and starmap as interim "until their true-GLSL capture path lands". That path has not landed; recorded as an unnumbered capture at the bottom so it stops being a comment nobody owns. | answered v2.1.367-beta - approximation justified, real path exists on the holo tier, extension scoped not built |
| A40 | **"Info text size" in the player preset editor stops at 160% — take it to 250%.** Requested 2026-08-02. **The change itself is one character:** `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte:622` reads `<input type="range" min="0.8" max="1.6" step="0.05" bind:value={draft.infoFontScale} />`. **What actually needs checking is what happens at 2.5x**, because `infoFontScale` reaches several surfaces and one of them may not survive it: the preview panel sizes its base font from it (`:749`), the document engine takes it as `fontScale` (`:752`, `:771`), and the starmap falls back to it when `starmapFontScale` is unset (`:728`). At two and a half times, a heading may no longer fit the panel width, a document block may overflow its measured height, and the starmap labels — which A4 and A8 spent two items making hold a constant screen size — may collide. Raise the cap, then LOOK at each of those four surfaces at the new maximum before calling it done; if something breaks it is likely to be a layout that assumed a bounded scale rather than the scale itself. **Related and worth doing in the same pass: [[A32]] found the identical fault in the WIDTH slider** — the editor's range was 0.15-0.50 while the live view clamped to 640 px, so the top third of the travel did nothing. Check that this slider's full new range actually reaches the live view rather than being re-clamped somewhere downstream, because that is the same trap one control along. **FIXED v2.1.369-beta, AND THE A32 TRAP WAS PRESENT.** `renderDocument.ts:56` clamped `fontScale` to **1.8**, and that is the single choke point between this control and every surface the item lists - the preview panel, the document engine on both stages, and the starmap document. Raising the slider alone would have moved the panel's CSS base font while the document inside it stopped at 180%: the two scaling APART, which reads worse than a capped control. Both ends now say 2.5, with the clamp commented to say it must track the control. **RULED OUT: the 3D starmap LABELS are not on this dial at all.** A4 and A8 made them hold a constant screen size through `labelSize`; the starmap surface that takes `fontScale` is the starmap DOCUMENT, which goes through `renderDocument` like the other three. So all four surfaces share one clamp and there was no second place to raise - the collision risk the item worried about does not exist on this control. **The test is pinned AT the old ceiling, deliberately.** Comparing 2.5 against 1.6 PASSES even with a clamp at 1.8, because 1.8 is still larger - the first version of this test did exactly that and caught nothing. Comparing against 1.8 is the assertion, and reverting the clamp gives 61 against 61 and fails. | fixed v2.1.369-beta |
| A41 | **Routes on the 3D starmap run to a system's PROJECTION on the grid plane, not to the star itself.** Reported 2026-08-03: they should be direct lines to the star position. **THIS IS NOT A DROPPED COORDINATE — IT IS STATED DESIGN THAT PREDATES DEPTH.** `starmap/starmapScene.ts:3` says so in the file's own header: "Routes are lines on the plane." That was correct before WS7 gave systems a z; nobody revisited it afterwards, so the routes are honestly implementing an assumption that stopped being true on 2026-07-30. Related in shape to [[A17]] (the measure tool losing z at the pick) but different in cause: A17 intended 3D and lost the coordinate, this never intended it. **TWO THINGS A WORKER WILL GET WRONG IF THEY TREAT IT AS A ONE-LINER.** (1) **THE GLOW GEOMETRY IS FLAT BY CONSTRUCTION.** The route glow (~`:690`) builds a quad from `dx`/`dz` alone and orients it with `quad.rotation.y = -Math.atan2(dz, dx)` — a yaw-only rotation in the ground plane. A quad that can only yaw cannot follow a line with height. Feeding 3D endpoints to the core `LineSegments` (`:698`) fixes the line and leaves the glow lying flat under it. The glow needs a real 3D orientation, or a different primitive. (2) **IT MUST USE THE EXAGGERATED Z, NOT THE RAW Z.** `Starmap3DView` carries a live `zExaggeration` prop and stars are drawn at exaggerated depth. A route drawn from raw z will MISS the stars at both ends whenever exaggeration is not 1, which is most of the time. Take the same position the star sprite is placed at, do not recompute it — that is the [[A17]] lesson exactly. **Check the route NAME too:** `:671` places it "at the midpoint", and a midpoint computed in the plane will float off the line once the line has height. **Then check the 2D starmap agrees** — depth is presentational there (2D is the plan view, per WS7's standing rule), so routes SHOULD stay planar in 2D and the two views will legitimately differ. Say that explicitly in the entry so nobody later "fixes" the 2D one to match. **ROUTING: `starmapScene.ts` is the file [[A37]] is working in right now** — this is the same file and a natural add-on if that session has room, but it is a distinct change and wants its own commit. **FIXED v2.1.381-beta, and both traps were real.** **The endpoints:** an endpoint is now the system's `center` STRAIGHT OUT OF the `centers` map — the very `Vector3` the star sprite copied its position from — with a constant `LIFT` of 0.02 added as an OFFSET rather than a `.setY(0.02)` that flattened it. Nothing is recomputed from raw z, so the A17 failure is not merely avoided but structurally impossible: there is no second derivation to disagree. **The glow band needed a full basis, exactly as predicted.** `quad.rotation.y = -atan2(dz, dx)` is a yaw and a yaw cannot leave the ground plane, so 3D endpoints on the core line alone would have left the halo on the floor under a sloped line. It is now oriented by `makeBasis(dir, nrm, side)` with local X along the route and local Y (the quad normal) the part of world-up perpendicular to the route — the band is as face-up as a sloped line permits, degenerates safely for a near-vertical route, and is EXACTLY the old flat quad when the route is horizontal. `len` is the true 3D length; `hypot(dx, dz)` was the length of the SHADOW and fell short of the star at both ends independently of the height error. The 0.005 separation that keeps the halo under the core line is now taken along the normal rather than in world y, or a steep route's halo and line would land in one plane and z-fight. The route NAME takes the real midpoint of the real line. **VERIFIED BY DRIVING THE REAL MODULE IN THE BROWSER, not by reproducing it.** The pane does not composite here (screenshots time out, as usual for a worker session), so instead `starmapScene.ts` was imported live from the dev server, `Object3D.prototype.add` patched to capture everything the scene emits, and a synthetic three-system map fed through `createStarmapScene` at three exaggerations. **At 0.5x, 1x and 3x every route endpoint equals its star sprite's position exactly in x and z and by exactly the 0.02 lift in y** — e.g. at 3x the star sits at y 26.496 and the route ends at 26.516, at 0.5x 4.416 and 4.436. The glow quad's local +X came back as the normalised 3D route direction and its length as the 3D length (23.781 at 1x, 34.490 at 3x, against a flat 22.08), and its normal tilts further off vertical as the exaggeration rises — so the band follows the line rather than lying under it. **The 2D plan view is byte-identical to before:** with `flatOverhead` set, every segment is at y 0.02, the quad normal comes back as exactly `(0, 1, 0)`, the quad at y 0.015 and the length 22.08 — the pre-A41 numbers. That path has no special case in the new code; it falls out because `flatMode` zeroes the exaggeration, which is the honest way for it to happen. **THE 2D STARMAP SHOULD STAY PLANAR AND THIS IS NOT AN OVERSIGHT.** 2D is the plan view by WS7's standing rule, so the two views legitimately differ and nobody should later "fix" 2D to match. Note also that the player's "2D starmap" is not `Starmap2DView.svelte` (which nothing mounts, per [[A37]]) — it is this same scene with `flat` set, so the planar behaviour is this file's, verified above. **STILL WANTS A HUMAN EYE, and it is an aesthetic call rather than a correctness one:** the halo is now edge-on to the viewer for a steep route, so a strongly exaggerated map may show thinner glow on its steepest links. Open a map with depth, push the exaggeration slider up, and say whether the band reads well or wants to billboard toward the camera instead. **SCOPE ADDED 2026-08-03: A SHIP SHOWN ON AN INTERSTELLAR ROUTE MUST SIT IN THE CORRECT 3D PLACE ALONG IT.** Moved here from [[A21]], which is otherwise banked for the interstellar-autopilot work. This half needs NO schema change and belongs with the route fix rather than with it: once a route runs star-to-star in 3D, a ship's position along it is an interpolation between two endpoints that already have z. Doing it in the same pass is nearly free; doing it later means re-deriving the same geometry. **The same two traps apply** — use the EXAGGERATED z the star sprites are drawn at rather than raw z, and if the ship marker carries any oriented geometry (a glyph quad, a heading indicator) check it is not yaw-only the way the route glow was. Related: [[A17]], [[A37]], [[A21]]. | fixed v2.1.381-beta |
| A42 | **REMOVE the Field Guide and the Projector outright. Player Views has taken over their role properly.** Decided 2026-08-03. Both have sat behind `PLAYER_VIEWS_ENABLED` since v2.1.287 — one door on beta, with the old pair restored only on a production cut — and the unified player-view engine now does everything they did, better and from one code path. Keeping two retired tiers alive costs more than it returns: they are a permanent source of the duplication that is this codebase's most recurring fault, and [[A10]] and [[A3]] were both faults found ONLY in the legacy skins. **Surfaces to work through** (from a grep for FieldGuide/Projector/projection): `src/routes/+page.svelte`, `src/routes/catalogue/+page.svelte`, `src/lib/components/RailNav.svelte` (which also holds the three `PLAYER_VIEWS_ENABLED` sites), `PlayerViewModal.svelte`, `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte`, `SystemView.svelte`, and `catalogue/document/blocks.ts`. **DO NOT JUST HIDE THEM — DELETE.** The flag exists precisely so this decision could be taken; leaving dead tiers behind the flag reproduces the problem the flag was buying time to solve. **Three things to check before deleting, not after.** (1) **Does anything ELSE render through those paths?** `SystemVisualizer` is mounted by the legacy console skin AND by live surfaces, so it stays — establish exactly what is legacy-only versus shared before removing a line of it. (2) **`PLAYER_VIEWS_ENABLED` itself should probably go**, since with nothing to gate it is a flag with one branch — but check whether a production cut still depends on it before removing it, because the masking plan for the V2.1 production release was built around exactly that flag. (3) **Saved campaigns may reference the retired tiers** in a stored preset or a view kind; decide what happens to one that does, and make it degrade to a Player View rather than to an error. **This unblocks the V3/big-feature work** — it is a subtraction that makes every later player-view change cheaper, and it removes two of the surfaces every future UI item has to be tested against. Related: [[A10]], [[A3]], [[F1]]. | decided, ready to build |
| A43 | **Changing the interstellar unit RELABELS the map instead of converting it — Alpha Centauri reads 14.33 ly.** Reported 2026-08-03: the default starmap was loaded while Traveller settings were on, so it came in as PARSECS; leaving that mode and selecting light years leaves Alpha Centauri measuring **14.33 ly** against a true 4.37. **The factor names the fault: 4.37 x 3.26 = 14.25**, so the parsec-to-light-year conversion is being applied to a number that is already in light years. **THE SCALE DOES TRAVEL WITH THE SAVE — that is not the problem.** Both `starmap.distanceUnit` and `starmap.scale.unit` are on the Starmap and persist. **TWO REAL FAULTS SIT UNDER IT.** **(1) THE DESIGN SAYS THE UNIT NEVER CHANGES, AND THE UI CHANGES IT.** `map/distanceUnits.ts` opens by stating the contract: "A campaign stores every distance in its own unit (`Starmap.scale.unit`), and THAT DOES NOT CHANGE: this module exists so a GM can TYPE a distance in whichever of light years or parsecs they think in... while the map underneath keeps storing exactly what it always stored." The module is explicitly for INPUT AND DISPLAY ONLY. But `SettingsModal.svelte:174-190` offers a unit picker and dispatches a save that sets BOTH `distanceUnit` and `scale.unit` to the new choice **with no conversion of any stored coordinate**. So switching units relabels the map: the numbers stay, their meaning moves, and every distance is wrong by 3.26 in one direction or the other. Either the picker must CONVERT the stored geometry when it changes the unit, or it must not offer the change at all — the module's own contract says the latter, and the former is a migration with real blast radius (system positions, route distances, scale bar, depth). **DECIDED 2026-08-03: ASK THE USER. Do not pick a side — offer both at the moment of change.** When the unit is changed, prompt with the two meanings: **"change the units"** (relabel — the numbers are right, the unit was wrong) or **"convert the distances"** (the map is right, express it in the other unit). Both are legitimate, the app cannot guess which is meant, and the user always knows. The reported case is the first: the default map was imported as parsecs by accident, the underlying figures were light years, and the wanted outcome was a relabel. **Word the prompt in terms of OUTCOMES, not mechanics** — "Alpha Centauri currently reads 14.33; should it read 4.37?" beats any sentence containing the word "convert", and it makes the consequence visible before it is taken. **Make it undoable or confirmable**, because converting rewrites every stored coordinate: the choice is cheap to offer and expensive to get wrong silently. **(2) TWO FIELDS HOLD ONE CONCEPT.** `starmap.distanceUnit` and `starmap.scale.unit` both exist, and `InterstellarTransitModal.svelte:157` resolves them as `starmap.scale?.unit || starmap.distanceUnit || 'LY'` — a documented precedence between two places that can disagree. That is the duplication fault the standing rule describes, and it is very likely why the change appeared to take in one place and not another. Locate every reader of each, and unify on one. **ALSO CHECK THE IMPORT PATH:** find out whether the Traveller import CONVERTED the bundled map's coordinates on the way in or merely stamped a different unit on them, because those two produce the same symptom and need opposite fixes. The screenshot also shows the depth annotation reading "-13 ly", so the z coordinate takes the same treatment and must move with whatever is decided. Related: [[G11]], [[A17]]. | captured |
| A48 | **PRE-SHIP: the player-view editor has grown to 99 controls in a flat wall — group it into collapsible sections that work like the filters.** Requested 2026-08-03 as a sanity pass over the player-view starmap and system-map configuration before shipping. **MEASURED: `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte` is 933 lines with 99 controls** (labels, selects, ranges, checkboxes, colour inputs). It already sections with `<fieldset>`/`<legend>` — Identity, Behaviour, Theme, Graphics library — but nothing collapses, so a GM scrolls a wall to reach anything. Most of this week's work landed here: grids and falloff, constellations, orbit opacity, live readings, body graphics, document arrangements, panel width, text scale, ship models. **REUSE THE FILTER PATTERN — AND REUSE IT PROPERLY, BECAUSE IT IS BETTER THAN A VISUAL MATCH.** `components/FilterParamControls.svelte` does not hardcode its structure: groups are DECLARED AS DATA (`meta.groups`, each with an `id` and a `collapsed` flag), it keeps an `openGroups` map, renders a `.grp-head` toggle button carrying `aria-expanded`, and honours declaration order (`:15-18`, `:43`). Describing the editor's structure the same way gives grouping, ordering, default-open-or-closed and one renderer in a single move — and stops the next feature appending another orphan control to the bottom. Use the COMPONENT if it will take a generic group spec; copy the pattern only if it will not, and say which. **GROUP BY WHAT A GM IS TRYING TO DO, NOT BY WHICH SUBSYSTEM OWNS THE SETTING.** A suggested cut, to be argued with rather than followed: Identity; Behaviour (followGM, interactive); Theme and type (font, colours, document style, text size); Info panel (width, live readings, body graphics, arrangement); System map (grid and falloff, orbit opacity, scale and body-size dials); Starmap (grid, routes, mono, z-exaggeration, constellations); Filters. The test of a grouping is whether a GM changing one thing finds the related thing next to it — panel width and text size belong together even though different code owns them. **THREE THINGS NOT TO BREAK.** (1) The PREVIEW must keep updating live as controls change — that is what [[F4]], [[F7]] and [[F10]] were all about, and a collapsed section must not stop its control reaching the preview. (2) Collapsing must not UNMOUNT in a way that loses in-flight state or re-triggers a load — [[F7]] was exactly a keyed loader firing wrongly. (3) Which sections are open is a per-USER convenience, not preset data: do not save it into the preset, or a GM's scroll position travels to a player's screen. **This is presentation only — no setting should change meaning, gain a default, or disappear.** If the pass finds a control that is redundant or misplaced, say so as a finding rather than removing it in the same commit. Related: [[F4]], [[F7]], [[F10]], [[A32]], [[A40]]. | captured |
| A3 | **Player view 2D loses every surface feature — Mars has no craters, Io no volcanism — while the GM view renders them correctly. DIAGNOSED, one line.** Both views mount the SAME `SystemVisualizer`. It draws plain canvas discs and only promotes a body to the feature-rich `PlanetDisc` SVG overlay (the one that consumes craters / lava / plumes / iceCracks) when `trueColorOn && sR >= DISC_OVERLAY_MIN_R` — `SystemVisualizer.svelte:1014`. `trueColorOn` reads the `trueColorMode` store, and that store is **only ever SET in `SystemView.svelte`**, the GM system view. The player route (`src/routes/catalogue/+page.svelte:1025`) mounts `SystemVisualizer` directly inside a `FilterFrame` and never goes through `SystemView` — so nothing sets the store, the promotion never fires, and every body is a flat disc. Nothing wrong with the tags or `deriveAppearance`. **The diagnosis above was for the LEGACY Field Guide skins, and it is not what was being looked at.** In a new Player View the 2D map is not `SystemVisualizer` at all — it is the holo scene locked overhead (`effectiveSystemTier` maps `diagram2d` to `holo`). The flat bodies were the body-graphics sprites of F1: Datapad stores `bodyGfx: 'photo'` and Console `'flat'`, and the sprite path drew a plain class-colour disc with no surface features. With F1 fixed, Mars in the Datapad player view renders fully cratered. No `trueColorMode` change was needed. **Still open, as a separate smaller thing (see A10):** the legacy console skin really does read a GM-local store, and `DISC_OVERLAY_MIN_R = 11` px still means nothing is promoted at whole-system zoom in the GM 2D orrery. | fixed v2.1.286-beta |
| D2 | **CORRECTED — narrower than first written. Craters and volcanism DO auto-spawn on the new worlds; only the air-and-water features cannot.** The first version of this entry claimed the 22 rebuilt systems "can't accumulate surface features because the inputs aren't there". That is wrong for the geological ones: `craterDensity` reads `ageDensity = surfaceAgeGyr / 4.5`, and `surfaceAgeGyr` is DERIVED by `geoActivity` from mass, makeup and system age — all of which a bare `massKg + radiusKm + makeup` body has. Volcanism/lava comes off the geology regime and tidal heat, likewise derived. So those bodies will crater and resurface correctly the moment A3 is fixed. What genuinely cannot appear without authored data: **clouds and haze** (need `atmosphere`), **oceans and ice caps** (need `hydrosphere`), **banding** (needs `rotation_period_hours` — `bandCount()` reads it directly). Original observation below, kept for the survey numbers. | corrected, captured |
| D2a | **Original text (survey still valid, conclusion superseded by D2 above).** Sol's bodies carry 35+ authored fields (`atmosphere`, `hydrosphere`, `biosphere`, `rotation_period_hours`, `axial_tilt_deg`, `magneticField`, `image`, `areas`…). Tau Ceti f/g/h carry FOUR: `massKg, radiusKm, makeup, tags`. Across the 22 systems added in the rebuild, 93% have `makeup` and 0% have atmosphere, hydrosphere or rotation period. So there is nothing for the appearance pipeline to work with — no atmosphere means no clouds/haze/greenhouse, no `rotation_period_hours` means `bandCount()` gives no banding, no hydrosphere means no oceans or caps. **Not a rendering bug**; the renderer is correctly drawing "almost nothing is known". And the data is honest — nobody has measured Tau Ceti f's air. **The question is what a GENERATOR should do about it.** The engine already infers a lot from nothing (makeup from density, classification, temperature, `tidallyLocked` from the despinning timescale). Rotation period is the obvious next inference — a locked body's period is known outright, an unlocked one can take a plausible primordial spin. Atmosphere is harder but volatile retention already gates what a world can hold at its mass and temperature. **Hard constraint if this is built: inferred must be distinguishable from measured** — a tag saying so — or the map starts quietly asserting invented facts about real planets, which is worse than looking plain. Alex noticed via Tau Ceti; Sol looks fine because Sol has been visited. | captured |
| A34 | **The construct icon vocabulary exists FOUR times and now three of them are stale copies.** Found while building [[A30]] (v2.1.343-beta), which needed the triangle/circle/diamond/cross/square shapes in the info block and refused to add a fifth private copy. The canonical one is now `src/lib/constructs/constructIcon.ts` (`constructIconShape` + `traceConstructIcon`, path-only so each caller owns fill and colour). The three that remain are `lib/holo/scene.ts` (`getConstructIconTexture`, canvas-to-texture), `components/Starmap.svelte` (`iconPath`, an SVG path string) and `components/SystemVisualizer.svelte` (canvas). All three were explicitly out of bounds for the session that found this, so none was touched. They agree today; nothing enforces that, and a new shape added to one is silently missing from the others. Small, mechanical, and wants a session that is allowed in scene.ts. **FIXED v2.1.367-beta - and "they agree today" was already FALSE, which is the finding worth keeping.** Starmap.svelte's SVG copy **fell back to a DIAMOND** where the canonical resolver and both canvas copies fall back to a **triangle**, so a construct with no authored icon_type - the commonest case - drew as a different shape on the starmap than on the orrery, in the holo scene and in its own info block. The drift this item was filed to prevent had already happened; nobody had noticed because it only shows on the default. **All three now read lib/constructs/constructIcon.ts.** The canonical module gains constructIconPath(), an SVG emitter, so the vector surface shares the shape TABLE rather than keeping its own - one table, two emitters, and a sixth shape falls out of both. **Pinned by constructIcon.spec.ts:** the triangle fallback, both emitters producing a closed path for every shape in CONSTRUCT_ICON_SHAPES, and every glyph staying inside the size it was given. | fixed v2.1.367-beta |
| B13a | **DUPLICATE OF [[B13]] — FOUND INDEPENDENTLY, AND ITS PROPOSED INVESTIGATION IS A DEAD END. Read B13 before spending any time here.** The finding is real and correctly reported; the DIAGNOSIS in it is wrong and would cost an hour. **"Two Earths with different inputs" was checked and refuted:** the fixture's Earth input is IDENTICAL to the campaign's — same composition (N2 0.7769 / O2 0.2092 / Ar 0.0095 / H2O 0.0040 / CO2 0.00045), same 1.013 bar, same ~0.5 G. Do NOT diff the two Earths field by field; they do not differ. The variable is not the DATA, it is HOW MANY TIMES `process()` HAS RUN — B13's pass-ordering bug, where `calculateSurfaceRadiation` reads `body.magneticField` in pass 2 and `deriveMagnetism` writes it in pass 3. The fixture runs one pass and reads 2.299; the app runs one on load and another after every edit. Original text follows. **Both are FIXED v2.1.356-beta**, and this entry's instinct that the data was not the variable was exactly right — the variable was `atmosphere.scaleHeightKm`, deleted on import and rewritten 58 lines after the radiation model that reads it. | duplicate — fixed with [[B13]] v2.1.356-beta |
| B13b | **The bundled Sol's Earth takes 229.6 mSv/yr at the surface; the generated fixture asserts 2.0–3.0 for the same world.** Noticed 2026-08-01 while verifying [[A33]] on screen — the display was the thing under test and it is reporting the number it is given, so this is a DATA or PHYSICS divergence, not a presentation one. `physics-baseline.test.ts` pins Earth between 2.0 and 3.0 mSv/yr from `tests/fixtures/solar-system-input.json` and passes; the Earth in the bundled Local Neighbourhood starmap reads about a HUNDRED times that, which would tell a player the surface of their homeworld is roughly as irradiated as low Mars orbit. Two Earths with different inputs is the obvious explanation (magnetic field, atmosphere composition or shielding coefficients), and if so the fixture is testing a world nobody plays in. Worth an hour: diff the two Earths field by field. NOT chased — bucket B belongs to another stream. **EXPLAINED AND FIXED as [[B13]] at v2.1.356-beta, and the obvious explanation above was wrong: the two Earths do NOT have different inputs.** The bundled map is loaded through `fixUpImportedSystem`, which deletes `atmosphere.scaleHeightKm` as stale derived data, and `process()` then ran ONCE — with no scale height the belt's inner edge collapses to the surface and Earth is handed its own Van Allen peak at ground level. Reproduced at 228.25 mSv/yr. The fixture never saw it because `stripDerivedData` does not strip the scale height. | fixed v2.1.356-beta — see [[B13]] |
| A35 | **[FOLD INTO THE [[B29]] SWEEP — it is the same audit, and a raw float is the clearest case of it.]** **Numeric tag values reach the player as bare unlabelled numbers.** Noticed during the [[A33]] sweep. The tags row renders values verbatim, so Earth shows "Brilliant aurora: 0.78" and a body can show "Surface age: moderate" beside it — a 0..1 float and a bucket, side by side, with nothing to say which scale the float is on. The architecture doc's own idiom says continuous physics should be BUCKETED into tag values for exactly this reason, so the floats are the exception rather than the rule. Cosmetic and low priority, but it is the same fault class as A33 and A5 and it will keep recurring while any emitter publishes a raw float. **FIXED v2.1.366-beta as part of the [[B29]] sweep, and the instinct that it "will keep recurring" is answered with an assertion rather than a fix.** Three emitters published bare numbers: `aurora/*` (the 0..1 strength this entry names), `thermal/self-luminous` (an effective temperature) and `feature/polar-vortex` (a side count). `formatTagValue()` in `tagPresentation.ts` now gives each either a UNIT or SUPPRESSION — aurora is suppressed, because its tier is already in the key and the number exists for the renderer — and `tagConsistency.spec.ts` fails the build if a new numeric value appears with neither. | fixed v2.1.366-beta — see [[B29]] |
| - | **A THIRD copy of the lattice geometry lives in the system view, and it has already drifted.** Found 2026-08-02 finishing [[G4]]. `holo/scene.ts` builds its own square and hex ground lattice inline (`GRID_RADIUS/7` cells; hexes on axial q/r coordinates with `60k-30` corners) - and those hexes are **POINTY-topped**, where the GM's `Grid.svelte`, the 3D starmap and the shared `map/latticeGeometry.ts` are all **FLAT-topped**. **It is harmless TODAY only by luck:** `forSystemScale()` folds `hex` and `traveller-hex` to `square` before the system view can draw them, so the drifted branch is unreachable. Nothing enforces that, and the moment anyone decides a system-scale hex overlay is meaningful the two conventions collide. **This is [[A34]]'s shape exactly** - a geometry vocabulary existing in several places, said to agree, with one copy already wrong. The square half should consume `squareLattice` from the shared module and the dead pointy-top hex branch should go. Small and mechanical, but it touches `scene.ts`, so it wants a session that is allowed in there. **DONE v2.1.377-beta, same session, on the instruction that the flat-top hex is the only one wanted and the pointy-top one is legacy that nothing should preserve.** The system view now calls `latticeFor` like every other surface, and the pointy-top branch is DELETED rather than ported. **The only thing the private copy did that the shared generator could not was clip to a DISC** - which is what makes the system grid read as a plate under the orrery rather than a field - so `clipRadius` is now an option on `LatticeOpts`, applied to both squares (per-line chord, `sqrt(R^2 - c^2)`, exactly the old expression) and hex centres. It is an option rather than a second generator because it is a BOUNDARY, not a geometry. **Two tests pin what the unification could quietly lose:** that a clipped lattice puts no vertex outside the disc while an unclipped one reaches past it, and that the hexes are FLAT-topped - asserted as a vertex due left and due right and NONE directly above the centre, which is the exact difference between the two conventions. Related: [[G4]], [[A37]], [[A34]]. | fixed v2.1.377-beta |
| - | **`Starmap2DView.svelte` is dead code, and it mis-scoped an inbox item before anyone noticed.** Found 2026-08-02 while doing [[A37]]'s 2D half. The file is imported once (`catalogue/+page.svelte`) and MOUNTED NOWHERE; the player's 2D starmap is `Starmap3DView` with `flat` set, the same renderer locked overhead. A37 was triaged as "the 2D starmap has no grid handling of any kind" on the strength of grepping this file, and the requested work - adding every grid style to it - could never have appeared on screen. The dead import is removed and the component now carries a header saying what it is not, so the next reader is not caught the same way. **The decision left open: delete it, or keep it as an intended lighter renderer.** It is a real SVG starmap and might be wanted for a low-power tier or an export; that is a call for whoever owns the starmap tiers. Whichever way it goes, it should not sit there looking like the 2D starmap. Related: [[A37]]. | captured |
| - | **The starmap overlay picker and the system overlay picker write the SAME preset field, offering different option sets.** Noticed 2026-08-02 during [[A37]]. `PlayerPresetEditor.svelte:400` renders `MAP_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` (all six, including hex and Traveller hex) and `:482` renders `SYSTEM_OVERLAY_OPTIONS` (the four that mean something inside one system), and BOTH `bind:value={draft.grid}`. So a GM who picks Traveller hex for the starmap and then opens the system tab sees a dropdown whose value is not in its own list, and changing it there silently rewrites the starmap's choice. **The vocabulary is right and shared - this is a STORAGE question, not a naming one:** one field is being asked to hold two independent settings. The fix is a second preset field (the pattern already exists - `starmapFontScale` falls back to `infoFontScale`, `starmapDocumentStyle` to `documentStyle`), so `starmapGrid ?? grid` would match how every other per-stage override in this editor works. Not done in A37, which was about the grid RENDERING. Related: [[A37]]. | captured |
| - | **cssFilterApprox.ts marks the COVER and STARMAP surfaces as INTERIM "until their true-GLSL capture path lands", and that path has never landed.** Surfaced 2026-08-02 by [[A39]], which asked whether the CSS approximation was obsolete. It is not - DOM chrome cannot go through a shader without a capture step, and the approximation is one source of truth (filterParams) rendered two ways rather than a rival implementation. But the word INTERIM is a promise nobody is tracking, and two surfaces are still living on it. **What "landing" would mean, concretely, now that the pattern exists:** the holo tier already routes its info card through the REAL shader by drawing it to a canvas and compositing it into the holo render as a HUD quad (catalogue/+page.svelte:644, hudCardOn), and [[A38]] has now done the same for the document's body graphic. Both work because there is a GPU surface to composite INTO. Cover and starmap have no such target on the tiers where they are approximated, so this is not a wire-up - it needs a compositing host on those tiers, which is real work. **Decide one of two things and write it down:** either scope that work, or drop the word "interim" and accept the approximation as the permanent answer for DOM-rendered chrome. A comment that says a fix is coming, on a fix that is not coming, is worse than either. Related: [[A39]], [[A38]], [[F9]]. | captured |
| B34 | **`body.stellarRadiation` has not been stellar since [[B17]], and one of its consumers is a CLASSIFIER FEATURE.** Found 2026-08-02 while writing the radiation layer for the Newton trace ([[D5]]) — the draft labelled it "incident starlight" and Io came out at **26,279 Earth-flux**, against actual sunlight on Io of about 0.037. `calculateSurfaceRadiation` sets `body.stellarRadiation = components.total`, and since B17 that total carries the trapped-belt term, so the field now means TOTAL INCIDENT FLUX and its name says otherwise. Same fault class as [[A33]] and [[B28]]: a quantity that is correct for its purpose, published under a name that misdescribes it. **The display sites are cosmetic and are already handled** — the trace now labels it honestly, and the aurora layer's "Incident flux (Earth=1)" reads correctly by luck. **The one that needs a decision is `SystemProcessor.ts`, which feeds it to the classifier as the feature `stellarIrradiation`.** So for the Galileans the classifier is being told they receive tens of thousands of times Earth's insolation, which is true of their total particle+photon environment and false of their starlight — and any fingerprint keying on `stellarIrradiation` was written against the pre-B17 meaning. Check which fingerprints read it before changing anything; the fix is either to rename the field to what it is and give the classifier a genuinely stellar figure, or to decide that total flux is what the classifier wanted all along and say so. Related: [[B17]], [[A33]], [[B28]], [[B29]]. | captured |
| B35 | **Should the trapped-particle belt feed the SPACE-WEATHERING model as well as the dose?** Split out of [[B28]] 2026-08-02 rather than bundled into its tag fix, which is why B28 could ship in an afternoon. `deriveIrradiationDose(teqK, magShield, surfaceAgeGyr)` sees stellar UV and a galactic-cosmic-ray floor and nothing else, so a moon sitting in a giant's radiation belt weathers at exactly the rate it would in empty space at the same distance from the star. **The case is Europa and it is a real one:** its surface discolouration is RADIOLYTIC — sulphur from Io's torus, implanted and processed by the very particles [[B17]] now models — so the belt is not merely an extra dose there, it is the dominant weathering agent. **The case AGAINST doing it naively is Io**, and it is the same trap B28 warned about from the other side: Io's weathering total is 0 because `ageFactor = surfaceAgeGyr / 4.5` and volcanism resurfaces it continuously, and that must STAY 0 however fierce the belt gets, or the tholin renderer starts darkening the most constantly-repainted surface in the Solar System. So the belt term has to enter the ACCUMULATION rate and remain multiplied by the exposure time, never bypass it. **This is a physics change with visual consequences** (`irradiationDose` drives tholin darkening and regolith greying in `planetAppearance.ts`, and `surface/irradiation` buckets off it) and needs its own before/after on the derived fixture plus eyes on the rendered discs. Anchors to hold it against: Io stays low, Europa should rise, Callisto and Ganymede should rise less, Luna and Mercury must not move at all. Related: [[B28]], [[B17]], [[B22]]. | captured |
| C7 | **[[C3]] audited the bundled STARMAPS and not the standalone examples, so `Sol_Expanse-System.json` still draws the Moon 23.4 deg out of plane.** Found 2026-08-02 while doing [[D4d]], which had to fix the same thing in `Sol_2030-System.json` because the starmap generator reads it. Both files carry the same Luna node with `i_deg: 5.145`, and that figure is quoted to the ecliptic, so without `orbit.frame: 'ecliptic'` the renderer rotates it into Earth's equator and the Moon ends up in the wrong plane by Earth's tilt — the exact fault C3 exists to fix, in a file a GM can load directly from the examples list. **`Sol_2030` is fixed; `Sol_Expanse` is NOT, deliberately** — it is not a generator input, so touching it would have widened a mechanical reproducibility task, and a one-line edit made without loading the file and looking at it is how the twelve happened in the first place. **The fix is one line**, `"frame": "ecliptic",` as the first key of that node's `orbit`. **Worth doing as a sweep rather than a line:** the standalone examples were never audited at all, so check every example in `static/examples/` for far-out satellites, not just this one. Related: [[C3]], [[D4d]], [[C5]]. | captured |
| D7 | **Every radial-velocity super-Earth in the REAL bundled map is authored as 55% ice, and the radius that decides it was never measured.** Found 2026-08-02 while doing [[D4d]] fix 6, which pinned Adrian's radius after v2.1.281 traced a white-rendering, wrongly-tide-locked world to one inflated figure. Checking whether the generator's own `estimateRadiusRe` was doing the same anywhere else turned up something better and worse: **it is never reached by the real map at all** — all 182 rows in `data/cache/archive-pscomppars.json` carry `pl_rade`, because pscomppars BACK-FILLS a radius from mass when none was measured, so the estimator has only ever run for fiction bodies. The archive is doing the estimating instead, and doing it the same way: densities computed from the archive's own mass/radius pairs run 1.6–2.6 g/cc across the whole 6–12 Earth-mass band (AU Mic b 0.45, HD 69830 c 1.57, GJ 674 b 1.66, GJ 581 c 2.40, GJ 887 d 2.62...). `defaultMakeup` hands anything 4–40 Me a 55% ice / 25% gas / 20% rock makeup unless the density exceeds 4, which none of them do — **so the map contains no rocky super-Earths whatever, by construction.** **This is NOT obviously wrong and must not be "fixed" on sight:** many super-Earths in that mass range genuinely are volatile-rich, and the archive's relation is the standard one. What is wrong is that it is UNIFORM — the same relation applied to every planet with no measurement means the map cannot express the distinction, and v2.1.281 showed what an inflated radius then does downstream: it feeds the makeup inference, the visible crust, and the despinning timescale as R^-3. **Where to start:** decide whether the kit should trust `pl_rade` when the archive back-filled it (a `pl_radeerr`/provenance column may distinguish measured from derived), and whether `defaultMakeup`'s 4–40 Me branch should key off something other than a density derived from the very radius in question — that is circular as it stands. Related: [[D4d]], [[B10]], [[C5]]. | captured |
| D8 | **Every fictional planet in the SciFi map has NO axial tilt, so [[C3]]'s satellite rotation is a no-op for all twelve of its moons — and [[B10]] is already shipping in the bundled data, not just in generated systems.** Found 2026-08-02 while measuring [[C5]]. Polyphemus, Gargantua, Reach, Tribute, Andor and Calpamos all carry no `axial_tilt_deg`, so `parentTiltDeg` resolves to 0 in `holo/scene.ts:2013` and Pandora, Caliban, Zulu, Zuni, Turul, Csodaszarvas, Emese, Andoria, LV-223, LV-426 and LV-178 are drawn in the system plane whatever their frame flag says. **This is the same hole B10 describes, arriving from the other direction:** B10 found it in the random generator, and it is equally true of hand-authored fiction data, because nothing requires a tilt and nothing warns when one is missing. Consequences beyond the moons: no seasonal term in `surfaceTempProfile` for any of those worlds, and no spin axis for the disc and holo renderers. **Deliberately not fixed here** — inventing obliquities for eleven fictional worlds is an authoring decision, not a reproducibility fix, and [[D4d]] means it must be made in `scripts/starmap-build/data/systems-fiction.mjs` and rebuilt rather than typed into the JSON. **Worth doing together with B10 so the same distribution serves both**, and worth a build-kit assertion afterwards: warn when a body with satellites has no tilt, the same shape as the duplicate-id check D3 added. Note the real Sol bodies are unaffected — they are authored with real obliquities. Related: [[B10]], [[C3]], [[C5]], [[D4d]]. | captured |
| A44 | **A generator-emitted tag is shown to the user as engine-derived and locked, when nothing re-derives it.** Found 2026-08-02 while building [[B10]]. `tagSource()` (`tags/tagPresentation.ts`) classifies a tag purely by NAMESPACE: anything in `NAMESPACE_META` that is not a PoI category returns `'physics'`, which `isManagedTag` turns into "the engine re-derives it every run" and the Tags editor renders as a red lock the user cannot remove. **But the engine does not re-derive any of these.** `origin/migrated`, `origin/captured` and now `spin/axis-inferred` and `spin/tipped` are emitted ONCE by the generator and never again; delete one and it stays deleted, which is the opposite of what the lock promises. **This is pre-existing** — `origin/*` has behaved this way since those tags were introduced — and B10 followed the existing convention rather than inventing an exception for itself. **Where it actually bites, and it is not cosmetic: `spin/axis-inferred` should disappear the moment a GM types a real obliquity in.** A tag that says "this number is a guess" is a lie once the number stops being one, and today the user cannot remove it. That makes this the first case where the missing third category — a tag that is engine-AUTHORED but not engine-MAINTAINED — has a user-visible consequence rather than being a modelling nicety. **Do not fix this in isolation:** `docs/dev/unified-tagging-design.md` is the live redesign of exactly this lifecycle (system/physics/user sources, `tagLifecycle.ts`), so the third source belongs in that spec, not bolted onto `tagSource`. **Concrete ask for whoever does it:** a source that is written once at creation, freely removable, and re-asserted only if the value it describes is regenerated. Related: [[B10]], [[D2a]]. | **FIXED v2.1.421-beta.** The missing third source is `authored` in `tagLifecycle.ts`: written once at creation, never re-derived, freely removable — which is the concrete ask, verbatim. Provenance now comes from `tagOrigin()` rather than from guessing at the namespace, so the Tags tab groups these under **Generated** with a plain statement that nothing re-creates them, and `physicsTrace` — worse, since it claims to show the working — stops attributing them to a physics layer that never ran. **The bite the entry named is closed too:** typing a real obliquity or rotation period now retires the matching `spin/*-inferred` tag on the spot, because a tag saying "this is a guess" is false the moment the number stops being one. |
| D9 | **The twelve standalone examples in `static/examples/` have NO guard of any kind, and one of them is a build-kit INPUT.** Established 2026-08-02 while doing the [[C3]] sweep, by checking what [[D4d]]'s pin test actually covers: `buildKit.spec.mjs` compares only `static/example-starmaps/` — the two bundled maps and the manifest. Nothing regenerates, compares or validates the twelve files a GM loads from the examples list. **This is not symmetrical with the bundled maps, it is worse in one specific way:** `Sol_2030-System.json` is read wholesale by `build-starmaps.mjs`, so an edit there silently moves BOTH bundled maps. The pin test would catch the consequence, but it would report the starmaps as drifted and point at the generator rather than at the example that actually changed. **Evidence that unguarded files drift exactly as predicted:** the C3 sweep found Luna's ecliptic frame missing from `Sol_Expanse-System.json` four months after C3 fixed the same body everywhere it was looking, purely because C3 audited the bundled starmaps and nothing told it the standalone examples existed. **What a guard would look like, and it cannot be D4d's:** these files are hand-authored, not generated, so there is nothing to regenerate and compare against. The useful shape is an INVARIANT test rather than a pin — every node id unique within a file (the [[D3]] check, which currently runs only in the generator), every `parentId`/`orbit.hostId` resolving, every satellite's frame decision consistent with its host's Laplace radius, no body missing the mass/radius/rotation the physics needs. The C3 sweep script is most of the last two already. Related: [[D4d]], [[C3]], [[D3]]. | captured |
| C8 | **Eight authored satellites sit past their host's Laplace radius with `i_deg = 0` and no frame declaration, and choosing for them is an AUTHORING decision, not a correction.** Found 2026-08-02 by the [[C3]] sweep: Ptolemy, Panoptes and Melinoe (Triple-Alpha Centauri), Diamedes, Radzen, Cerebus Gamma and Macula (Uggi), Chazon (e Eridani). **Why they were left alone rather than swept:** with `i_deg = 0` the flag is not a small correction — it moves the orbit by the host's ENTIRE axial tilt, 47 degrees in Hades's case and 32 in Xygax's — and whether the author meant "in my host's equator" or "flat in the system" is unknowable from the data. They are also invented bodies, so unlike Luna there is no real figure to be right about. **The same sweep flagged IAPETUS in two files and that one is a definite DO NOT TOUCH**, which is the reason this list is a finding rather than a patch: at a/r_L 1.37 the proc-gen rule says system plane, but its 15.47 degrees is quoted to Saturn's EQUATOR, so equatorial renders it correctly and the flag would move it about 7 degrees wrong ([[C5]]). **The rule is for INVENTING a plane, not for reinterpreting a catalogue number somebody else already referenced** — apply it to authored data unexamined and it breaks the one body it most looks like it should fix. **Four more could not be evaluated at all** (Charon's host is a barycentre; Testion's "super jupiter" has no rotation, mass or radius), which is the same null path the generator takes, where it leaves the frame alone. Related: [[C3]], [[C5]]. | captured |
| D10 | **`axial_tilt_deg` and `generation_parameters` are used everywhere and declared nowhere, so the types cannot be relied on in exactly the areas this session worked.** Found 2026-08-02 building [[B10]]'s shared provenance helper: `Pick<CelestialBody, 'axial_tilt_deg' | …>` does not compile, because **`axial_tilt_deg` is not a key of `CelestialBody`** — every site that reads or writes it, including `physics/cloudDecks.ts` and `holo/scene.ts`, does so through an untyped access. Same story for `pack.generation_parameters`, which errors at every one of its call sites in `generation/planet.ts` including the long-standing `frost_line_base_au` one. **This is the same drift that left `Orbit.frame` untyped until C3(c) needed to write it** — a field can ship in the data, be read by the renderer and be relied on by the physics while the compiler knows nothing about it. **Deliberately not fixed in passing:** adding either to its interface would newly type-check dozens of existing accesses at once, and the fallout is its own change with its own verification, not a rider on a generator commit. `svelte-check` currently reports 1165 errors, and this class is a visible share of them. **Worth doing as a typed-surface pass** rather than one field at a time, so the count moves once and visibly. Related: [[B10]], [[C3]]. | captured |
| D11 | **Bodies in the bundled data carry a ROCKY and a GIANT class at once, and their composition agrees with neither.** Found 2026-08-02 while choosing [[B33]]'s gate, by measuring `isGiant` against `makeup.gas` across all 366 bundled bodies. `Bellum (Red Horse)` is classed `planet/desert + planet/cloudless-gas-giant` at gas 0.00; `Fuller` is `planet/desert + planet/gas-giant` at gas 0.00; `Hades` is `planet/ice + planet/ice-giant` at gas 0.00; `Iota Horologii b` is `planet/sub-brown-dwarf + planet/desert`. **A desert IS its surface and a gas giant has none, so these are not two compatible readings of one world — they are a contradiction the classifier is emitting about the same body.** Consequence beyond looking wrong: `isGiant` in `physics/reasonsToVisit.ts` is `classes include a giant type OR gas >= 0.4`, so it returns TRUE for a body with no envelope, which is why B33 could not use it as its gate. Anything else keying off giant-ness by CLASS inherits the same fault. **Two candidate causes and they need separating before anything is changed:** either the fingerprint engine is stacking two BASE types where bases are meant to be mutually exclusive (`classifyByFingerprint` pushes one base then only modifiers, so this should be impossible and would be a real bug), or these are AUTHORED legacy `classes` arrays that `importFixup` is not stripping and the derived class is being appended to. **Start by checking whether the second class is present in the source JSON** — `static/examples/e_Eridani__Four_Horsemen_-System.json`, `Uggi_(Traveller_Example)-System.json` and `Iota_Horologii___HR_810-System.json` — because that decides which of the two it is and they want completely different fixes. Note [[B25]]'s gate and [[B16]]'s tier rule both key on composition rather than class, so neither is affected. Related: [[B33]], [[B25]], [[D3]]. | captured |
| D12 | **The legacy `classifier.rules` still contain eyeball rules with no surface test and the WRONG temperature, ready to fire the moment a pack ships without fingerprints.** Found 2026-08-02 while doing [[B25]], by checking whether the gate could be bypassed. `classifyBody` early-returns into `classifyByFingerprint` whenever `pack.classifier.fingerprints` is non-empty, so for `starter-sf` these are dead — but they are still in the pack, and `classification.json` carries two `addClass` rules for `planet/cold-eyeball` and `planet/hot-eyeball` gated on `starTidallyLocked` plus **`Teq_K`**, not `SurfaceTemp_K`. **That is doubly stale:** [[B6]] moved the eyeball family onto surface temperature precisely because their notes describe the ground and a greenhouse world can look cold from orbit while its surface runs at 307 C, and B25 has now added the surface gate — neither change reached the legacy copy. A pack authored without fingerprints would silently get the pre-B6, pre-B25 behaviour. **Cheapest honest fix is deletion**, since the fingerprint engine has been the live path for the whole of Phase 04 and a fallback nobody exercises is a fallback nobody has tested; if the fallback is meant to stay supported it needs the same two corrections and a test that drives it. Related: [[B25]], [[B6]]. | captured |
| D13 | **Pluto loses its methane wisps once its nitrogen frost is modelled, and real Pluto has methane haze.** Found 2026-08-03 as the single regression in [[B5]]. Pluto's dominant gas is N2, which freezes at 63 K; its surface sits near 40, so the new volatile-frost term correctly lays nitrogen on the ground and takes its albedo **0.299 → 0.579 against a measured Bond albedo of about 0.72** — a large improvement. But the brighter surface then drops it to 36 K, and at that temperature the cloud model no longer forms the methane deck it used to (`structure/cloud-deck=methane wisps` and its virga both go). **Triton, which is the same physics at 39.9 K, KEEPS its methane wisps**, so this is a narrow threshold effect rather than a broken branch — the two differ by about 4 K and land on opposite sides of something. **Where to look:** `deriveCloudDecks`' minimum-fraction / saturation test for a condensate whose own melt point (CH4, 90 K) is far ABOVE the surface temperature — at 36 K methane is not condensing out of the air, it is already on the ground, so arguably the honest answer is that Pluto's methane is frost rather than cloud and the deck SHOULD go. **That is the question to settle**: whether a species far below its freezing point should still be allowed a haze layer (real Pluto's is photochemical tholin haze, which is a different mechanism from condensation and is not modelled at all). Do not simply widen a threshold until the deck returns — decide which mechanism is being claimed. Related: [[B5]], [[B1]]. | captured |
| B36 | **THE NINTH DUPLICATION: "does this body have a solid surface" is answered in three places and they do not agree.** Found 2026-08-03 while doing [[B11]], which unified two of the three. The copies: (1) `physics/radiation.ts` `hasSolidSurface` — now `makeupFractions(n).gas <= 0.5` plus a star exclusion, and shared with the info block; (2) `SystemProcessor.ts:1302`, a local `const hasSolidSurface = habMakeup.gas <= 0.5` gating the habitability score ([[B18]]); (3) the same expression again, inline, in [[B25]]'s classifier gate, [[B33]]'s `SURFACE()` PoI wrapper and the geology branch's `mk.gas <= 0.5`. **They now all use the same BOUNDARY, so nothing is currently wrong — which is exactly why this is worth recording before it drifts.** Five sites evaluating one physical question is the standing rule's own description of how this codebase fails, and the version B11 just fixed had been silently wrong for months (it read a stored field that is usually absent, and its class regex omitted `ice-giant`, so Uranus and Neptune reported a "surface" dose). **The fix is to make `hasSolidSurface` the single call and delete the four inline copies** — cheap, but it touches the habitability gate, so it moves bodies if the boundary is not exactly preserved and wants its own before/after sweep. Note the star exclusion currently lives only in copy (1): the others never see a star because their callers gate on roleHint first, which is a coincidence rather than a guarantee. Related: [[B11]], [[B18]], [[B25]], [[B33]]. | captured |
| C9 | **THE TENTH DUPLICATION, and the worst-placed one: `computeWorldPositions3D` does NOT apply [[C3]]'s satellite reference frame - that rotation exists only in the RENDERER.** Found 2026-08-03 building [[G8]], which had been told to reuse the propagator because "orbital inclinations are now framed correctly after C3". They are not - not in the propagator. **Every copy, located.** The rotation itself was `toParentEquator` at `holo/scene.ts:2710`, private, so nothing outside that file could reach it. It is applied at `holo/scene.ts:2100` for a satellite's live position and again at `holo/scene.ts:2717` inside `buildLocalOrbitRing` for its orbit ring. The tilt-and-ecliptic-gate DECISION is made in two different spellings, at `holo/scene.ts:2032` (gate at the caller) and `holo/scene.ts:2747` (gate inside `buildMoonOrbitRing`), with a third bare read at `holo/scene.ts:1739` that passes an ungated tilt. `physics/worldPositions.ts` has none of it. **So the propagator and the renderer answer "where is this moon" differently, by the parent's axial tilt** - 25.19 deg for Mars, 97.77 for Uranus. Measured: Phobos's orbit normal is 25.2 deg off the reference plane through the framed path and 1.1 deg through the bare propagator, and there is now a test pinning both (`eclipses.spec.ts`, "the satellite reference frame reaches the search"). **Partly unified already:** `toParentEquator` has moved to `system/satelliteFrame.ts` and `scene.ts` imports it, so G8 shares the rotation rather than writing an eleventh copy, and `framedWorldPositions3D` there is the corrected walk. **What remains is the real fix and it is not small:** the frame belongs IN `computeWorldPositions3D` so there is one answer - but the renderer needs parent-RELATIVE offsets for its toytown spread and would have to stop applying the rotation itself, which is a visual change across every moon system and wants its own scoped pass with before/after on the bundled maps. The two gate spellings should collapse into `satelliteTiltRad` at the same time. **Not attempted here on purpose:** `src/lib/physics/**` was off limits to this session, and a renderer change that cannot be eyeballed is not one to make blind. Related: [[C3]], [[C5]], [[G8]]. **FIXED v2.1.410-beta, at the source, and the renderer stopped.** `computeWorldPositions3D` applies the frame itself now - a `frame` op on the 3D walk's ops only, calling `satelliteTiltRad` + `toParentEquator` on each parent-relative offset before it is accumulated. The flat 2D walk deliberately does NOT get it: the orrery propagates omega-only in the reference plane, so there is no out-of-plane axis for an equatorial rotation to tilt into, and 2D is the plan view. **`framedWorldPositions3D` IS DELETED, not kept alongside** - a correction that some callers apply and others do not is the same fault one layer up, which is exactly how this item came to exist. `satelliteFrame.ts` keeps the KNOWLEDGE (the rotation and the gate) and nothing else; `eclipses.ts` calls the propagator directly and no longer knows reference frames exist. **The renderer's placement rotation is gone with `BodyVisual.orbitTiltRad`** - the difference of two world positions is already framed, so double-rotating was the trap and it was avoided. **One call site survives in `scene.ts` and it is not a duplicate:** `buildLocalOrbitRing` samples `propagateState3D` directly, so it holds a raw parent-relative offset rather than a world position - a different input needing the same frame, via the same helper. **The two gate spellings collapsed into `satelliteTiltRad`:** the caller decides, `buildMoonOrbitRing` no longer re-decides. **That closed a latent bug nobody had recorded** - the renderer's test for "satellite" was "not one hop from the root", which makes a planet orbiting a binary's SECONDARY star a satellite and rotates it by that star's tilt. Its inclination is system-framed like any planet's. No shipped map hits it (checked: 20 secondary stars with children across the bundled maps and examples, every one with no tilt or zero), but generation gives stars 0-8 deg via `starTiltFromPack`, so a GENERATED binary would have. **VERIFIED NUMERICALLY over 96 systems in every bundled map and standalone example at three epochs each**, by reproducing the OLD renderer path (bare walk + its own rotation and gate) beside the new one: **the rendered offset is unchanged to 1.1e-10 of the orbit radius**, and that residue is floating-point noise the new path REMOVES rather than adds - the old one recovered a 1e-4 AU offset by subtracting two AU-scale numbers, and this one never does that subtraction. **The world POSITION moves by up to 1.178 AU** (a station orbiting Xygax, host tilt 32.25 deg; Phoebe's station at Saturn moves 0.046 AU) and that is the correction every non-renderer consumer was missing. **G8's eclipse times are unmoved and all 24 of its tests pass unchanged**, for a stated reason: `framedWorldPositions3D` and the framed propagator compute the same thing, so the search sees what it always saw. `tests/output/solar-system-derived.json` is byte-identical - nothing in a derivation pass reads positions. New pins live in `worldPositions.spec.ts`, the frame's own module now, with the control being the same propagator over a system whose host does not lean. **NOT SEEN: the Browser pane does not composite here, so there is no visual check** - the scene module was mounted live in the page against the bundled Sol and driven through Saturn and Uranus without error, but nobody has looked at it. Short list appended below. | fixed v2.1.410-beta |
| D14 | **Pluto and Charon are authored with the BARYCENTRE's heliocentric inclination instead of their own mutual orbit's, so the model has them eclipsing every orbit when really they do so twice in 248 years.** Found 2026-08-03 when [[G8]] began answering for barycentre partners. Both members carry `i_deg` 17.16 - which is Pluto's orbital inclination to the ECLIPTIC, the barycentre's own number, copied down onto them. Their mutual orbit is really inclined about 119 deg to the ecliptic, because that is Pluto's obliquity and Charon orbits in its equator. **The consequence is real and it is in the app, not just in a prediction:** with a 17 deg plane the pair sit permanently lined up with the Sun, so the eclipse row reads "every 5 d - total", where the truth is that mutual events happen only in seasons around Pluto's equinoxes - the famous 1985-1990 run, which is how Charon's radius was measured, and the next is not due until the 2100s. **This is DATA, not the engine**, which faithfully answers what it is told. It is also the one case [[C3]]'s rule cannot express, since barycentre members are system-framed by design and must not inherit a partner's tilt. **The fix belongs in `scripts/starmap-build/`**, and read [[D4]] before touching it: the kit can no longer simply be re-run. Whoever takes it should decide whether a barycentre member wants its own `orbit.frame` declaration - the same lever [[C3]] reached for, which does not exist for this case. Related: [[C3]], [[G8]], [[D3]], [[D4]]. |
| E2 | **Sessions share one git INDEX as well as one working tree, so `git add` followed by `git commit` is not atomic and one session's staged work lands in another's commit.** Observed three times on 2026-08-03 between the scene/grids stream and two others. Twice it went one way — a [[G9]] inbox entry and the whole of [[G10]] (eleven files, `niceInterval.ts`, the metric grid, the 43x ring-label fix) were swept into commits titled "docs(inbox): D6 sharpened..." and "Real-sky import phase 1..." — and once the other, when a concurrent inbox edit was carried into a [[G8]] commit. **NOTHING WAS LOST ANY OF THE THREE TIMES**, which is exactly why it is worth writing down: the failure is silent, the work is committed and pushed under a message that does not describe it, and the only symptom is `git commit` reporting "no changes added to commit" to the session that staged them. **The consequence is not corruption but ARCHAEOLOGY** — `git log -- <file>` and `git blame` now point at the wrong intent, and a future session reading "why did this change" is misled. It also breaks commit-per-item, which exists so one bad call can be rewound alone. **Cheap mitigations, in order of cost:** stage and commit in ONE shell invocation rather than two tool calls, so the window is milliseconds rather than minutes; check `git log -1 --stat` after committing rather than trusting the push result, which is the standing rule for the changelog already and should be generalised; and for anything larger, a session-per-worktree (`git worktree add`) removes the shared index entirely, which is the real fix if five streams are now normal. Related: the coordination rules above, which cover numbering, debt and duplication but not this. **CLOSED v2.1.411-beta: the worktree-per-session recipe is written into the standing rules above, and was proved by using it.** The whole of C9, E3 and this edit were done in `../sse2-frame-hygiene` on branch `wt/frame-hygiene`, pushed with `git push origin HEAD:beta`. A worktree closes BOTH races at once - its own index and its own working tree - and as a bonus each one regenerates its own `tests/output/solar-system-derived.json`, so a before/after diff finally means something. Two frictions found and written into the recipe: `beta` cannot be checked out twice (use `-b wt/<name> beta`, push `HEAD:beta`), and **do not junction `node_modules`** - it half-works, which is worse than not working. Also recorded: `npm ci` is ten seconds here, and the preview server needs its own port in `C:\Development\.claude\launch.json`. | closed v2.1.411-beta |
| A45 | **The GM's snap-grid picker is a second, hand-written copy of the overlay option list, and it is already missing one.** Found 2026-08-03 while confirming [[G10]] part (1), which turned out to be done everywhere except here. `SettingsModal.svelte:352-358` spells out four options — No Grid / Square / Hex / Traveller hex — instead of rendering a filtered `MAP_OVERLAY_OPTIONS`, and it uses the legacy `'none'` / `'grid'` spellings that `normaliseOverlay` exists to translate. **It is NOT offering something it cannot draw**, which is the fault G10 was chasing: `Grid.svelte` is typed `'grid' | 'hex' | 'traveller-hex' | 'none'` and genuinely does only those. But it is missing **`subsector-hex`** — borders without numbering, which the shared vocabulary has had since v2.1.378-beta and which the GM's own map therefore cannot show while every player view can. That is the drift the shared list exists to prevent, arriving exactly as predicted. **The fix is a third filtered view of the one list** (`SNAP_GRID_OPTIONS`, the same shape as `SYSTEM_OVERLAY_OPTIONS`), plus teaching `Grid.svelte` the subsector boundary — which is not free, since the 3D starmap draws that boundary as ribbons from `subsectorLattice` and the 2D snap grid would need its own line version. **Not attempted here:** it is a change to the GM's persisted `starmapUiStore.gridType` spelling as well as to a renderer, and this session's item was about the SYSTEM map. Related: [[G10]], [[G4]], [[A37]]. |
| B37 | **A belt carries an ascent Δv budget derived from a mass that is not a mass.** Found 2026-08-03 while sorting what legitimately applies to a belt for [[B11]]. `loDeltaVBudget_ms` is computed for belts and rings and shows in the info block as "Ascent Δv" — but a belt's `massKg` is a DEBRIS-DENSITY PROXY, not gravitational mass: `scripts/starmap-build/build-starmaps.mjs` sets it via `densityProxyMassKg()` and says so ("NOT gravitational mass (excluded from perturbers)"), and [[B19]]'s stability work excludes belts from gravitational pairing for the same reason.
